# Claire architecture

This document describes Claire's overall structure as the code stands today, to make it easier to find code. Work not yet done or only half done is written up in [`roadmap.md`](roadmap.md).

Path abbreviations: `core/…` = `core/src/main/kotlin/com/wangzan101/mika/core/`, `app/…` = `app/src/main/java/com/wangzan101/mika/`.

## 1. Modules and layering

The repository has two Gradle modules:

- `:core`: pure Kotlin (JVM), with no Android dependency. The main loop, tools, the platform-independent parts of capabilities, context, verification, memory, knowledge, triggers and run records all live here, and can be run on a computer with unit tests and a fake model. Prompts, built-in knowledge and skills are its resources (`core/src/main/resources/`).
- `:app`: the Android host. UI, foreground services, virtual screens, accessibility, the built-in phone assistant, WebView, sandbox, file storage, and the Android implementations of the interfaces in `:core`.

There is also an optional standalone Go gateway `companions/relay/`, used for single-user, single-device public-network remote CLI / MCP. It is not part of the APK; the phone connects through outbound WSS; see [remote-mcp.md](remote-mcp.md) for deployment and phone authentication.

```
 Source   chat UI │ timed/conditional trigger │ parent task (child task) │ computer (CLI / MCP)
          └──────────┬──────────┘
          TaskService (foreground service, Android host)
          TaskCenter (one Run per conversation, parallel limit, queue) · RunStore · Outbox
                     │
          TaskRun ── one run: install capabilities, build AgentLoop, wrap up
                     │
          AgentLoop (scheduling only) ← RunInbox (interjections, answers, confirmations, hand-backs, cancel)
            ModelCaller · ContextBuilder/ContextCompressor · TurnPolicy/StopRules
            ToolDispatcher → ActionExecutor (the only execution entry)
                     │  CapabilityProvider (the core does not know concrete capabilities)
          phone      web       sandbox      (mcp client)
```

Dependency direction between packages in `:core` (checked by `PackageDependencyTest`):

- Capabilities (`core/cap/<capability>/`) depend on the core; the core references no capability, and capabilities do not reference each other; only the assembly layer (`core/assembly/`) knows several capabilities at once. In the App, the directories of the various capabilities under `cap/` likewise do not reference each other.
- The tool engine (`core/tool/`: `ToolDispatcher`, `ToolContext`, `AgentTool`, etc.) does not reference UI state (`core/screen/uistate/`). The engine only uses the layer that has a screen through `SurfaceLayer` (`core/tool/SurfaceLayer.kt`); the interface contains only the screen id, the tool, the parameters and the result; the implementation is `Interfaces`, created by the assembly layer at startup and put into the tool context, and tools that have a screen get the screen-reading state from the context through the extension in `core/screen/uistate/ScreenContext.kt`.

### Directories

Repo root:

| Directory | What goes here |
|---|---|
| `app/`, `core/` | Two Gradle modules |
| `companions/` | Companion programs that are not part of the APK and are released to users separately: `mika` (computer-side CLI and Agent skills), `mika-feishu` (Feishu connector), `relay` (Go remote gateway) |
| `plugins/` | Official plugins; `plugins/examples/` holds examples for plugin authors |
| `tools/` | Scripts for development only: translation check, MCP catalog generation, sandbox runtime build, log pulling, offline voice pack publishing |
| `eval/` | Device eval tasks |
| `docs/` | User documentation; `docs/dev/` developer documentation; `docs/design/` design drafts, plans, prototypes and acceptance records |
| `openspec/` | OpenSpec change records (tool conventions are in the repository root) |

`:core` (`core/…`) is grouped by responsibility, with packages nested under each group:

| Group | Packages |
|---|---|
| `agent/` | Main loop, context, compression, stop rules; the audio, image and video records generated in one turn (`GeneratedAudio` etc.) |
| `run/` | Run contracts, child tasks, resource coordination; `run/record/` run events, `run/trace/` tracing |
| `tool/`, `general/`, `action/` | Tool engine and plugin tools; general tools; the single execution entry point |
| `capability/`, `assembly/`, `cap/<capability>/` | Capability interfaces; assembly layer; each capability (including `cap/wechat/`) |
| `screen/` | Screen reading: `uistate/`, `observation/`, `map/` (UI map), `user/` (screens the user sends), `vision/`, `visual/` |
| `safety/` | `gate/` (confirmation gate, irreversibility decision), `guard/` (including the script source check `JsSource`), `verify/` (verification and sources) |
| `learn/` | `memory/`, `knowledge/`, `skills/`, `teach/`, `replay/` |
| `model/` | Model calls; `chatgpt/`, `oauth/` (account sign-in), `decision/`, `usage/` (token usage) |
| `device/` | Device access; `adb/`, `shell/` (built-in phone assistant), `access/` (the App access list) |
| Others | `proactive/`, `trigger/`, `mcp/`, `remote/`, `calls/`, `frontdesk/` (front desk), `search/`, `net/`, `export/`, `eval/`, `prompt/`, `self/`, `selfcheck/`, `workspace/`, `personal/` (personal assistant) and other single-responsibility packages |

`:app` (`app/…`) has one package per feature at the top level (`chat/`, `task/`, `calls/`, `voice/`, `browser/`, `mcp/`, `settings/`, `files/`…), and the feature's pages live in its own `ui/` subpackage (such as `chat/ui/`, `settings/ui/models/`). `download/` is the generic verified downloader (`VerifiedDownload`) and download URL allowlist (`DownloadUrls`), shared by App updates (`about/`) and offline voice packs, so it is not placed in any feature package. `voice/ondevice/` is on-device offline recognition: voice pack catalog, storage and installation, inference backends and model residency (see §16). `ui/` holds only what is shared globally: `MainActivity`, `Navigation`, `components/`, `theme/`, `viewer/`, `terminal/`, Markdown rendering. `cap/` is the Android implementation of each capability.

Components registered in the manifest (Activity, Service, Receiver, Provider) keep their original fully qualified names: the system stores these names (accessibility authorization, notification listener access, Quick Settings tiles, alarms), and changing the package name would invalidate the user's authorizations and scheduled alarms. The small packages `a11y/`, `sandbox/`, `shell/` and `setup/` exist as entry points for this reason.

## 2. Tasks and runs

### Run

A run is described by `RunSpec` (`core/run/RunContracts.kt`): `id`, `goal`, `threadId` (conversation), `priorTurns`, `provenance`, the capability subset `capabilities`, `readOnly`, `parent`, `stepBudget`.

- `RunProvenance` (`core/run/RunProvenance.kt`) records where this run came from and whether the user is present: `RunOrigin` = Ui / Channel / Trigger / Mcp / Child; `Attendance` = Present / Reachable / Unattended.
- Run phases `RunPhase`: Queued, Preparing, Running, WaitingUser, Parked, Paused, Finishing, Aftercare, Ended, Interrupted.
- "Who started this step" is another matter; see `Origin` in §4.

### Task center

`TaskCenter` (`app/task/TaskRegistry.kt`, held by `TaskService`) manages starting, queueing and finishing runs:

- Each conversation has only one Run at a time. If the user sends another message while a task is in progress in the same conversation, by default it is handed to the running Run as an interjection (`TaskService.begin`); the user can also choose "Queue" on the input field, and that message goes into this conversation's persistent queue (`FollowUpQueue`), becoming the next item when the current task ends.
- Tasks in different conversations can run in parallel, with the limit in §8; tasks over the limit go into a first-in-first-out queue and are filled in as Runs end (skipping conversations that already have a Run). When the parallel switch is off (the default), the UI cannot switch conversations while a task is in progress (`ChatViewModel.canSwitch`; other conversations in the sidebar `ChatDrawer` are not tappable and show a hint), and tasks handed to the service for another conversation (conversation page, floating capsule, browser page, schedule, computer) all go into the queue and are not lost (`TaskService.begin`). The conversation page first waits for the service to become idle before handing over (at this time that message shows "Wait until the previous task finishes" underneath, `ChatViewModel.begin`), to prevent a new message from being treated as an interjection while the previous task in the same conversation is still wrapping up; if another task started somewhere else between the check and the handover, it is still queued, and starts after that task finishes. Tapping stop at this point only stops this conversation's task (`TaskActions.cancelIn`) and does not touch the one that is running.
- When a scheduled task is due and the original conversation has a task running, it queues in that conversation and waits for it to end (`queueInConversation`); it is not treated as an interjection and is not lost.
- Run state is written to `runs-v3.json` (`app/task/FileRunStateStore.kt`, atomic replace write).
- `app/task/TaskCenterState.kt` is only the state the UI needs to display (run list, parallel limit, interjection state, etc.).

### Inbox and interjections

Everything that comes in from outside during a run goes through `RunInbox` (`core/run/RunInbox.kt`); the kinds are `RunInput`: Steer (interjection), Redirect, Answer, Confirm, HandBack, Cancel.

- **Interjections add to the work**: everything already done is kept. Each interjection cancels the model request currently being waited on (`AgentLoop.routedOrRedirect`), and the question is asked again after the existing history; messages already sent are not changed by a single character, and the request prefix still only grows. Tool calls that have already entered the execution entry finish as usual and their results are recorded as usual, and the interjection is attached before the next model question (`AgentLoop.appendSteers`). Steer and Redirect take the same path.
- **Text already displayed when interrupted is kept**: if the cancelled request had already streamed out some text (the UI's `TaskState.Running.preview`), `AgentLoop.keepInterrupted` records that text in the history as a text-only reply, placed before the interjection, so that what the model sees when it asks again is the same as what the user saw; thinking content and half-written tool calls were never displayed anyway and are not recorded. Nothing is recorded if no text was displayed (streaming display was off, text had not appeared, or a retry cleared it) or if this batch has no interjections. The live text on the UI is cleared on the spot, and text that arrives late from the network thread after the cancellation is no longer displayed. This text also emits a run event `reply_interrupted` (after truncation and redaction), and `TaskRun` attaches it to the interjection that is handed to the model right after (`SteerNote.interrupted`); the conversation page shows it above that interjection in a light color, with a small line underneath "Interrupted by your new message"; it is stored with this turn in the conversation (`Turn.interruptedReplies`, one-to-one with `steers`, with an empty string where there was no interruption) and is still displayed when the conversation is reopened. It is for the UI only and does not enter the context given to the model in later turns.
- **Replies already finished before the interjection are kept**: when a reply without a tool call has already been displayed and recorded in history, and only then an interjection is picked up (the reply just happened to finish first and the interjection arrived afterwards, or an interjection arrived while tidying up sources before handing over), `AgentLoop.keepFinished` turns that UI text into a run event `reply_finished_before_steer` (after redaction, not shortened: the UI needs the full text), clears the live text on the spot, and then attaches the interjection. `TaskRun` likewise attaches it to the first interjection in this batch (`SteerNote.finished`), and the conversation page shows it above the interjection in the same way as an answer (without marking it as an interruption), stores it with this turn (`Turn.finishedReplies`, one-to-one with `steers`); nothing is attached if no text was displayed. Interjections on the conversation page that have not yet been handed to the model are placed after the answer that is still growing (`steersAroundDraft`): that answer was being written before the interjection arrived, so when it finishes or is interrupted it stays in place and does not jump from below the interjection to above it.
- **When interjections enter the context**: `AgentLoop.appendSteers` takes the inbox before each model call at every step; when the model gives an answer without tools it first takes a look, and continues working if there is a new interjection. Before the answer is handed over (after checking and tidying sources), `RunInbox.closeUnlessSteered` looks and closes under the same lock: if there are still interjections it does not wrap up and continues with them; only when there are none does it close the inbox, and only then do later messages become the next item. When the model calls end_not_done itself, an interjection that just arrived likewise makes it continue; a guard stop or an error still ends the run.
- **Waiting ends early**: the inbox emits a signal that "there is an interjection not yet handed to the model" (`core/run/SteerSignal.kt`), installed in the coroutine context of the whole run. Calls that lose nothing when cancelled are wrapped in `waitOrSteer` and end early as soon as an interjection arrives: pure waits and tools that only ask a vision model (declaring `AgentTool.yieldsToSteer`: wait, phone_wait, phone_event, web_wait_for, describe, find_visually, wrapped uniformly by the dispatcher), waiting for text in `phone_run`, each segment of `do_steps` that waits for the UI by condition, the dwell before a web page change (`PageDwell`), and the interval between two outward actions on the same website or in the same App (`ActionExecutor`). A cancelled call reports `skipped_for_new_message` and did nothing this time; inside `do_steps` this step is skipped together with the following sub-steps. The moment after an action completes that waits for the UI to settle and judges the effect is not cancelled (it gives the action's result). The retry wait of a model call is already inside the model request and is cancelled with it.
- **Pacing protection is not lost**: when a dwell or interval is cancelled, the action itself is not performed (the tool result explains why, and it can be called again if needed). Both are measured by instants (the interval from when the previous outward action finished, the dwell from when this page arrived), the part that was not waited out does not count as waited, and the remaining time is made up before the next page change or outward action; an interjection only lets the model think now, and does not make the next action come sooner.
- **In the middle of a multi-step batch**: when `do_steps` finds an interjection between two steps, it stops there, the result states "Skipped to handle the new message" (`skipped_for_new_message`), and it does not count as a failure (`core/agent/BatchRunner.kt`). If an interjection arrives while a step has finished and is waiting for the UI by condition, the wait also stops immediately (that step's `wait.stopped` is `new_message`), and the following sub-steps are likewise skipped.
- **Immediate feedback on the UI**: when `TaskRun.steer` is successfully put into the inbox, the message is marked "Received; the current task will be adjusted at the next step" (`SteerState.Received`); while the task is paused/taken over it shows "Received; it will be handled when the task continues"; when handed to the model it changes to "Added to the current task"; if it missed the wrap-up and was queued as the next item it is "The current task has ended; queued as the next item". The conversation page and the conversation card in the floating capsule both write this small line under the message (`steerLabel`).
- **Withdrawing and editing interjections**: by default a message is still inserted directly into the current task. Long-pressing a message on the conversation page that has not yet been handed to the model allows withdrawing it or taking it back for editing; `RunInbox.withdrawSteer` shares one lock with `takeSteers` and the wrap-up, and returns failure if it has already been handed to the model or the task has wrapped up. On success it is marked `SteerState.Withdrawn`, the conversation page and the capsule no longer show it, and it does not count as an added interjection. If the task has not yet been handed to the service, it is withdrawn from `ChatViewModel.held`. Editing restores the body and quotations; editing is not offered when the input field already has a draft, a quotation or an attachment; editing is not offered for interjections inserted by the floating capsule with a screen or by the browser page with page notes (`editableSteer`): when taken back, the start of the input field is the screen marker or several lines of the page note's original text, deleting it loses the screen and the page, and damaging it makes the page unrecognizable. Interjections appended by the computer (`SteerNote.remote`) are also displayed in the conversation, but withdrawal and editing are not offered, and the inbox also refuses withdrawal (editing would turn words that belong only to the current task into a phone interjection, possibly converting it into the next item; whether to withdraw is decided by the computer); `remoteSteers` reports `Withdrawn` as `not_applied` and does not add an outward-facing state.
- **Interjections that were not handled in time**: interjections not yet consumed when the run ends become this conversation's next item if the user did not press stop; if the user pressed stop they are dropped (`TaskRun.handoffUnconsumed` only handles those unconsumed at wrap-up). An interjection that arrives after the run has closed is queued as the next item when `TaskRun.steer` cannot put it into the inbox, or when `TaskService.begin` finds that the task has already wrapped up.
- **Queueing (`FollowUpQueue`, `app/task/FollowUpQueue.kt`)**: messages the user explicitly chose to "Queue" and steers that missed the wrap-up all go into the same FIFO, separated by conversation, persisted in `files/followups-v1.json` (temp file + flush + atomic rename; a corrupt file is moved to `.corrupt-<timestamp>`). All additions and removals write to disk first and then publish the in-memory state; if writing fails, the UI never shows a "queued" state; the segment where the main conversation first waits for the service to become idle also only writes to disk and does not start early. Attachments are first copied into the conversation workspace, and their paths are then queued together with the original words; capsule screenshots are also saved first. The service calls `TaskCenter.tryStart(..., followUp)` to decide under the same lock whether to start or put it back in the queue; `onRunEnded` takes only **this conversation's** next item, one at a time via `claimNext`; after `cancelQueued` it also takes the next item, so that items queued behind are not stranded. Items in the capacity queue are likewise kept until the actual `markRunning` before removal, and cancelling while capacity-queued removes them at the same time. `claimed` lives only in memory: if the process is killed between taking an item and registering the run, after restart the item is still pending; after registration, the `followUpId` in the run record handles deduplication and reconciliation, so it will not be executed twice. Stopping the current task does not cancel items explicitly queued; the queue file is kept after the process is killed or exits, and is not automatically started at launch; the next time a conversation is opened, `ChatViewModel.openConversation/switchTo/refresh` calls `TaskService.drainFollowUps`. The UI uses `app.followUpQueue.changes` to show the queue bar above the input field; the steer fallback is shown by the `SteerState.Next` line on the interjection line and is not displayed twice.
- **Stop**: Cancel is a control signal and is not queued behind interjections. `TaskRun.cancel` cancels child tasks at the same time; if a takeover is in progress it ends the takeover; items in the queue are removed directly.
- **Account after stopping**: the UI immediately shows stopped and the input field becomes free; then a few lines of account are added as this turn's answer (what was done, what was seen, how many times batched actions were sent and which ones, which were not finished), and the outcome is still cancelled (evaluation, result classification and learning all count it as cancelled). When `TaskService` wraps up, it first registers the run number in `TaskCenterState.summarizing` (the UI uses this to write "Organizing what was done…" under "stopped"), records the "cancelled" turn, then writes in a process-level scope via `TaskRun.stopSummary`, and when done uses `ConversationStore.updateTurn` to update that turn; whether writing succeeds or fails, the registration is removed; it does not block the wrap-up, the next item, or another stop tap. The writing is in `StopSummary` (`core/agent/StopSummary.kt`): it only reads this run's record (activities, the intent of each step, the two most recent passages read, the half-written text when stopped, all bounded, without images), asks a model once (preferring a lightweight model, falling back to the main model on error, no tools, 12 seconds total), and if there is no model, an error or a timeout, it assembles directly from the record (`StopSummary.plain`). Outward actions (send, submit) are counted by the `outward` flag on the activity (`ActionExecutor` marks it according to the irreversibility decision). If nothing was done and no text was written, it just says it was stopped. In the next turn's context it is written as "the user stopped this task partway; before the stop: …" (`Conversation.context`), so that "continue" knows where to pick up. The original text read in earlier turns does not enter the context; when it is needed the model looks it up itself (`history_read`, see "Records of earlier turns").

### Delivery

Results, questions and notifications are sent through `Outbox` (`core/run/Outbox.kt`): first record what is to be sent, then send. States Pending → Attempting → Delivered / Failed / Uncertain; the same delivery id is sent only once; one whose result is unknown halfway is marked Uncertain and not retried automatically. Records are in `outbox-v3.json` (`app/task/FileOutboxStore.kt`).

Channel tasks only send one acceptance confirmation, and do not forward run phases or step counts. After a task is persistently registered, it enters `Outbox` as a `Notice` with `accepted:<run key>`, and sending does not block task execution; a redelivery with the same channel message id does not create a task and does not confirm again; different ids are registered separately even if the text is the same, and are executed in order in the same conversation. Questions, confirmations and takeover still only go through `ChannelInteraction`. The result chain separately records the milliseconds of each segment — main loop completion to sending, Outbox plus sending, WeChat HTTP — without recording message content.

The local channel is `LocalChannel` (`app/task/LocalChannel.kt`): the local UI plus system notifications. `WechatChannel` (`app/wechat/`) is the second channel: a single account, the owner's private chat text and images, credentials stored encrypted on the device, an independent foreground service long-polling, no relay server built, and no separate task state machine. Inbound messages are first written to the pending ledger and then the sync cursor is advanced; `TaskCenter` confirms only after deduplicating persistently by channel message id; after a process restart, unregistered messages are submitted first. Task records save the source `ChannelPeer`, and questions, results and `RunRecovery` are all routed by source. The channel interfaces `Channel` and `ChannelGateway` (owner identity, question id correspondence, payment confirmation only on a channel that can confirm, `Channel.confirms`) are in `core/run/`; WeChat has `confirms=false`, and payment confirmation and phone takeover still go through the phone.

WeChat image sending and receiving reuse the existing workspace attachments and task export files: inbound images are downloaded in a batch, decrypted and imported, then the attachment paths and the original words are first recorded in pending, and then handed to the task; outbound sends only images explicitly exported by this task, and each image and its text has its own Outbox record. The screenshot tool can save to the workspace with `savePath`, and then deliver it via `share_file`. For image limits, failure and recovery semantics, and verification entry points, see [WeChat image sending and receiving](wechat-images.md).

Automatic screen wake and unlock for WeChat tasks is separately authorized by `WechatStore.allowUnlock`, off by default; the authorization is stored independently in `unlock.json`, bound to the current owner and bot, and a missing, corrupt or mismatched account is treated as off. Revocation only deletes the authorization file and does not change the message ledger; when turning off receiving, a failure to write to disk also revokes the authorization, to avoid restoring an unlockable state after a restart. Unlocking is not possible while receiving is off; unbinding or rebinding by scanning the code each time revokes the authorization, while rebinding the same account still keeps the message ledger. `canUnlock` checks the current binding, WeChat channel, owner and thread mapping every time. `taskUnlockAuthorization` (`app/task/TaskUnlockPolicy.kt`) provides the `TaskGate` unlock callback only for scheduled tasks and for the owner's WeChat tasks that were already authorized when they started running; other sources or unauthorized tasks still wait for a manual unlock. The WeChat callback re-checks the authorization dynamically at the moment of actual unlocking and does not carry over a revoked permission. This switch does not modify `DeviceAccessSettings.enabled` or lock-screen credentials, still requires the existing local device access permission, and does not change `RunOrigin.Channel` or the payment and takeover rules.

### Pausing Claire

In Settings › "Pause Claire" the user personally stops Claire entirely, until they personally resume it. The UI promises only the following and says nothing about what other Apps do.

- **Single source of truth**: `MikaPause` (`app/settings/MikaPause.kt`) is one persistent flag; it first writes to disk synchronously (`commit`, on an IO thread) and then changes memory, and if the write fails nothing changes and the UI says so truthfully. Everything else only reads it (in core through `PauseFlag`, `core/run/PauseFlag.kt`) and does not keep its own copy; the built-in assistant's `BuiltinShellStatus.Paused` is only a projection of it (`BuiltinShellEngine.pauseChanged` only tells the engine to project again, and the engine reads the flag anew each time). After the process is killed and restarted, what is read is whatever was last written.
- **What pausing does** (`MikaPauseControl.pause`, `app/task/MikaPauseControl.kt`; once started it finishes and is not cancelled when the UI goes away): first write the flag; `TaskService.pauseAll` clears the persistent queue and stops running and queued tasks (queued ones are marked cancelled, running ones go through normal cancellation, and at wrap-up each settles its scheduled tasks, computer, workflows and channels); `WorkflowRuntime.haltForPause` makes in-progress workflows stop at the next step; stop the built-in assistant and pairing discovery; turn off Claire's accessibility service (when the service is running `disableSelf`, otherwise ask the user to go to system settings) and wireless debugging (when there is permission to modify system settings, write the switch, otherwise ask the user to go to developer options). Pairing keys, port cache and the "paired" flag are never touched.
- **Entries blocked while paused** (all re-read the flag where the request starts and at asynchronous hand-offs):
  - At the task service entrance: `TaskService.begin/startNew` (`pausedBlocks` → `finishPausedStart`), taking the next queued item (`drainReady`), `queueFollowUp`/`deferMessage`, `enqueueFollowUp`/`drainFollowUps`. Blocked requests settle themselves and are not left to run automatically after resume.
  - Conversation UI and floating capsule send (`ChatSendOps.send`, `CapsuleController.send`): tells the user it is paused, does not queue, and leaves the words in the input field untouched.
  - Scheduled (`TriggerEngine.fire`, `TriggerRuntime.checked`): a due window is recorded as skipped (`PauseFlag.REASON`, the plans page shows "Skipped while Claire is paused"), advances to the next one, and is not made up after resume; "Run now" reports an error.
  - Channel (`ChannelGateway`): a new message starts no task and leaves no hidden queue; it first confirms receipt, then through `Outbox` makes one best-effort reply, "Claire is paused; this one was not started" (id `paused:<channel>:<message>`); if the reply cannot be sent it does not affect the confirmation, and the channel does not redeliver. Channel messages stopped only at the service entrance (`finishPausedStart`) likewise confirm first and then reply. Confirmation replies during the pause are not processed.
  - Tasks handed over by the computer (`RemoteTaskHub.launch/startOwned`): reports `UNAVAILABLE` and one explanatory sentence (`PauseFlag.REMOTE_MESSAGE`), and neither starts nor queues. Other requests from the computer that would move the phone go through the same guard `PauseFlag.refuseRemote()` (`core/remote/RemoteTaskService.kt`, the pause switch is injected through `RemoteEndpoints`): answering, appending requirements (`RemoteTaskService.answer/steer`), and MCP dialing, answering, waking the screen and unlocking (`McpTaskTools`); reading status, hanging up and handing the earpiece back work as usual.
  - Workflow (`WorkflowRuntime.start/resume/resolve`): reports `WorkflowProblem.Paused`; checks again before dispatching the next step (`advance`).
  - Built-in assistant: the watchdog, retries, pairing discovery and pairing, and Wi-Fi/wireless debugging changes do not start the assistant; pausing while it is starting cancels that start (also re-checked before every connection), and wireless debugging that this start turned on is turned back off; an assistant handed over late by the computer (`HelperHub.offer` returns `Reject.Paused`, and the engine also stops it on the spot when it receives it) and Shizuku binding are both refused; while paused it does not read the pairing code and does not show the pairing notification.
  - Automatic repair (`BuiltinShellRecovery`, `RecoveryPermissions`, `AccessibilityRepair`): does not reopen accessibility, does not apply pending capabilities, and does not restore automatically at boot; if the user pauses in the gap during an accessibility restart, it is not added back.
- **Resume** (`MikaPauseControl.resume`): first clear the flag; if the assistant has paired and can write the switch, turn wireless debugging on, and the assistant reconnects with the existing pairing without a new pairing code; re-schedule the alarms for scheduled tasks (`TriggerRuntime.rearm`). Scheduled runs, refused messages and tasks skipped during the pause are not made up afterwards; a workflow stopped at "paused" is continued when the user taps continue. The accessibility service is turned on by automatic repair (`AccessibilityRepair`, when the App can write system settings or the assistant is connected); the resume page first waits for it to have a result (`awaitAccessibilityRepair`), and only when it cannot be repaired does it list that and the steps other Apps could not do as buttons that go straight to system settings (`planResumeSteps`).
- **Not blocked**: incoming-call standby and proactive learning are outside the pause (the pause promises only the above).

### After a process restart

- `RunRecovery` (`app/task/RunRecovery.kt`) is the single entry point for run aftercare, for runs that were "interrupted and the user has not been told yet". Two occasions: after a process restart, `MikaApp.onCreate` calls `recover` as soon as it starts (`TaskService.onCreate` also calls it, but only the first one in the process takes effect, and later ones do nothing); when the task service is destroyed while the process is still alive, `TaskService.onDestroy` marks the interruption and then calls `settleInterrupted` on the spot, without waiting for the next launch (aftercare uses an App-level scope, so it finishes even after the service is gone; the startup scan then picks up those that were killed before finishing on the spot). Marking is done synchronously inside the call (`RunStore.interruptUnfinished()` marks unfinished runs as Interrupted, excluding runs that are running at that moment via `live`), and aftercare is done in the background: a reply that was stored but not delivered is delivered once more (noted as recovered); an unknown delivery state is not resent, only "The previous result may have been delivered" is shown; when there is no reply, a system notification "The run was interrupted; you can start it again in the conversation" is sent, and a failure-state record "The task was interrupted; you can start it again" is written into the conversation (the same set as other failure results, displayed in the UI as an unfinished turn). **"Already told" does not get a separate field; it is read from the delivery ledger**: if there is any one of `interrupted:<run>`, `uncertain:<delivery>` or `failed:<delivery>` notice record, it is not processed again; a run whose reply was delivered becomes Ended. So repeated calls, or both occasions colliding, only notify once. **Never resume from the middle**: the screen state cannot be restored.
- `TaskRecovery` (`app/task/TaskRecovery.kt`, run when the App starts) restores system state rather than runs: an App muted during the task gets its sound back, and a clipboard borrowed during the task is returned to the user.
- Child run records have Running changed to Unknown when loaded and are not re-run (`app/task/FileChildRunStore.kt`).
- Handling of scheduled task windows is in §10.

## 3. Main loop

`AgentLoop` (`core/agent/AgentLoop.kt`) only does scheduling; the specific decisions each live in one class:

| Class | What it does |
|---|---|
| `ModelCaller` | Assembles requests, calls the model, streams output; compression and backfill, retries for temporary errors, retries for oversized context, routing between large and small models |
| `RetryPolicy` | Bounded retries for 429, 5xx and network loss (at most 3 times, exponential backoff, with an overall wait cap per task) |
| `ToolDispatcher` | Finds tools by name, holds the evidence ledger, assembles each capability's prompt section |
| `TurnPolicy` | Progress checks (only when there is a step limit), a reminder once on an empty or truncated reply, one fallback when a tool call leaks into text, one reminder when follow-up is due, one fallback when the answer's language changed, and only `end_not_done` on the last turn (only when there is a step limit) |
| `StopRules` | When to end: giving up, a guard stop (repeating the same action, no progress, repeated reading, too many failures, going in circles in the sandbox), model failure, steps exhausted (only when there is a step limit) |
| `BatchRunner` | `do_steps`: give 1–20 known actions at once, execute and verify them one by one; a step can carry wait conditions (`expect` appears, `expect_gone` disappears, `expect_app` foreground App or page address, `wait_stable: true` UI stable, `timeout_s` default 8 seconds, at most 20 seconds), and after doing it, code reads the screen until the condition holds before the next step, no longer asking the model about "still loading"; stops early on a dialog, a window from another package covering the screen, or an App change, and writes `wait` (`met`, `stopped`, `waited_ms`) into the result. The waiting itself is in `core/screen/uistate/UntilWait.kt`, shared by phone and web, waking through the port's `awaitChange` (accessibility event / page change); waiting time counts as waiting in the run summary, not as operation |
| `IntentGate` | When a task started from the conversation UI first wants to use a non-lookup tool, judges whether the user is chatting, looking up information or asking for something to be done; if it is chat, answers again without tools. When it cannot tell (the call failed, or the reply is not one of the three words), it follows the main model's original decision; judged only once per run (`AgentLoop.assessIntent`) |
| `StuckAdvice` | Adds advice to the tool result when stuck; on the second time stuck, suggests giving up |
| `NoticePolicy` | After looking at a screen, decides whether to supply the model with knowledge about the corresponding App/website |
| `GuideRecommender` | The opening index: picks at most 3 pieces of App/website knowledge based on the task text, and separately lists all skills (built-in ones and personal skills in use or on trial); the model reads the full text itself with `skill_read` |
| `RouteReplay` | When "do it the way I did last time" is turned on, replays a matching successful route before the first model call (see §9) |
| `UserExchange` | The three interactions that need user participation: follow-up questions, confirmations and takeover |

Step limits are not set by default: Settings › Task runs › Step limit per task (`FeatureFlags.taskStepLimit`, 1–500, storing 0 means unlimited). `TaskRun` reads it once when starting, puts it into `RunSpec.stepBudget`, and then as `AgentLoop`'s `maxSteps` (the evaluation's `EvalHooks.maxSteps` takes precedence; when neither exists it is `AgentLoop.UNLIMITED_STEPS`); changing the setting midway only affects tasks started afterwards, and a task that was interrupted does not resume, so it does not pick up the new value either. When there is a limit: guards are graded by it (`OperationGuards(maxSteps)`; the thresholds for the three kinds of stop — returning to the same state, going in circles, and never reaching a new state — are computed as a fraction of the limit, see `GuardRules.lateStop`; when unlimited these levels of stop never arrive), progress is reminded at the halfway and three-quarter points (when the limit is at least 10 steps), and after the steps are used up there is one final wrap-up turn (only `end_not_done` is offered, counted as step limit plus one), and if the wrap-up does not answer either, the run ends with `FailKind.StepLimit`. The start event records this limit (`RunEvent.TaskStarted.stepLimit`, not written when unlimited, also present in the summary), and the run details page displays "steps used / limit" from it (the wrap-up turn is not counted and does not exceed the limit); when unlimited this line is not shown.

What the setting currently covers (all through `TaskRun.execute`): the conversation UI (including the floating capsule and asking on the browser page), schedule-triggered tasks, and tasks handed over by the computer via CLI / MCP all follow the setting; a schedule's condition check (the read-only check) is not governed by it and is always unlimited; child tasks do not look at the setting, and their step count is decided by `spawn_agent` itself (15 steps by default, the model may ask for up to 40, `SpawnAgent`), and is not counted toward the parent task's steps.

### Result judgement

- **There is no "done" tool**: a reply from the model without any tool call is the answer; text attached to a tool call is displayed as process explanation. The text recorded when a reply is interrupted by an interjection is not an answer, it only lets the model know what the user has already seen (see "Inbox and interjections").
- **`end_not_done`** (`core/general/OutcomeTools.kt`): when something cannot be done or only partly done, use it to finish; `message` states clearly what was confirmed done, where it got stuck, and what is on screen now; `subtype` is one of `blocked`, `login_incomplete`, `captcha_incomplete`, `route_failed`, `device_unavailable`, `interaction_failed`.
- **Before handing over the answer**: an empty or truncated reply gets one reminder; some inference services occasionally fail to parse the model's tool call and the call becomes the body text as-is (`LeakedToolCall`, `core/agent/LeakedToolCall.kt`: a common call marker followed by a Claire tool name), which is not an answer: it falls back once and lets the model call again through the tool interface, and records `tool_call_as_text` in the run record; if it leaks again immediately it ends with a model error (`StopRules.toolCallAsText`), never handing the raw marker to the user, and the count restarts after a normal tool call; if the user just answered something and the Agent did nothing, one reminder; if the last action that changed the UI did not read back its result, one reminder. If the answer's body is clearly not in the language of the user's most recent message (`ReplyLanguage` counts by the script each letter belongs to, Han characters by character and the rest by word, not counting original text inside quotation marks and brackets or lines that only list names; the user's message counts only what they typed this time, not the screen, the browser page's page notes, the quoted passage, attachment lines and interjection markers it carries (`UserWords.typed`); not checked when the opening only sent a screen, only selected text or only carried an attachment, and for an interjection that only sent a screen or only selected text, judged by the previous message), it falls back once and has the model rewrite the whole thing in the user's language, and if it is still wrong after the rewrite it is handed over anyway.
- **Sources are generated by the system**: `AnswerSources` (`core/safety/verify/AnswerSources.kt`) picks the observations that support the answer from the evidence ledger `EvidenceLedger` (at most 6), as the "sources" below the answer; the model does not write them itself.
- **Background result classification**: after the answer is delivered, `TaskRun.settleOutcome` uses `OutcomeJudge` (`core/agent/OutcomeJudge.kt`, preferring a lightweight model, 30-second timeout) in the background to classify this run as one of `RunOutcome`'s Done / Partial / NotPossible / Unknown. It does not change the answer; the result is written to the run record and the conversation record; scheduled tasks use it to mark whether this run failed. Learning and replay only accept Done: admission is decided in exactly one place, `LearnGate.done` (`core/agent/LearnGate.kt`): an answer was handed over, it was judged Done, and there is no value that still does not match after a fallback; if verification ran, it must also have passed (running and failing still blocks, not running does not block). Drafting personal skills and App knowledge, moving a trial to formal, and recording experience routes and success counts all ask only it (see §9, §11); whether to record a route is decided in one place, `LearnGate.shouldSaveRoute` (routes are on, the task succeeded, there is a run id, and the goal does not carry the user's screen).
- **Independent verification**: `Grounding` (whether the numbers, ids and quotations in the answer appear in what was read, and whether the arithmetic is right) and `Verifier` (an independent verification model) are both in `core/safety/verify/`, controlled by the `reviewFinalAnswer` switch. The product currently has it off (`reviewFinalAnswer = false` in `TaskRun`), so all answers are recorded as "unverified", see "Known issues" in the roadmap; learning looks at the outcome judgement and is not affected by it.

## 4. The single execution entry point and tools

- **Single entry point**: every action that changes the outside world goes through `ActionExecutor` (`core/action/ActionExecutor.kt`). `ToolDispatcher.execute` requires an `ActionPass` (`core/tool/ActionPass.kt`), whose constructor is internal and which only `ActionExecutor` can create, ruling out bypasses at compile level; read-only calls go through `executeRead`, and anything not read-only is rejected. The order is fixed: guard → lock-screen check → irreversible action check and consent → execute → post-action verification → record.
- **Interval between outward actions** (`core/action/OutwardPacing.kt` pure logic, `ActionExecutor` waits according to it): when sends and submits recognized by the irreversibility decision (including buttons that contact someone or submit an application with one tap, such as "Chat now", "Say hi", "Apply", "Say hi", "Easy Apply") are done in a row on the same website (by registrable domain) or in the same App (by package name), the first one does not wait, and afterwards each one is separated from the previous by a random 8–20 seconds; the more done in the last 30 minutes, the more the lower and upper bounds each increase by 0.5 / 1 second, up to 30 seconds; it counts from when the previous one finished, including the time spent reading pages and thinking about the next step in between, whether or not the page changed (the dwell on a page change is separately governed by the web pacing). Phone and web use the same decision and the same set of intervals; during autonomous execution the decision still runs to recognize outward actions (based on the screen the model currently holds, without reading the screen separately), but it does not block. The wait is after the decision and before the action starts, and the task can still be cancelled during it; how long it waited is recorded in this call's `waitMs` and is not counted in operation time. It is installed only by the App's tasks (`AgentLoop(outwardPacing = …)`); core tests do not wait by default.
- **Who started this step**: `Origin` (`core/action/Origin.kt`) = Model / Batch / Script / Replay / McpTool, each with its own policy (whether it can use the user's consent to let a payment through, whether it stops on a guard warning). Each action inside a composite tool (a multi-step batch, `mika phone` in the sandbox, web scripts) returns to the same entry point through `SubActions` (`core/tool/SubActions.kt`), on the same execution and recording path as single-step tools.
- **Tool declarations**: one class per tool, implementing `AgentTool` (`core/tool/AgentTool.kt`), declaring its verb, risk (Read / Write / External), capability, whether it can join a multi-step batch, whether it can join an experience route, its concurrency mode, etc.; code acts on these attributes and does not hard-code tool names. `ToolRegistry` filters the available tools by task, and a task's tool definitions are frozen once set.
- **Tools are in two layers, expanded only when used** (`core/tool/ToolGroups.kt`, `ToolDispatcher.offeredNow`): always present are the general tools (ask the user, end when not done, wait, knowledge and skills, memory, schedules, child tasks, …) and the capability that is never withdrawn (MCP); phone, web and sandbox are each a group (`Capability.folds`), and at the start only the entry tools are given (`AgentTool.entry`), with a short description from `prompts/entries/<tool name>.md`: after `phone_launch_app`, `web_navigate` or `run_command` is called, its group is expanded (`Entry.Opens`), while `phone_list_apps` and `web_read_page` (read the body of a URL) are enough on their own and do not expand (`Entry.Alone`). Takeover, reporting an obstacle, looking back at screens and multi-step batches are only given after a group with screens is expanded (`AgentTool.withScreens`, counted as the `screens` group). After expansion, the whole group's full text is given from the next request, the entry switches to the full text, and the capability's description section (`capabilities/<capability>.md`) is also spliced into the system prompt and kept for the rest of the task; `moreTools` in the result says a word about it. If the model calls a withheld tool from memory, it still executes as usual and likewise expands its group (it expands even with wrong parameters, so the full text can be seen and corrected next time). The order is fixed: always present → `screens` → phone → web → sandbox; expanding a group only changes the part from that group onward, and the request prefix is invalidated at most once because of expansion. Pre-expansion at the start: groups actually used in the previous turn (`Turn.groups`, recorded by `AgentLoop.usedGroups` and stored in the conversation file; entry tools that are enough on their own do not count as used), and capabilities deemed certainly needed by this message (the sandbox when carrying files from the workspace, `Capability.preload`). In a read-only task commands are not offered, and the sandbox group has no entry, so it is expanded from the start. Child tasks, schedule triggers and other sources are all the same. The run record records expansion as `tools_opened` and pre-expansion as `tools_preloaded`; `PromptVersion` is computed as if every group were expanded, so the same prompt version does not differ because of which groups were expanded this time.
- **Approval**: `ApprovalPolicy` (`core/action/Approval.kt`) asks about only one kind of action: the step that spends money (`Risk.payment`: a payment or order recognized by the irreversibility decision, or, when the screen has an amount due with a currency (¥, yuan, $), an outward or irreversible action that matches the decision, see `ClassifiedRisk`; characters on a Traditional Chinese UI are first converted to Simplified and judged with the same word list, `core/screen/uistate/Hant.kt`). Everything else is done directly: sending, posting, submitting, deleting, contacting others, changing account or security settings, typing into password fields, taps found by looking at an image but whose nature cannot be told, and taps whose target cannot be read are never asked about. The money-spending step is asked about only when it was started on the phone with the user present (`Ask`); runs started from channels, schedules or MCP, child tasks and runs with nobody present cannot ask anyone, and are always rejected (`approval_unavailable`). There is only one kind of consent: it lets through only the immediately following matching operation (`ConfirmationGate`: same App, same kind of action, the object is on screen, the declared amount is on screen, within 3 steps, with no other write operation in between). Payment itself (judged as pay) does not go through consent and is handed to the user to pay personally (`hand_to_user`, reason=payment). All approval conclusions are recorded in the run record (`RunEvent.Approval`: outward ones, money-spending ones, and ones not let through), and verification uses this to know that the task did outward things. There are no approval levels, and no per-task or long-term grants.
- **Ask / autonomous**: one global switch, in the first group "Working style" in Settings › Task runs ("Autonomous mode", test tag `work-autonomous`), off by default, which means ask; the value is stored in `ApprovalStore`'s JSON (`autonomous`). `ApprovalControl` (`settings/ApprovalControl.kt`) gives each task a `RunApproval` when it starts (whether it is autonomous comes from the global value), and `TaskRun` installs accordingly: when asking, `confirmations = ConfirmationGate()` and `autonomous = false`, the money-spending step is blocked as `confirmation_required`, and for orders and the like the model calls `request_confirmation` (it must write the amount on screen; an application that cannot write the amount is rejected directly and not given to the user), and the user answers on the card in the conversation page, the card in the floating capsule or the notification (agree only this time / do not agree), and the answer goes through `TaskService` → `TaskRun.confirm` → `RunInbox` to the loop; when autonomous, `confirmations = null`, `autonomous = true` and `request_confirmation` is not offered, the decision still runs (and outward action intervals are still honored) but even payments are not blocked. Changing the switch (`ApprovalControl.setAutonomous`) saves it for later tasks, and running tasks switch along with it: a task that was asking at start and is switched to autonomous midway is no longer blocked from the next step (a confirmation already popped up still waits for an answer); a task that was autonomous at start and is switched to asking midway leaves it for the next task (tools and prompts do not change within a task). Tasks started in the floating capsule, tasks continued from a chat, child tasks and schedule triggers all follow this switch; tasks handed over by the computer via CLI / MCP are the exception: `ApprovalControl.start(remote = true)` always starts as asking and does not follow the switch, and the payment step is never done in such tasks (see the "Computer connection" section); child tasks are read-only anyway, and when asking they pass the gate, and any money-spending step is rejected. Runs with nobody present, such as scheduled tasks, do not pause and wait for someone when they hit a money-spending step in ask mode: the execution entry rejects it directly (`approval_unavailable`), the model truthfully says which step was not done, the rest is finished as usual, and the result is notified as usual. In autonomous mode `ConfirmationGate` does not exist, and the early return for autonomous in `ConfirmDecision.judge` comes before the "do not act on Claire itself" (`ownApp`) check, so this protection does not take effect in autonomous mode; when asking, Claire does not operate itself.
- **Old tool names after renaming**: `LegacyNames` (`core/tool/LegacyNames.kt`) maps old names to current tools, and is used only to read old routes, run records and personal skills; when the model calls an old name, it is only told the new name and the call is not executed for it.

## 5. Observation, action, place

- **Observation** is the only unit the core consumes: `Observation` (`core/screen/observation/Observation.kt`) carries kind `ObsKind` (Read / Output / Reply / UserSaid / Attachment / Fact), provenance (capability id), place, title, text, image, confidence, step, time and id. The evidence ledger, sources, verification and run records all read it.
- **Untrusted content** (web pages, child task results, etc.) is wrapped once in `<untrusted>…</untrusted>` when the observation is built (`core/screen/observation/Untrusted.kt`).
- **Place** `Place` (same file): App, Site, Page, Command, File. Knowledge is supplied to the model by place (§11).
- **UI state** (`core/screen/uistate/`): control tree, element ids and staleness detection, waiting for the UI to settle, action effect (`ActionEffect`: every action that changes the UI reports back the actual effect, and input is read back and verified; on the phone, when a tap or gesture shows no visible change but the tree for this screen cannot read that spot — the tree is unusable, or a part is missing and the tap is not on any element — the kind is still `no_effect`, additionally marked `treeBlind`, and the hint says instead "no visible change does not mean it was not tapped", and does not say it probably was not tapped; what is done by kind (consecutive no-effect reminders, multi-step early stop, statistics) is unchanged; in the UI after the action, short new text "limit reached" — such as use count exhausted, limit reached, come back tomorrow, too many operations, limit reached — in a hint bar, dialog or page popup does not count if it was already on screen before the action (`LimitNotice`) — the result writes `limitReached` and a stop hint, and a multi-step batch stops at this step). Phone and web share this. The dispatcher, before and after each call, uses `SurfaceLayer` to have it do the UI preparation and wrap-up (take a look before the action, compare out the effect, attach the UI after the action); the tool engine itself does not know it (see §1). The execution entry's irreversibility decision and approval, and replay when judging by screen reading, use it directly (`ActionTarget`, find a target by description).
- **Visualization** (`core/screen/visual/`): frame ids, `view_past_screens` look-back, frame differences. The image is only there so the user can see what Claire is doing and as supplementary observation when text cannot be read; getting things done relies on UI state.

## 6. Capability modules

Capabilities are assembled by `CapabilityProvider` according to `RunSpec` (`core/assembly/RunAssembler.kt`, `TaskTools.kt`), installed once at the start of each Run:

- Capabilities are chosen by `spec.capabilities`; runs with nobody present drop the tools for follow-up questions, confirmations, takeover, creating schedules and editing the profile; read-only runs keep only read-only tools (plus opening a URL on the web).
- General tools (`core/general/`) are always present: `ask_user`, `hand_to_user`, `handle_obstacle`, `end_not_done`, `wait`, `view_past_screens`, etc. Offered only when conditions are met: `request_confirmation` (ask the user for consent before spending money; only when there is a confirmation gate: ask mode, someone present), memory tools (only when "Use memory" is on), knowledge and skill lookup (only when there is a knowledge catalog, `LookupTools`), `schedule_task`, `spawn_agent`, `history_read` (only when there are run records and earlier turns that recorded runs), `notices_read` (read the original notifications from the last two days, plus the stored daily, weekly and monthly summaries; only when the proactive capability's "Same-day message summary" is on, the aggressiveness is not the most conservative level, and the task was started by the user on the phone), `web_search` (only when the user has configured a web search service in Settings; when not configured it goes through the browser).

### phone: phone Apps

- **Virtual screen**: the task operates Apps on a separate virtual screen, and the user keeps using the phone as usual. The virtual screen is created by the built-in phone assistant (`app/cap/phone/display/AgentDisplay.kt`, `app/cap/phone/shell/ShellService.kt`) and has the same size as the main screen; from Android 14 it has its own focus and does not affect main-screen input (on Android 12 and 13 main-screen focus may be lost briefly, and Settings says so; below Android 12 phone Apps cannot be operated, while chat, web pages and the sandbox work as usual). It connects and creates the screen only the first time an App or link has to be opened, or a system command or event listener is used; tasks that only use the web and the sandbox never touch it. The target App is muted on the virtual screen, and sound is restored during takeover. Muting works by setting the App's `PLAY_AUDIO` to ignore, with the rules in `app/task/AppMuter.kt`: it is muted before Claire issues the start (opening an App, opening another copy, sharing, opening a link that specifies an App or has only one App to receive it, and system actions that open a UI; quietly set alarms and timers are not muted), and the first few seconds are silent too; if the start is rejected, the early mute is reverted; afterwards every new App that appears on Claire's screen (opened from the target App, or another copy coming to the front; accessibility window changes make it re-read the top task on Claire's screen, `AndroidScreen.foregroundApps`) is muted the first time it is seen. Muting acts per "package + user" (`AppAudioKey`: the original copy is the package name, another copy is "package name@user id", and the several `*InUser` interfaces of the built-in assistant take `--user`), so the copy in a clone or work profile is muted separately and does not affect the original copy in the user's hands. What is not muted: Claire itself; system packages with no launcher entry (system UI, chooser, permission dialog); whatever the user can currently see on the main screen (the assistant's `visibleOnMainScreen`, counting as in use when the main screen cannot be read) — muting acts per package, so it also silences the copy in the user's hands, and in that case the progress notification truthfully says "this App may make sound", and likewise when the mute command fails. When muting is turned off in Settings, nothing is muted. At wrap-up `AudioMute.restoreAll` restores everything muted during this run, including what was muted midway; the mode before muting is recorded in the built-in assistant's ledger file (`app/cap/phone/shell/AudioMuteLedger.kt`), and when it starts the assistant first restores whatever it did not get to restore last time. Restoration rules: a mode explicitly set to allow/ignore/deny etc. is written back as it was, one that was never set is written as allow and not as default (Android only lets sound through with allow), and only the packages in the ledger are touched.
- **Virtual screen orientation**: after the virtual screen is created it is first set to fullscreen windowing mode (`setFullscreenWindowingMode`; some systems give new screens freeform by default), and when the interface is unavailable it falls back to `wm set-display-windowing-mode`. To avoid stealing main-screen focus, the virtual screen carries STEAL_TOP_FOCUS_DISABLED, so some systems ignore orientation requests from Activities on a background screen, and a landscape App is squeezed into portrait with black bars. From Android 14, while Claire's screen exists the built-in assistant runs an orientation watcher (`app/cap/phone/shell/DisplayOrientationWatch.kt`): it registers a task-stack listener (ITaskStackListener) with the task manager, and task changes are coalesced into one scan (`CoalescedScan`, events within 50 ms are scanned only once); when registration fails it falls back to scanning every 500 ms, scans only while Claire's screen still exists, and stops after all screens are released. Each scan (`ShellService.syncDisplayOrientations`) takes one snapshot of Claire's screens under the lock, and looks up tasks, reads fields and changes orientation outside the lock, so it does not block creating or releasing screens; for each Claire screen it reads the letterbox field of the top task (`appCompatTaskInfo`: `topActivityBoundsLetterboxed` exists from 15, 14 reads the app window width and height from the task directly, but some letterbox modes do not fill in width and height); when the size is valid it is computed from the app window's orientation, otherwise the watcher's saved Activity/Task requestedOrientation is used to detect a fixed orientation request (the static `topActivityInfo` does not reflect runtime `setRequestedOrientation` and cannot replace the callback), and that is combined with the screen's current orientation to compute where to rotate (pure logic `DisplayOrientation.rotation`, no change when they match); the hidden interface `IWindowManager.freezeDisplayRotation` locks only this screen, and when the interface is unavailable it falls back to `wm user-rotation -d <screen> lock <orientation>`; the main screen is never touched. Below Android 14 the watcher is not started and the display stays as it was. On the picture side (`AgentDisplay`, `DisplayFrame`): the physical buffer is unchanged, each frame is turned upright according to the screen's current orientation (`DisplayFrameGeometry`), and the preview is limited to 360 pixels on the short side, so a landscape screen does not end up as one low-resolution strip.
- **Apps when the task ends**: by default they stay and are not destroyed with the screen. At wrap-up (in `TaskRun`'s finally, before `AudioMute.restoreAll` restores muting) `AppsAfterTaskEnd` hands the keep-or-close marks the model made (`phone_app_after_task`, stored in the phone port's `AfterTaskMarks`) to the built-in assistant's `finishDisplay`; the assistant decides each task's fate according to `app/cap/phone/shell/AppsAfterTask.kt`: marked ones follow the mark; unmarked ones that are making sound right now are closed (it first asks `AudioManager.getActivePlaybackConfigurations` for uids whose player state is started, then maps uid → package + user; when the uid is hidden it reads `dumpsys audio`; ones muted by Claire also count as playing; when nothing can be read, ones currently muted by Claire count as playing), otherwise they stay; Claire's own pages and anything that is not an ordinary App page is always closed. Kept ones are moved to the main screen and put at the very back: `WindowContainerTransaction.setHidden(true)` → `moveRootTaskToDisplay(…, 0)` (the system always puts it on top, and hiding means it is not displayed and takes no focus) → in the same transaction `reorder(…, false)` plus un-hide; after moving it looks once more at the ones making sound (ones not marked keep that make sound are moved back to Claire's screen and closed with it), then waits for the TO_BACK transition to finish (during this `TaskInfo.isVisible` is still true, so one reading of visible cannot be treated as failure): it checks every 100 ms, calls `reorder` again every 750 ms on ones still visible, and waits at most 2 seconds, with the rules in a pure function `app/cap/phone/shell/SettleBehind.kt`. While waiting it does not hold the start lock (moving and wrap-up are inside the lock, released in between), so other tasks can still open Apps as usual; a kept task is recorded as "kept" only at wrap-up, the next open does not take it while waiting, and one that another task has taken onto its screen no longer counts as kept. One still visible at 2 seconds cannot be left in front of the user: it looks once more inside the lock, and if it is still visible it moves it back to Claire's screen and closes it with the screen (only when it cannot move it does it `removeTask`, the same path as one that was moved and then made sound), and the result records that it was not kept (reason `still_in_front`, diagnostics note "still visible on main, closed"); ones that have already gone behind by then stay. If un-hiding failed after moving (it stays hidden and is not visible in recents), it stays, diagnostics note "N left hidden on main", and the next time the same copy is opened it is moved back to Claire's screen and un-hidden; a cold start closes it first as usual. The screen still carries `FLAG_DESTROY_CONTENT_ON_REMOVAL`, and anything not moved is destroyed with it. When this system cannot hide or move at all (interface missing or refused), it stops moving and destroys with the screen as before, with one diagnostics line "apps after task: … move not supported" (the run record has the details under `apps_after_task`). The assistant remembers the ids of kept tasks (in memory only); the next time `startApp` (including another copy) opens the same App, if that task is still behind the main screen and the user is not looking at it, it is moved as a whole back to Claire's screen to continue, instead of opening a new one; a cold start closes it first and opens from scratch. When only releasing the screen (`releaseDisplay`, the assistant's `releaseAll` on unbind), everything is destroyed as before.
- **Built-in phone assistant**: an auxiliary process running as shell, started through the phone's own "Wireless debugging" (`app/cap/phone/setup/BuiltinShellManager.kt`, `app/cap/phone/shell/HelperProcess.kt`; the ADB protocol is implemented here, in `core/device/adb/`). One pairing is enough: the pairing code can be read automatically from the settings page by the accessibility service, or entered from the notification or the setup wizard. The code-entry notification (`app/cap/phone/setup/PairingNotifier.kt`, sent by `PairingCoordinator` according to the assistant's state and progress in finding the pairing window) needs no screen-recording or overlay permission and relies only on the notification itself: the "Pairing" channel is high priority, with sound and vibration, and can pop up above the system pairing window; a channel's importance cannot be changed by the App after it is created, so `PairingChannel.plan` looks at existing channels — if there is none it creates one, and if an older version created it at too low an importance and the user has not changed it (`hasUserSetImportance`) it recreates it with a new id; a channel the user turned off or set not to pop up is not changed back, and `SystemStatus.pairingChannel` gives it to the guide page (`SetupView.notifyIssue`): when notifications are not allowed or the channel is off, the pairing step's main button first goes to allow notifications / open the settings page for this channel, and when it is set not to pop up it only shows a reminder and an entry. `PairingCoordinator.begin` starts watching for pairing (finding the pairing window, reading the code through accessibility, sending the code-entry notification) for a limit of 10 minutes counted from the last `begin` (`SlidingDeadline`): tapping "Go to wireless debugging", or leaving while the guide page sits on the pairing step (`ON_STOP`, usually the user switching to system settings), calls it again, and if it is already watching it only pushes the deadline back instead of popping the notification again; mDNS for finding the pairing window (`BuiltinShellEngine.startDiscovery`) works the same way, restarting the clock when called again while still searching. If the guide page has been open for a long time, or the user switches from recents to system settings, it still reads the code and pops the notification. When the pairing window is found it sends "Enter pairing code" (accessible reading delays it by 5 seconds), and the "Fill pairing code" action carries RemoteInput (no unlock required, no generated replies); if nobody fills it in, it pops again after 8, 20 and 40 seconds, at most three times, ringing and popping each time, and after it has been replaced by other content it no longer pops. The entered code is first normalized locally (spaces, various dashes, full-width digits) and checked for 6 digits; if it is wrong it says so in the same notification on the spot and does not try; if it is right it switches immediately to "Pairing…" (which stays until pairing starts, not another line computed from the state before entry), and on success it changes to "Paired" and then disappears on its own; if the code is wrong, expired or the window is closed, the same notification says to get a new code and enter it again, keeping the input field. When accessibility is off, the guide page uses three numbered mini-illustrations for these three steps, and the fallback of entering the code directly on the page stays. The setup wizard's (`app/settings/ui/PhoneSetupScreen.kt`) state machine is a pure function `PhoneSetupFlow` (`core/device/shell/PhoneSetupFlow.kt`), and the switches it reads are only used to tick automatically and give hints, never to block: for developer options, USB debugging, accessibility and wireless debugging, reading them as on ticks them and moves on automatically; reading them as off stops at that step, but the three steps that read switches all have "I have turned it on, next" (recorded in `SetupInputs.confirmed`), and accessibility can be skipped (recommended: after pairing Claire turns it on itself, and it is not blocked by "restricted settings"), while the pairing step can be filled in on the page in addition to the notification and automatic code reading, so no step can get stuck. A completed step can be tapped to go back and redo it (`SetupInputs.revisit`, and the step gone back to returns to its place automatically when it reads off → on). Success only looks at whether the assistant is running, regardless of what was read before. When pairing or connecting fails (the pairing window closes, it cannot connect, the reason is unknown), it uses the developer options and wireless debugging readings to point at the step most likely to be the problem (`SetupView.suspect`: read as off, or unreadable on this phone) and gives a button back to that step; the same happens when the user confirms wireless debugging is on but the assistant reads it as off and does not connect, so it does not spin forever. After a disconnect it retries automatically (exponential backoff). The wireless debugging connection port (`core/device/adb/AdbConnectPort.kt`) is tried in order, stopping at whichever connects: the port that connected last time (stored in the App's private settings, and rejected quickly when stale), the system property `service.adb.tls.port` (used only when readable), every local port obtained from mDNS lookup of `_adb-tls-connect._tcp` (`NsdAdbServiceFinder`), a scan of local ports (`core/device/adb/LocalPortScan.kt`), and the port the user entered on the guide page. A TLS rejection does not necessarily mean the key is wrong (some systems let another service take the first TLS handshake on the same port and reject any certificate, and only an immediate reconnect reaches adbd): it tries the same port again after a moment, and if that still fails it goes on to the remaining candidates; a port rejected this way still leads to a local scan (skipping ports already tried), because the cache, property and mDNS may give an old port where some service still answers but does not recognize the current key, and adbd has moved to another port; only when every candidate fails and some port rejected us does it report `BuiltinShellFailure.AuthorizationRevoked` (re-pairing is needed, and the watchdog does not retry automatically). Scanning is the fallback when all of the above fail (some networks block multicast, some systems do not let the App read properties): wireless debugging's adbd listens on a port the system assigns at random, so it scans only the ephemeral port range (using `/proc/sys/net/ipv4/ip_local_port_range` when readable, otherwise 30000–65535) and only the machine's own addresses — 127.0.0.1 first, and only when nothing like adbd is found does it scan the Wi-Fi IPv4 address, never touching other devices; `NioPortScanner` uses java.nio non-blocking connects, with at most 200 in flight and at most 150 ms wait each, skipping ports already tried and listeners Claire itself has open (`OwnListeningPorts`, such as the sign-in callback); it scans 1024 ports at a time and verifies the open ones from that segment immediately, stopping when it finds one like adbd (not scanning the following segments or the next address), otherwise scanning the next segment, so an adbd that was open long ago is still verified in time even when scanning is slow and the budget is spent on scanning; an open port counts as like adbd only if it answers CNXN and replies STLS (connecting to itself, other services never reply STLS), and then it completes the TLS handshake with the paired key as usual, with the first one that connects winning and being cached; scanning and verification together take at most 10 seconds and run on an IO thread, `BuiltinShellManager.scanningPorts` is true while scanning, and the guide page shows "Looking for the wireless debugging port on this phone…". When it cannot connect, the guide page's connection step has "Enter the wireless debugging port" (numeric keyboard, 1–65535, and pasting a whole "IP address and port" takes what follows the colon); once entered it retries once with it (`BuiltinShellManager.connectWithPort`, used only this once, with no scan this time, and cached when it connects). On the guide page, besides success, every stage (waiting for the user, connecting, failed) has "Later" at the bottom, and back always leaves, so the user is never trapped on this page. Several capabilities — "Turn on accessibility for me", "Not limited by battery saver", "Restore automatically after restart", "Turn on wireless debugging automatically" — are on by default: they all need a paired connection to take effect, so each time the assistant becomes running (after pairing, after reconnecting from a drop, when the App starts already paired, or when automatic restore at boot connects) `BuiltinShellRecovery.start` (`app/cap/phone/setup/BuiltinShellRecovery.kt`) watches the state and through `RecoveryPermissions.applyPending` (`core/device/shell/RecoveryPermissions.kt`) fills in the ones the user has not personally turned off and that are not yet in effect, without a dialog, and on failure only records it for the settings page to mention. The record (`app/settings/RecoverySettings.kt`) keeps two things per item: "in effect" and "personally turned off by the user"; older versions had only the first, and anything never turned off counts as on by default. Unpairing withdraws the items in effect without touching the user's choices, so pairing again turns them on automatically as before, while ones personally turned off stay off; automatic restore at boot looks at whether "Restore automatically after restart" is in effect. Accessibility only adds Claire's own service and does not touch other services. Claire's accessibility service can stop for two reasons: after the process is killed by the system, the system treats it as a crash, so it is still on in settings but no longer bound; when it is force-stopped, the system removes it from the accessibility list directly. `BuiltinShellRecovery.start` watches all at once whether Claire's service is in the system list, whether the service instance is connected, and the assistant's state (all through callbacks, no polling); when the service has not been running for more than 3 seconds and there is a way to write system settings (if the App itself has permission to modify system settings it writes directly, without waiting for the assistant, and can repair even with wireless debugging off; only without permission does it wait for the assistant to be running), `AccessibilityRepair` (`core/device/shell/AccessibilityRepair.kt`) turns it on through `RecoveryPermissions.restartAccessibility`: if it is not in the list it is added directly, and if it is there but not bound it is removed, paused and added back, so the system rebinds it; it counts as repaired only when the service connects; if adding it back fails, the original list (together with the master switch) is written back and it still counts as a failure. When the user has not unlocked once since boot (`UserManager.isUserUnlocked` is false), the service cannot bind anyway, so it is not repaired then, and the status card only says it will be restored after unlocking; after receiving `ACTION_USER_UNLOCKED` it judges once more and repairs as usual if it is still not connected. Regardless of who turned it off, it is always turned on automatically, and is left alone only when "Turn on accessibility for me" is off in Claire; each stuck case is tried at most twice, and if that still fails it stops and leaves a hint for manual handling in the UI. When repaired, it only mentions the time under that item on the settings page. When a task connects to the phone it waits for the accessibility service for 5 seconds as usual; if a repair is about to run or is running (`AccessibilityRepair.phase`) it keeps waiting until the repair finishes (about half a minute at most), and only reports the phone unavailable after giving up. It is enabled only on Android 13 and above.
