Once the assistant is up, wireless debugging is no longer needed, but restarting it (phone reboot, Claire update, assistant gone) depends on wireless debugging, and stock Android turns it off when Wi-Fi disconnects and after a reboot. "Turn on wireless debugging automatically": when the watchdog wants to start the assistant, wireless debugging is off and Wi-Fi is connected, `WirelessDebuggingAutoOn` (`core/device/shell/WirelessDebuggingAutoOn.kt`) writes `adb_wifi_enabled` to 1 and then connects as usual; when Wi-Fi connects (network callback) it does the same if the assistant is absent. It only turns it on, never off, and does not touch USB debugging or developer options. It is written only by the App itself (it needs permission to modify system settings, shared with "Restore automatically after restart", and withdrawn only when both are off; and it is used only when no other connection is available for starting the assistant); without permission, or when the user turns this item off, it does not write, and the UI still offers the manual way to turn it on. Stock Android immediately flips the switch back to 0 on a Wi-Fi network that is not allowed and pops a dialog asking the user: if it reads the switch flipped back within 3 seconds of writing, it stops and reports "This Wi-Fi has not allowed wireless debugging yet" (`BuiltinShellFailure.WifiNotAllowed`), asking the user to turn it on personally once and check "Always allow", and it writes again only after the Wi-Fi changed, the user turned it on personally, retry was tapped, or the assistant came up, so it does not pop dialogs repeatedly; if the write is refused, or it has written 3 times in a row and the assistant still did not come up, it also stops; between two writes the interval starts at 10 seconds and doubles.
- **Root start and Root commands**: only when the phone already has `su` does Settings › Advanced and others show a separate Root page (`app/settings/ui/RootSection.kt`); capability detection only looks at the generic `su` path, does not execute an authorization request and does not identify a specific root implementation. Assistant start has no visible switch: only after the user personally taps authorize for the first time does `RootControl` request `su 2000` and persist the explicit shell UID authorization result; `RootStart.of` is always Off when there is no historical answer, so opening the App, a watchdog restart or a capability refresh never suddenly pops an authorization request. Once authorized, `BuiltinShellEngine` prefers starting the assistant through root with the same restricted shell identity, with the same handshake, token and signature checks as wireless debugging; after a refusal, no response, a wrong identity or `su` disappearing it stops requesting automatically, and retries only when the user taps authorize again. When wireless debugging pairing still exists, a root failure can fall back, and the USB start path is unchanged; unpairing clears the automatic start request and invalidates unfinished authorization callbacks. Root commands are another high-risk setting, off by default, requesting UID 0 authorization separately; only when both the switch and the authorization are valid does the main task `TaskRun` assemble `RootCapability`, and child tasks and condition checks do not get it. `RootCommandClass` lets only definite read-only commands run directly; modifying, deleting or indeterminate commands still go through the unified approval of `ActionExecutor`, and output is truncated uniformly.
- **adb identity (pairing key)**: Claire's own RSA-2048 key (public exponent F4) plus a self-signed certificate (`core/device/adb/AdbIdentity.kt`); pairing hands the public key to the device, and every later TLS connection presents the certificate, which the device accepts only for the key from pairing. The key is stored as PKCS#8 in the App's private directory, wrapped in an AES key from the Android Keystore (`WrappedKeyFile`; clearing `AndroidAdb.keyStore` deletes the wrapping key with it). Reading it back does not rely on the platform handing out a private key with CRT parameters: as long as it is an RSA private key it takes the modulus, uses the public exponent from the CRT form first, and when there is none reads it from the PKCS#8 RSAPrivateKey structure, falling back to F4 only when that is 0 too; before falling back it verifies with a signature, and if that does not match it treats the key as unreadable rather than connecting with a wrong public key; the same stored bytes read back the same public key as before, so upgrading needs no re-pairing. "Never stored" and "stored but unreadable" are strictly separated: `AdbKeyStore.read` returns null for the former, and throws `AdbKeyUnreadableException` for the latter (cannot be unwrapped, empty or truncated file, cannot be parsed). There is only one `AdbIdentitySource` (`AndroidAdb.identities`) per process, and pairing (`forPairing`) and connecting (`forConnect`) get the same cached key; if it was never stored it creates and stores one; if it is stored but unreadable, connecting throws and logs (tag MikaAdbKey) without touching the file, and the engine reports `BuiltinShellFailure.KeyUnreadable` ("the key used for pairing can no longer be read"; the one-tap fix is re-pairing, with no automatic retry), and only the pairing step deletes the unreadable key and stores a new one (pairing then hands the new key to the device); unpairing (`forget`) deletes the key and clears the cache. The `adb key status` line in diagnostics is the key's state in this process (NotLoaded / Loaded / Created / Unreadable and the reason); it only reads the state and never reads the key for diagnostics.
- **Starting the built-in assistant from the computer (USB)**: when wireless debugging pairing does not succeed, or when an Agent on the computer does the setup, the computer runs `adb shell sh /sdcard/Android/data/com.wangzan101.mika/files/usb-start.sh` through USB debugging, which starts the same assistant (the same `app_process` command and arguments), and hand-off also goes through the same `HelperHub.offer`, with the same checks of shell identity, APK, Binder process, token and signature fingerprint. The script is written by `UsbStartScript` (`app/cap/phone/setup/UsbStartScript.kt`) into `Context.getExternalFilesDir(null)` (the App itself needs no permission to write it, shell can read it and other Apps cannot), and its text comes from pure functions `HelperHandoff.usbScript` / `usbStub`. It is rewritten along with `HelperHub.shell`, and also when tokens are invalidated as a whole (`HandoffNonces.resets`, such as on unpairing) (`HelperHandoff.usbScriptWrites`; otherwise, while the assistant stays absent, the script would keep an invalidated token and hand-off would be refused with BadNonce): when the assistant is absent (App start, someone needs the assistant but it is not there, opening the guide page) it writes a script with a one-time token, `HandoffNonces.usbNonce` — kept together with the start token and the resume token, and when any one of them is used the rest are invalidated; it expires 24 hours by wall clock (it has to survive a reboot), and is reused while more than half of its lifetime remains, so it is not swapped out while the computer is running it; when the assistant is connected it is replaced by a note without a token ("already running", and if the process is gone it asks the user to open Claire first), leaving no usable token on disk. The script only starts when `id -u` is 2000; the assistant uses setsid to start a new session and is put in a background subshell (which is reparented to init after it exits), the token goes through the built-in echo into standard input through a pipe, and output is discarded, so when the adb session ends and adbd kills that process group after the cable is unplugged, the assistant is not affected; the script waits 2 seconds and then uses `pidof` to check whether the assistant process is still there, and says "Starting; open Claire to confirm" or "It did not start; open Claire first and run it again". When unpaired it accepts the assistant, the engine records "started by the computer" (`BuiltinShellPlatform.usbStarted`), the state is running as usual, and what follows (filling in those capabilities, turning on accessibility) is the same as after pairing: `commandRunner` runs commands through the assistant's `runCommand` while it is there, and only connects to the paired adbd when it is not. An assistant started this way is gone after a reboot; when it was never paired, Claire cannot start it itself, so a new process waits a while first (a still-living assistant comes to hand off by itself), and if none arrives it reports `BuiltinShellFailure.UsbHelperStopped` ("the phone assistant has stopped": run the command on the computer once more, or pair wireless debugging so it recovers automatically), and automatic restore at boot also does not touch wireless debugging; unpairing clears this record too. The guide page normally does not offer this path; only when needed (`SetupView.usbFallback`, computed by `PhoneSetupFlow`) does it put a card in the current step, "Can't turn on wireless debugging on this phone? Start it from the computer", with one explanatory sentence, the command and a copy button. When it is needed: the system has no `adb_wifi_enabled` setting (`SetupInputs.wirelessMissing`; stock Android always writes it to 0 or 1 at boot since Android 11, readable whether it is on or off, so not reading it means the system does not have wireless debugging, and a read error draws no conclusion); the user tapped "Can't find wireless debugging?" on the wireless debugging step (normally the only hint line on that step); the pairing window is never found, it cannot connect, the computer-started assistant stopped, or the user says wireless debugging is on but the assistant reads it as off; and while stuck on the wireless debugging step, the pairing step (pairing window not found yet) or automatic connection (`PhoneSetupFlow.stallPoint`) with no progress for 45 seconds (`SetupInputs.stalledMs`, restarted when the place changes). The first two are offered only after reaching the wireless debugging step (starting from the computer also requires finishing developer options and USB debugging first). Once the card has appeared it stays (recorded in `SetupInputs.usbAsked`) until success. It does not treat "wrote 1 and it was flipped back to 0" as a signal: stock Android does the same on a Wi-Fi network that is not allowed (it pops a dialog asking the user), so on a normal phone that would be a false positive; and the wireless debugging page has no separate public deep link to judge by either.
- **Pulled back up by the assistant after Claire is killed**: when the system or a cleaner ends Claire's process (on force-stop the system also removes Claire's accessibility service from `enabled_accessibility_services`), the floating capsule disappears with it. The built-in assistant does not die with it: it learns from the death notification of the Claire token it received at hand-off (`HelperProcess.onAppDied`), and first waits `HelperRestorePolicy.GRACE_MS` (8 seconds); if Claire comes back during that time (the uid importance listener reports it up and it hands off again) it does nothing; if it does not come back, it decides by the wish Claire handed over last (`RestoreWish`, through `IMikaShell.setRestoreWish`): pull it up if the capsule is set to always show, or if a task was running when it died, otherwise (for example the user force-stopped it personally, or the capsule only shows during tasks) leave it stopped. When pulling it up it first appends Claire's accessibility service back to the list and turns on `accessibility_enabled` (`RestoreCommands.accessibility`, not touching any other service; it writes only when the user has not turned off "Turn on accessibility for me" in Claire and the list really had it while Claire was alive, and it does not turn it on for a user who turned it off in system settings while Claire was alive — absence from the list only counts after 3 seconds, so that the force-stop moment does not change the wish), then hands off again as usual: when it takes the provider Claire uses for hand-off, the system starts its process (shell can take any App's provider, which is the path the `content` command uses; a provider is not subject to the restriction on starting services in the background and shows no UI), `MikaApp.onCreate` runs as usual, and the capsule, watchdog and automatic accessibility turn-on come up with it. It pulls at most 3 times in 10 minutes, stops if the pull immediately dies again, and waits for the window to pass; every step is recorded in the assistant's log (logcat tag MikaHelper). The wish is handed over by `RestoreWishSync` (`app/cap/phone/setup/`) when the capsule mode, whether a task is running, "Turn on accessibility for me", or the accessibility list changes, or when the assistant changes (`RestoreWishSender` hands it over only when something changed or the Binder changed). At every hand-off the assistant reports to Claire the last pull time, the most recent ones, and the last time it gave up because it was too frequent (`HelperRestoreReport`); at every start Claire looks at whether the accessibility service was removed, or is in the list but still not bound after 5 seconds (`A11yStartCheck`, only counted after the service has connected once), and records it in `RecoverySettings`; both are written into the Recovery section of "Copy diagnostics" (`restored by helper`, `accessibility on start`). When paired, the watchdog starts the assistant as soon as Claire starts (`BuiltinShellEngine.start`: if nobody comes to hand off, it starts one through the paired connection after 3 seconds, without waiting for a task to need it), so that it is present; when not paired it does not connect. Some systems also shut down background Apps, and a sentence below the floating capsule in Settings asks the user to allow Claire to run in the background and start at boot in system settings.
- **Reading and operating**: the accessibility service (`app/a11y/MikaAccessibilityService.kt` → `app/cap/phone/a11y/ScreenAccessibility.kt`) reads the control tree per screen and performs gestures on a specified screen. Each screen read first judges from the control tree whether it is good enough (`core/screen/uistate/TreeQuality.kt`): it counts as clearly good only if enough elements have names, most controls have names, and no large region of the App window has not a single character, and then it does not recognize text; when unsure it reads once with the on-device text recognition bundled in the APK (`app/cap/phone/a11y/PhoneOcr.kt`), and if the characters missing from the tree add up to three lines or more the tree counts as missing a piece (partial); when the tree is missing a piece or unreadable (unusable) it always recognizes text (main models that cannot see images, answer sources and verification, and `phone_collect` all rely on it); screens protected against screenshots are not recognized. The filled-in characters (`core/screen/uistate/VisibleText.kt`) first drop misrecognized lines (low confidence, all symbols, scattered Latin digit strings on a mostly Chinese screen), and each line carries a 0–999 position and tap center; when the tree is not good enough the screen-read result writes `treeQuality` and one hint: aim at the center of text on the screen, locate controls without text (icons, images, input fields) by sight, to type into a field give the point where typing starts or say in element which field it is, and use a whole-screen description only when you cannot tell what page it is (it is several times slower, so do not use it to find one control). When the main model can see images and "read elements first, see images when necessary" is on, the picture is attached as well. Before recognizing text it first waits for the picture to settle (self-drawn UIs almost never send accessibility events, and when events go quiet the picture may still be transitioning), and if the whole page changed during recognition it recognizes the new page once more, while local motion (animated avatars, cursor) is still used (`app/cap/phone/a11y/StillFrameRead.kt`); it also recognizes as usual when the tree changes during the read. When the tree is not good enough and not one line was read, the screen-read result writes `ocr` (empty, illegible, timeout, unavailable, failed) and the hint switches to finding input fields and buttons by sight. Input is normally written directly into the input field, web-like editing areas are pasted from the clipboard, and afterwards the user's original clipboard is put back. When the tree has no input field holding focus (the App draws its own UI), it selects all and pastes from the clipboard, with Ctrl+A / Ctrl+V sent by the built-in assistant only to Claire's screen (`injectCtrlKey`), and then checks by comparing the text recognized on screen before and after typing (`core/screen/uistate/UnseenField.kt`: one more occurrence means it matches, no change at all means nothing was typed, and anything else means unverifiable; characters on the keyboard while the input method is showing do not count). It does not require the input method to be showing: key events go to the window holding focus on this screen, and the field receives them without the input method; if the input method is not showing and nothing changed at all, the focus is most likely not on the field, so it reports `no_focus` asking the model to tap the input field first instead of blindly pasting again. When nothing on the screen holds input focus (no input focus in any window) and the input method is not showing, it does not paste at all and reports `no_focus`: there is no field to receive it, and pasting while something else on the UI moves would be taken as "possibly typed in" (the same for clearing). Clearing likewise uses select-all plus the delete key, and likewise reports `no_focus` when the input method is not showing and the UI did not change. Input and clear also accept a point (coordinates, or `@vN` found by sight, checked like a tap that the picture did not change first): it first taps that point and waits for the UI to settle; if that reveals an input field visible in the tree it types into it as usual, otherwise it pastes as above, with the on-screen text before pasting read after the tap; if nothing changed at all after pasting it reports `point_not_typed` (that point is most likely not an input field), does not retry automatically, and does not press enter when unverifiable (`ScreenPort.typeAt` / `clearAt`, `AndroidScreen.typeAt`). On a screen whose tree cannot expose the input field there is no need to tap before typing: a tap that only gives the field focus changes neither the tree nor the picture, and the typing check is the evidence that it was hit. When typing at a point, that tap also goes through the irreversibility decision (if it hit a send-like button it is judged as a tap). When an input field is named by description (`element`) and the tree has no match, and this screen's tree is missing a piece or unusable, the execution entry finds that field by sight at the step where it swaps the id (`Interfaces.bySight`, when an image model is configured; the same checks as `find_visually`, with ones lacking evidence marked), replaces the parameter with the `@vN` found, and afterwards verifies the picture, judges irreversibility and verifies after pasting just as when the model passes `@vN` itself; the result writes `foundBySight` and the characters seen, saving the round of finding a point before typing.
- **Gesture pacing** (`core/cap/phone/GesturePacing.kt`; random values, arcs, easing and the web mouse's `InputPacing` share `core/screen/uistate/Pace.kt`): before each finger-down it pauses 30–120 ms; a tap holds 50–120 ms, a double tap 40–90 ms each with 80–140 ms between the two (within the system's double-tap limit); when tapping an element the landing point is slightly off center (no more than one fifth of the width or height, at most 12 pixels, no offset in a direction narrower than 24 pixels, and always inside the element), while tapping by coordinates is off by at most 3 pixels. For swipes (`phone_swipe`, the gesture that `phone_scroll` falls back to, single-finger `phone_gesture`) the start and end points are unchanged, the path between them follows a short arc (deviating by no more than 6% of the distance and at most 30 pixels; multi-point paths follow the drawn path), the duration varies by 8% around the given value, and the start is slow: a fast flick shorter than 800 ms releases at a speed comparable to the previous uniform swipe (the fling distance is unchanged), while a slow drag of 800 ms or more settles before lifting and does not fling; when the start and end almost coincide (holding in place) it only lengthens and never shortens. Pacing requires each point to have its own timing, which accessibility gestures cannot do (the system moves uniformly along the path), so a paced stroke is injected point by point at the right times by the built-in assistant (`injectStroke`, sent only to Claire's screen); when the assistant cannot inject, it falls back to an accessibility gesture along the same arc; taps still use accessibility gestures, and only fall back to assistant injection when that fails. Two-finger gestures only vary the duration. The control's own click and scroll actions (`ACTION_CLICK`, `ACTION_SCROLL_*`) and input are unaffected. `GesturePacing.Instant` is exactly as before (for tests).
- **Operating pace** (Settings › Task runs › Operating pace, `core/cap/phone/OperatingPace.kt`, stored in `FeatureFlags.operatingPace`): all three gesture modes look human, and the other three things follow it — "Fast" (`FAST`: no extra pause between actions, typing written in one go, no screen cap, i.e. the previous behavior), "Standard" (`STANDARD`, the default), and "Human" (`HUMAN`: every App uses the slow dwell and typing set). It is read once at the start of each task (`TaskRun`), the eval and device acceptance override it through `EvalHooks.operatingPace`, defaulting to "Fast" (`-e evalPace` / `-e livePace` switch it to `standard`, `human`), without changing the setting; core's `toolDispatcher` defaults to `AppPacing.Off` and `AndroidScreen` to `TypingPacing.Instant`, so tests keep running fast.
  - **Typing** (`core/cap/phone/TypingPacing.kt`, pure logic): after finding the input field, `AndroidScreen.typeText` first writes it piece by piece according to the pace into an ever-longer prefix (`ACTION_SET_TEXT`, which works for any native input field, CJK text included), and the last piece hands the whole text to `typeAndVerify` as before to write in full, read back and verify, and retry. Latin letters take 60–170 ms each; CJK characters take 160–380 ms each, with 1–3 consecutive ones appearing together (like choosing words in an input method), and after punctuation and spaces there is sometimes an extra 0.3–0.9 s pause; with many characters it writes several per piece, at most 40 pieces, for at most 6 seconds in total (for pacing-limited Apps and "Human": 90–230 / 220–520 ms, pauses 0.5–1.4 s, at most 60 pieces, 12 seconds). A single character is never split (surrogate pairs, combining marks, variation selectors, zero-width-joiner emoji sequences). After writing the first piece it reads back: if the field does not accept partial characters (the read-back does not match or cannot be read) or cannot be written, it stops and writes the whole text as before. Password fields, web fields that only accept pasting (`TypingPacing.forField`) and fields not visible in the tree (paste) are still written in one go as before.
  - **Dwell between actions** (`core/cap/phone/AppPacing.kt` pure logic, `AppDwell.kt` wired into the phone capability): every action that changes the screen goes through the single execution entry `ActionExecutor`, which asks this screen's capability before executing (`Capability.beforeWrite`; the phone capability dwells according to `AppDwell`) and tells it the result afterwards (`afterWrite`); single steps, each step in a `do_steps` batch and replay are all the same, composite actions (such as `phone_run`) do not dwell themselves and each step inside them dwells on its own; the web still uses its own page-by-page dwell (`PageDwell`) and does not go through here. If the previous action's result effect is `done` or `place_changed` it counts as having moved to a new screen, otherwise it is the same screen: in Standard it dwells 0.5–1.2 s on the same screen and 0.9–2 s on a new one, with a 5% chance of an extra 0.6–1.5 s, at most 3.5 s at a time; for pacing-limited Apps (App knowledge `pace: careful`, looked up by package name through `GuideCatalog.appPace`) and for "Human" it dwells 0.9–2.2 s on the same screen and 2.5–5 s on a new one, plus reading time of 15 ms per character of the screen the model read most recently (at most 8 s), with a 10% chance of an extra 2–5 s, at most 15 s at a time. The dwell counts from when the previous action finished, including the time the model spends thinking and reading the screen, and only fills in what is missing; how long it dwelt is recorded together with the outward-action interval as this call's wait (run record `tool_result.waitMs`) and is not told to the model; if the user interjects during the dwell the action is not performed and it reports `skipped_for_new_message`. On a new screen in the slow set, when the dwell is long enough and the next action does not act by position on the screen (back, key press, scroll by direction), the dwell slowly scrolls a short distance at the center of scrollable content that fills most of the screen according to `IdleScroll` (`AndroidScreen.idleScroll`, a slow drag that settles before lifting; it does not scroll while the input method is showing, an input field holds focus, another app window covers it, or there is no scrollable content large enough), and the scrolls only go into the run record (`idleScrolls`), with the screen in the model's hands marked stale (`ScreenSession.movedByMika`).
  - **Screen cap** (not set by default: `AppPacing.screenCap` is null. Stopping hard to ask a person would make read-and-summarize tasks and unattended tasks such as scheduled and proactive ones unable to finish, so the risk of being blocked is pressed down by longer dwells): when `screenCap` is given, a pacing-limited App may move to a new screen at most that many times in one task (scrolling down to read the same page does not count); after that, actions that change screens in the App (taps, swipes, back, key presses, gestures) are not performed and it reports the same `page_limit` as the web (`core/screen/uistate/DwellStop.kt`, whose explanation tells the model to answer first with what it has read and to use `ask_user` if it needs to keep looking); typing, clearing, scrolling, turning a wheel and opening other Apps work as usual. The count restarts after the user has answered (`Capability.onUserAnswered`); outward actions themselves are not blocked, and once one has been done in this App the task no longer sets a cap (through `Capability.onOutwardEnd`). Ordinary Apps have no cap.
- **Tools** (`core/cap/phone/`, `core/screen/uistate/tools/`):
  - Basic: `phone_observe`, `phone_list_elements`, `phone_tap`, `phone_type_text`, `phone_clear_text`, `phone_scroll`, `phone_swipe`, `phone_back`, `phone_press_key`, `phone_launch_app`, `phone_share_text`, `phone_open_link`, `phone_list_apps`, `phone_screenshot`, `phone_diagnose`. Vertical `phone_scroll` by default prefers a scrollable control and falls back to a gesture only when none is found.
  - Query and composite: `phone_query` (find by text, resource ID or control type in the full control tree, text only), `phone_run` (an ordered list of commands, each step re-locating on the current page, with text conditions deciding whether to continue; it stops and hands back to the model when the target is missing, cannot be told apart, or the App switches), `phone_collect` (read a long list or chat screen by screen and merge by overlap), `phone_find` (scroll to find a target), `phone_extract` (visible text, list rows, form fields; passwords are not returned), `phone_dialog` (read a dialog and tap only when a unique button text is given), `phone_wait`, `phone_event` (wait for or collect window, content and notification events), `phone_gesture` (single-finger path or two fingers), `phone_set_wheel` (turn a wheel to a value, see below).
  - System actions: `phone_set_alarm`, `phone_set_timer`, `phone_show_alarms`, `phone_add_calendar_event` (see below).
  - Other: `phone_system_command` (run one system command through the built-in assistant, separate from the Linux sandbox; 10-second timeout; when the command does what a dedicated tool does — tapping, swiping, typing, pressing keys, opening Apps — or fails twice in a row, the result reminds the model to use the dedicated tool instead; after that reminder in this task, commands that do those kinds of things are not executed (`use_phone_tool`, which only replies with the tool to use), sharing text through shell (`am start` with SEND and no file) is never executed and points to `phone_share_text`, and commands that check status or read settings are unaffected, `core/cap/phone/CommandSteer.kt`); the sight-based tools are below.
- **Sharing text to an App**: `phone_share_text` starts a text share (ACTION_SEND text/plain, EXTRA_TEXT, delivered only to the specified package) on Claire's screen (the built-in assistant's `startShare`, which like opening an App checks whether the user is using it on the main screen and whether another task occupies it), and then, like `phone_launch_app`, waits for it to reach the foreground, recognizes the system asking which one to open (installed twice; when this App has several pages that receive shares the system asks which one, and the top window becoming the system package's window also counts), and records the task. It only opens the share page and does not send: the recipient and the send key are tapped by the model on that page, and sending goes through the irreversibility confirmation as usual. When the App does not accept text shares it reports `cannot_share`. In Apps that cannot be read or typed into, sending messages and taking notes go through it first (`core/cap/phone/AppTools.kt`, `AndroidScreen.shareText`).
- **System actions (alarms, timers, calendar)**: `phone_set_alarm` (hour, minute, label, days of the week to repeat, vibration), `phone_set_timer` (seconds, label), `phone_show_alarms` and `phone_add_calendar_event` (title, start and end, all day, location, notes) are handed to the phone's clock and calendar Apps through the standard Android interfaces (`AlarmClock.ACTION_SET_ALARM` / `ACTION_SET_TIMER` with `EXTRA_SKIP_UI`, `ACTION_SHOW_ALARMS`, and the calendar's `ACTION_INSERT` with the type written explicitly as `vnd.android.cursor.dir/event`; no calendar permission is needed, and `SET_ALARM` for setting alarms is a normal permission), instead of operating their UIs slot by slot (`core/cap/phone/SystemActionTools.kt`, where the intent is first written by `app/cap/phone/shell/SystemIntents.kt` as an Android-independent Spec and then turned into an Intent). It first asks the package manager on Claire's side who handles it (the manifest's `<queries>` lists these kinds): when no App handles it it reports `not_supported` and the model falls back to operating the clock and calendar UIs; when several do, the user's default is used if one was set, otherwise the system-bundled one is chosen first, targeting that page directly so no "which one" chooser is left on screen. Then it is handed to the built-in assistant to start on Claire's screen (`startSystemAction`, which only lets these kinds of intents through, and like opening a link checks whether the receiving App is being used on the main screen and whether another task occupies it; it watches the main screen before and after the action as usual, and moves back anything that opened on the main screen). When the receiving page requires a permission shell does not have (the clock's set-alarm page requires `SET_ALARM`), the assistant instead sends a PendingIntent prepared by Claire: it starts as Claire, its permissions are checked as Claire's, and the screen and background start are checked as the sending assistant's, while still landing on Claire's screen (from Android 14 the sender explicitly allows background starts). Alarms and timers are supposed to be set quietly: before and after setting an alarm it reads the system's next alarm (`AlarmManager.getNextAlarmClock`, waiting at most 3 seconds), and the result's `check` is verified (the next one is it), earlier_alarm_first (an earlier alarm is in front of it, so it cannot be seen), not_seen or unknown, with the latter two and the blocked case telling the user to look once with `phone_show_alarms` when they need to be sure; when the App does open its own UI instead it reports `screenOpened` and asks the model to confirm on that page. For the alarm list and a new calendar event it waits for that App to reach the top and records the task; a new event is not saved for the user, and the model taps the save key on that page. Setting alarms and timers are entries that are enough on their own inside the collapsed phone group (`Entry.Alone`, without expanding the whole group), a new calendar event is an entry that expands the whole group, and the alarm list is in the group; the risk is the same as opening an App (it only changes things on the phone and spends no money, so no confirmation).
- **Wheel pickers**: for wheels that turn slot by slot, such as time, date and region, `phone_set_wheel` (the wheel or the id of the item it currently shows, and the value to show) turns to the value in one call in the port, without the model looking and swiping one slot at a time (`core/cap/phone/SetWheel.kt`, `app/cap/phone/a11y/WheelDriver.kt`). The given id can be the wheel or one of the items it shows (self-drawn wheels are often the selected item): in that case the wheel is the column found upwards that aligns horizontally with this item (at least three items, with similar spacing, `WheelMath.column`), and other wheels side by side in the same block (the minutes next to the hours) are not read or dragged; a scrollable container found upwards is used for accessibility scrolling only if it covers the middle of this item horizontally (a page-wide scroll area does not count). It tries in order what the control can do: if the wheel contains an input field (stock numeric wheels do) it writes the value in, makes it lose focus to confirm (so the input method that popped up is dismissed) and reads it back; if it supports accessibility "scroll forward / backward" (stock numeric wheels one slot at a time) it reads the current value and scrolls in batches of at most 5 steps according to `WheelSteps`, reading back and recomputing each batch, deciding direction by numeric difference and taking the nearer side when a cycle is visible (…58 59 00 01…), correcting on the first read-back whether "forward" increases or decreases, and when the value stops moving: if no cycle is visible this side has reached the end and the target is out of range (`at_end`), if a cycle is visible (no end) this attempt did nothing and it hands over to dragging (`not_moving`), and if one step turned more than one slot or it crossed the target back and forth several times it hands over to dragging, at most 80 steps; for text lists, a target in view is reached by position difference, and one not in view is scanned toward one side and reversed at the end; when none of that works (a wheel the App draws itself) it reads the several items visible in this column (or recognizes the characters on screen when accessibility cannot read them) to estimate the slot height and the current value, puts the finger at the horizontal center of this column and drags up starting from a lower item (dragging down starts from a higher one, and it may drag beyond this column), dragging slowly by the number of slots (at least 1 second, and when pacing is on, settling before lifting without flinging, so as not to overshoot), and after reading back it calibrates the slot height from the number of slots actually turned and drags again, at most 5 rounds (`WheelDrags`); a drag that does not move the value does not count as the end, and only when no cycle is visible and there are no more items in the direction to go is it `at_end`, otherwise it tries a fast flick (0.4 seconds) and drags a bit farther when only one slot is left, reporting `not_moving` after 3 consecutive non-moves and suggesting the model swipe this column with phone_swipe itself. The pure algorithms for reading values, direction, cycles, slot counts and slot height are in `core/cap/phone/WheelMath.kt` (`"09"` is `"9"`, `"40min"` is `"40"`; a selected item's accessibility name often carries status words, so `"Selected, 21"` is `"21"`, and a whole string with only one number takes it; text values are also compared by segments split at punctuation; a stateDescription the system reports separately is removed first when it is spliced into the name; when only one item is marked isSelected it is the selected one, otherwise it is the item whose own upper and lower range covers the center line, and when none covers it (some wheels only report the two above and below) the middle slot is derived from the two adjacent numbers (between 39 and 41 is 40, between 59 and 01 is 00, between 11 and 1 is 12, `WheelMath.between`), falling back to the nearest to the center line only when that cannot be derived; after every scroll, drag or input it waits for settling (two reads 0.35 seconds apart with the same value) before reading, and after reaching the target it checks once more when settled, continuing to turn if it is wrong, and reporting reached only when the settled value matches; the `value` and `from` reported to the model keep only the value segment). The result gives `value` (currently shown), `from`, `attempts` and `method` (text, actions, drag), and when it does not reach the target it reports `wheel_at_end`, `wheel_unreadable`, `wheel_not_moving` or `wheel_not_reached` and truthfully gives the value currently shown. A node in the screen-read tree that looks like a wheel is marked `wheel: true` (a stock numeric wheel control, or one that scrolls and has three to nine short values arranged vertically and evenly around the center; it prefers missing one over misidentifying, `WheelMath.looksLikeWheel`, `ScreenReader.isWheel`). It can be put in a multi-step batch, setting several wheels (hour, minute) in one batch.
- **App installed twice**: when the same App is installed for two users (clone, work profile), some systems ask which one to open. After `phone_launch_app`, if the top window has switched to the system package's window and a tappable option in it mentions this App's name, that counts as opened, and the result carries `chooser` (the options and which one is the other copy) and a hint: choose as the user said, and if they did not say, choose the original copy and do not open it again; that window being in front does not count as being switched away, and once the App is in front after the choice its task is recorded (`core/screen/uistate/LaunchChooser.kt`, `AndroidScreen.openApp`). The other copy's user name shown next to the options is still read through `pm list users`.
- **Opening another copy of an App**: many systems ask which one to open only on the launcher icon, not when a program starts it, so another copy can never be opened. Therefore `phone_list_apps` lists each other copy as a separate item (the same package name, name written as "App name (another copy: that user's name)", with `user`), and `phone_launch_app` with this `user` opens that copy; without it, it opens the original copy as before. Which copies users can use is judged by the built-in assistant by Android's multi-user concepts (`core/screen/uistate/AppCopies.kt`, reading UserManager's UserInfo and UserProperties, with the labels taken from the system's own names: reflect `UserInfo.flagsToString`, and when that fails read the name and parentId in the parentheses of `dumpsys user`, without hard-coding flag bits): types with private, label names with HIDDEN or PRIVATE, ones the system marks as hidden from other Apps (private space and the like), and guest/demo/ephemeral/disabled ones are all neither listed nor opened; the rest must be running and unlocked, not in quiet mode, connected to the current user (same profile group, or the parent user is the current user), and recognizable as a clone (type with clone, label name with CLONE, or sharing media with the parent) or the original work profile. If any item cannot be read it counts as not satisfied. Some systems make a clone a full user with a clone flag (the type is not the original's clone type), recognized by the label name. The built-in assistant's `appCopyUsers` gives the usable user ids and a one-line judgment for diagnostics (only the id, flags and label name, type, profile group, parent), `startAppInUser` checks once more before starting, and returns 8 (`copy_not_found`) when it is not among them. In the other user it first queries that user's launcher page (`queryIntentActivitiesAsUser`, or failing that asks `cmd package query-activities --user N`), first going the launcher-icon route: ILauncherApps.startActivityAsUser (the interface behind `LauncherApps.startMainActivity`; the system checks cross-user permission by caller, shell has INTERACT_ACROSS_USERS_FULL, and the package name matches the uid), with ActivityOptions that start onto Claire's screen — some systems always pop "open with which one" for another copy started by a program, and this launcher route does not; failing that it uses `startActivityAsUser` as that user, and finally falls back to `am start --user N --display D -n component`. When the system still asks on Claire's screen (the option names may be exactly the same), `phone_launch_app` taps the options one by one itself and reads the user the top task belongs to: if it is the wanted copy that counts as opened; if not it presses back once to send it to the background (not finishing it, since that may be the user's own copy), opens again and taps the next; if none of them, it fails with `copy_not_opened`, says which copy was opened, and does not treat the wrong copy as success. When the target App is installed twice, every result carries `inProfile` and `profileUser` (the user the copy on screen currently belongs to), and when the other copy was wanted it also carries `requestedCopy`; when the original copy was opened it says plainly that it is not the wanted one. Occupancy is counted per "package name + user" (`ShellService.sameCopy`): when the user is using the original copy on the main screen it does not block Claire from using the other copy, and vice versa; when the user a task belongs to cannot be read it counts as occupied. A cold start only `am force-stop --user N` that copy. The target App records the user its task belongs to (`TargetApp.userId`), and another copy of the same App replacing it on Claire's screen also counts as switched away; parallel tasks occupy the two copies separately; muting acts per "package + user", and when the target is the other copy only that copy is muted, without touching the copy in the user's hands. Screen reading, tapping and typing follow the screen and need no change.
- **Apps opened onto the user's main screen**: when an App on Claire's screen opens another App not from its own page on this screen, but from the background (a service, a redirect, the system chooser, a cross-user hand-off), Android by default puts it on the main screen (and when it is installed twice it first asks on the main screen which one to open), and nothing appears to change on Claire's screen. Before every tap, swipe, key press and similar action (`AndroidScreen.verified`) it asks the built-in assistant to watch the main screen for 6 seconds (`watchMainScreen`): the assistant registers a task-stack listener with the system (ITaskStackListener, only as a "changed" signal, re-reading the task list when received; when registration fails it looks once every 100 ms during the watch), records the task that came to the top of the main screen, which screen it was on before, and which user it belongs to (`app/cap/phone/shell/MainScreenWatch.kt`, `MainScreenLog.kt`), and looks once more on the spot when queried (`mainArrivals`). After the action it judges by the pure logic `MainScreenSpill`: an ordinary App page that appeared during this time (not the launcher or recents or the like, not Claire itself or the launcher App), that was not moved from another screen (takeover), that is still on the main screen right now, while the user did not tap anything on the main screen during this time (accessibility tap events), and that is not the previous top one showing up again, is moved back to Claire's screen (the same task-moving call as takeover, `moveTask`). If what spilled onto the main screen this time is a login verification, account confirmation, payment confirmation or similar dialog that needs the user's own hands, it is not moved: pages of the system account sign-in service (packages that registered an account authenticator and have no launcher icon of their own; `AccountManager.authenticatorTypes` is read fresh each time, and it counts only when no LAUNCHER entry is found; what such packages spill onto the main screen is only add-account, sign-in and verification pages; authenticator packages that do have a launcher icon, such as a chat App or mail App that registered an account to sync contacts, do not count and are judged by the other criteria as usual, `MainScreenSpill.authenticatorUi`; the manifest queries the authenticator services so that such packages are visible to Claire), whose top window is not a full-screen dialog or bottom sheet, whose window has a password field, or whose page itself stays out of recents (`excludeFromRecents`) (`AndroidScreen.needsUserWindow`; a window counts as not full-screen when its width or height is less than 80% of the main screen; it looks again after 300 ms so as not to mistake an ordinary App still in its opening animation for a dialog) — all of these stay on the user's screen, are reported as `onUserScreen` and `needsUser`, and a hint asks the user to handle them there through `hand_to_user`; after the dialog has appeared, if the user has already tapped on it (the accessibility tap event's package is it) it is not moved either (`MainScreenSpill.Tap`). **Claire never opens or reopens another App or a system page on its behalf**: the opening party must remain the original App (for example a shopping App opens a payment App, and the payment side has to know who opened it), moving a task does not change the opening party but reopening does, so it only moves and never reopens. If the top task on the main screen changed page before the action, that is the App the user is using being opened, so it is left alone; nothing is moved during takeover. When the system asks on the main screen which one to open (a system package's window, being the system chooser, or options in the window matching an App installed twice; each system's chooser is not necessarily the stock one, `MainScreenSpill.handling`), it is **not moved**: if moved to another screen it has to rebuild for the new screen, and some close themselves during the rebuild. It stays on the user's screen, and the action result carries `opened` (picker, onUserScreen), the recognizable options `chooser`, and a hint: the system is asking on the user's screen, do not tap again and do not choose for the user; the next action waits for the user to finish choosing. The task stops before the next screen-changing action (`TaskRun.awaitUserPick` hooked in front of `TaskGate`, `AndroidScreen.awaitUserPick`), the state is a wait that needs your handling (`GateReason.needsYou`, on the capsule `CapsuleKind.NeedStep`, "Needs you: \"<App>\" is asking which one to use, please choose on the screen"), and it keeps watching the main screen during this time; after the user has chosen (the chooser is gone) it looks at what was chosen (`MainScreenSpill.picked`): an ordinary page opened on the main screen is moved back to Claire's screen to continue, reported as inProfile; one opened on Claire's screen counts in as well; one that needs the user's own hands (the window has a password field, or the tree reads nothing while screenshotting the main screen and the system reports a secure window, like payment or password entry; an App whose tree simply cannot be read is moved back as usual, `MainScreenSpill.needsUser`) stays on the main screen and becomes a pause that needs your handling and can be continued by tapping ("\"<App>\" needs you to handle it on the screen; tap continue when done"), and after the user taps continue, if it is still on the main screen it is moved back, and if it has been closed it says so truthfully (`byUser`); if the user closed the problem (`closed`) it says so truthfully, and the model decides the next step. These results carry `opened` in the next screen read, and the next action is not performed and reports `opened_elsewhere`. The `opened` in the result (package, app, pulledBack, onUserScreen, needsUser, inProfile, picker, closed, byUser) comes with hints: if it was moved back, do not tap again and continue there; one moved back counts as opened by the target App, not as switched away, and the effect is that the place changed rather than no effect; one left on the user's screen (the user is using it) says do not come again and asks the user to handle it; a dialog that needs the user's own hands says do not move it back and asks the user to tap there (`needsUser`). An App installed twice reports which copy was opened after asking which one (`inProfile` main/other, by the user the task belongs to), and for the other copy hints that the user did not want it, so go back and choose the other one. If it opens only after a while, it is moved back before the next screen read, the screen read carries `opened`, and the next action is not performed and reports `opened_elsewhere`. A batch of actions or a phone script stops when it encounters this.
- **Takeover**: when something needs the user's own hands (sign-in, verification code, etc.), `hand_to_user` sends a notification; the user taps "I'll handle it" and the App on the virtual screen is moved to the main screen; when done they tap "Handled" or "I've finished it myself" and it is moved back, and the Agent re-reads the UI to verify before continuing; "Skip" does not count as done (`app/takeover/Takeover.kt`). Takeovers of several tasks are queued.

### web: built-in browser

- WebView renders on a private virtual display created by the App (`app/cap/web/WebHost.kt`), does not take up the phone screen, and does not need the built-in assistant; it uses desktop pages by default (`app/cap/web/cdp/DesktopProfile.kt`), and can remember desktop or mobile per website (`web_site_mode`). When the user has chosen an interface language in Claire (not following the system), web pages are requested in that language (`app/cap/web/cdp/PageLanguage.kt`): Accept-Language and navigator.language(s) are changed through `Emulation.setUserAgentOverride`'s acceptLanguage (e.g. English → `en-US,en`), added on top of the desktop identity for desktop mode, while mobile mode keeps the WebView default UA and UA-CH and only changes the language; if the first page was already sent before the session was set up, it reloads once after setup, and a tab whose language changed midway switches before the next main-document load; when following the system it does not override and sends the string the system gives.
- Pages are operated through the WebView DevTools protocol (`app/cap/web/cdp/WebDevtools.kt`); clicks and input are real input events; when it cannot connect it falls back to injected page scripts. It never opens the Runtime group (`Runtime.enable`): with it on, the page can tell a debugger is attached, and on real devices some websites therefore keep reloading forever; reading scripts and page scripts uses only the isolated world and evaluation (a page script's mika calls go through an outbox in that world and are handed back by an always-attached intake evaluation, without the binding that needs Runtime), and a lost world is recognized by the main frame replacing the document (`Page.frameNavigated`) and by evaluation reporting that the context is gone.
- In the element tree of the screen-reading script (`app/src/main/assets/web/page.js`), password fields, and text input fields and textareas whose autocomplete marks them as bank card number, security code, expiry or one-time code, only report how many characters were filled, without the value; text in such textareas also does not enter the body of page reads and summaries. Verification after typing still reads and compares the value inside the App as before, and is not handed to the model.
- Tab pool: at most 4 per task and at most 8 globally (`app/cap/web/WebTabPool.kt`, `TabBudget.kt`). Links that would jump to an external App are not followed.
- Downloads land in `downloads/` in the current conversation's workspace and are not opened or installed; uploads submit only files from the workspace (`WebDownloads.kt`, `UploadStaging.kt`).
- Read-only scripts: `web_evaluate_js` runs in the page's isolated environment; the page object is a read-only wrapper, writing, tapping and navigating are all blocked, and it cannot access the network either (fetch, XMLHttpRequest, sendBeacon, WebSocket, EventSource, new Image() and the like report blocked_network, and any request seen from it on DevTools also ends the run with an error; for data behind the page use `web_capture` to read the responses the page itself received); secret input fields such as password fields read out as `***` (the same for same-origin iframes, shadow roots and templates), while on their own interfaces only page-defined attributes such as type and name are read, innerHTML and the like, and the textContent of outer elements containing secret textareas, are masked on a copy that belongs to no window (without constructing the page's custom elements), and child text nodes of secret textareas and value attribute nodes of secret inputs also read out as `***`, even after being obtained and detached from the page; selecting elements by value attribute reports invalid_selector, selectors are first restored according to CSS rules (escapes, comments, namespaces) before judging, anything with `url(` is blocked, and `web_script`'s `mika.query` uses the same set (`core/cap/web/ReadScript.kt`, `SelectorPolicy.kt`). Site commands (scripts in website knowledge with a `/* mika-command` header, `core/learn/knowledge/SiteCommands.kt`) also run read-only through `web_run_site_command`; currently there are no site commands in the built-in knowledge, so this tool does not appear. Scripts run by `web_script` cannot reach the page and can only operate through Claire's action entry.
- Login state: the site registry `SiteRegistry` records visited sites, whether they have been signed in to, and whether they have been blocked by verification; Settings → Website sign-in can open sign-in, sign out of one site, or sign out of all (`app/cap/web/WebLogin.kt`). Web takeover moves the same WebView to a full-screen page on the main screen (`app/web/WebTakeoverActivity.kt`), keeping forms and the sign-in flow as they are.
- The user's own browser (sidebar "Browser", UI in `app/browser/ui/BrowserScreen.kt`, `BrowserStart.kt`, `BrowserPageParts.kt`, tabs in `app/browser/UserBrowser.kt`): a visible `WebTab` that shares the same web configuration (cookies, web storage) as the Agent's tabs, so signing in here also means being signed in when Claire does tasks. Normally it does not enter any task's tab pool, does not count against the tab limit, and does not go through the web takeover session (`WebHandover`); task tabs and takeover proceed as usual; when the user asks Claire on a page it is lent to that task (see the next item). **Identity** (`BrowserIdentity`): browsing on one's own defaults to the mobile version; switching a website to desktop in "More" is recorded in `UserSiteModes` (`browser_site_modes.json`, subdomains follow the parent domain), and desktop is used next time too, stored separately from the Agent's (`SiteModes`); opening a site that is not signed in yet from Frequent sites is "sign in for Claire": the whole tab follows the same version as when Claire does tasks (`WebDevtools.agentWantsMobile`, desktop by default), so the cookies left by signing in are the same version of the site Claire sees when doing tasks, a banner at the top "Sign in for Claire · …" carries "Signed in" (it takes a look with `LoginProbe`: if it sees that it is not signed in it prompts, and if it sees that it is signed in or cannot tell, it trusts the user, records it as signed in and returns to the start page), and it also takes its own look when the page finishes loading, and hides the banner when it sees that sign-in succeeded. The rule is installed only on this tab (`WebDevtools.setModePolicy`, one per tab, null meaning follow `SiteModes`), and the Agent tab's identity is unchanged. This tab is viewed and tapped by the user personally (`WebDevtools.setHandOperated`): in desktop mode the UA, UA-CH and identity patches are as usual, but no viewport emulation is added (Agent tabs use it to fit the whole page into the view and fix the zoom), leaving it to the WebView's own wide viewport and overview mode, so when opened the whole page fits the screen and can be pinch-zoomed (`DesktopProfile.viewportCall`); when switching versions it changes the UA, wide viewport and emulation via `beforeLoad` and reopens the current page, and if the user taps through to another website midway, `onPageStarted` switches it according to the rules and reloads. **Start page**: the top bar has only back and "Browser"; a large search box (with a line below "Search with Bing", which can be tapped to switch among built-in engines, or to add and delete custom engines), and while typing, suggestions appear below (`StartPageModel.suggestions`: the first is to search this phrase, and if it looks like a URL, to open it; the rest are recent openings whose title or URL matches); the "Frequent sites" row has four cells (`BrowserFavorites.combine`): the user's own additions first, then sites from the site registry, one cell per site (registrable domain), opening the shortest host name, with a small green dot if any host name of this site is signed in (and a line below "Signed in; Claire can also use it directly when doing tasks"), and the last cell "+" (Add) hints to add in "More"; tapping "Organize" or long-pressing enters organizing, where icons shake slightly (no shake when animations are off), × removes, and "Done" exits. Frequent sites record only the user's changes (`browser_favorites.json`: their own additions and removals); removal only hides it, and the registry, sign-in state and cookies are untouched (signing out is done on the settings page's "Website sign-in"). "Bookmarks" are specific pages the user saved (`PageBookmarks`, `browser_bookmarks.json`): added or removed in a page's "More", and the start page lists the latest 5, with "All" opening the bookmarks panel, where titles can be edited and entries deleted while organizing. "Recently opened" is the latest 10 pages listed on the start page (`RecentPages`, history keeps at most 500 entries, ignoring `#` and keeping only the latest for the same URL), with "All" to the right of the title opening the history panel (delete one entry or clear all while organizing); a search is written as "Search: <term>", a web page shows the site icon, title and site name, and on the right is a relative time ("10 minutes ago", "today 11:20", "yesterday", "October 21"), with "Clear" to the right of the title. Site names and icons (`SiteLooks`): when a page finishes loading it reads the site name declared by the page (og:site_name, application-name), and if there is none takes a segment from the title (the beginning for a home page, the end for other pages), and only replaces it when the source is more reliable; the icon is the favicon the WebView receives (`WebTab.Listener.onIcon`), scaled to at most 128 pixels and stored as PNG; both are recorded on the host name, and also recorded on the registrable domain when it has none (or a worse one), so other subdomains of the same site are recognized too. When neither exists, the name is the registrable domain without its suffix (shop.example.com → example), and the square is a background color chosen deterministically by host name plus the first character. **Web page**: at the top are close (back to the start page), the address bar (normally the lock and site name, the search phrase on a search page, with a small "Desktop" tag in desktop mode; tapping expands it to the full URL for editing, and "Cancel" collapses it), refresh / stop and a 2dp progress bar; below are back / forward / start page / share (the system share sheet, URL plus title) / "More". The "More" panel: site (icon, name, host name), refresh, copy link, open in another browser, add to Frequent sites / remove from Frequent sites, switch to desktop / switch back to mobile (this site does the same next time). The system back key first goes back within the page, then closes a popup if one is open, otherwise closes the current tab; when there are no tabs it returns to the start page, and pressing again on the start page (exiting organizing or typing first) goes back to where it came from. **Multiple tabs**: `openTabs` saves open ordinary tabs, and the tab panel can switch, close and create (a new tab shows the start page first); popups are still a temporary layer and do not count as tabs; switching away from a tab lent to a task hands it to the task to keep using. Tabs, history and clear browsing data can all be opened from the start page's top bar and the page's "More"; the page's "More" also has find in page (using `findAllAsync/findNext`, showing which match out of how many). Clear browsing data can separately clear history, cookies and site data (which signs out of all sites, including the one Claire uses) and cache. Video full screen (`onShowCustomView`) uses a full-screen dialog covering the whole screen and hides system bars, restoring on exit. Input in the address bar and search box is judged by `AddressInput`: anything with `://` is opened as-is, localhost and IPs get http://, things that look like domains get https://, and the rest use the currently selected search engine (built-in `SearchEngine`: Bing, Google, DuckDuckGo, Baidu; a custom `CustomSearchEngine` can also be added, with %s in the URL template standing for the search phrase; the choice and custom engines are recorded in `browser_search_engines.json`); before opening it goes through `WebUrlPolicy.checkNavigable`, and a short message is shown for ones that cannot be opened. After a page finishes loading it pauses a moment and checks sign-in state with `LoginProbe`: for sites in the registry it records or removes according to what it sees, and for ones not in it, it records only when it sees that sign-in succeeded. Page dialogs (alert / confirm / prompt / leave confirmation) are shown here for the user to answer; a new window (opened by the user, only one layer) is displayed over the current page while the current page stays: a popup sign-in such as "Sign in with a third-party account" must hand the result back to the page that opened it after signing in; when the popup closes itself it returns to the current page; links that jump to an App are not followed and a short message is shown; downloads go to the system downloader (`DownloadManager`, files stored in the system "Downloads" directory, with the tab's UA and cookies), and "Downloads" in the start page top bar and in "More" opens the downloads panel, asking the system for the list, where entries can be cancelled, opened, shared or deleted (links generated by the page itself such as `blob:` and `data:` cannot be downloaded by the system downloader, and a short message is shown); when a page wants to select a file, or use the camera, microphone or location, the user's own tab pops up the system picker or asks the user (granting the corresponding Android permission to the page on agreement), while the Agent's tabs refuse as before. The web view lives in the process and is kept across rotation, and is closed when leaving this page.
- **Asking Claire on the browser page** (`app/browser/ui/BrowserPageAsk.kt`'s UI and `PageChat`, `app/browser/PageLoans.kt`, `PageConversations.kt`, `PageAskModel.kt`): on a web page (not the start page) there is an extra bar at the bottom "Ask Claire, or have it do something on this page…" (PageAskBar); when text on the page is selected, the text selection menu gets an extra item "Ask Claire" (`PageFrame` wraps the web engine's selection menu callback, and on tap first reads out the selected text and then collapses the menu), and the selected text is shown above the input field and carried with this message.
  - **How this sentence is written**: the very front is a paragraph of English explaining which page it is (`core/agent/AttachedPage.kt`: `[Web page I'm viewing in Claire's browser. It is attached to this task as your current web tab.]`, Title, URL, with each line of selected text starting with "| ", and `[End of page]`), followed by a blank line and then the user's own words. It is stored into the conversation as-is and handed to the task, and this is what the model sees; only tasks started from the browser page carry it (an interjection carrying newly selected text is also written as the same paragraph). The conversation page draws "Web page · Site" and the selected text above the bubble (SentPage), while the title, list, notification and the titling model do not carry this paragraph (`ChatCopy.plainTitle`, `ScreenCopy.plain`, `ConversationTitler`), and neither do the language hint, the answer's language check and memory quotes (`WebCapability.userWords`). A task carrying it expands the web group at the start (`WebCapability.preload`); the web capability description has a "The user's page" paragraph: "this page" is the borrowed tab, read and acted on directly without opening its URL again; the user is watching it, so change it only as needed; opening another web page opens a new tab.
  - **Which conversation to continue** (`PageConversations.pick`): for the same page (same URL, ignoring `#` and a trailing `/`) it continues the most recent one asked on it, no matter how long ago; otherwise for the same site (same host name) it continues the most recent one asked within 2 hours (clicking through is still the same matter); when there is neither it opens a new conversation. The line above the input bar "Continue … · New conversation" can switch to a new conversation. Which page a conversation started from is recorded in the `.page` file next to the conversation file (`ConversationStore.pageOrigin`, JSON: url, host, title; old conversations do not have it and it is deleted with the conversation), and asking again on the same page replaces it with the latest page; in the sidebar such a conversation has a small "Web page" tag after its title, with the site on the line below (`DrawerConversation.site`).
  - **Borrowing a tab** (`PageLoans`, main thread only): when asked, it first "promises to lend" to that conversation (`UserBrowser.lend`), and the task takes it when it starts (`UserBrowser.claim` in `TaskRun.execute`, only for tasks started from the UI; it can be borrowed only if the page is still open at that time), with only one lent at a time. Once borrowed it builds the web set on the spot (`LazyWebPort.prepare`) and lends this tab into the task's tab pool (`WebStack.borrow` → `WebTabPool.attachExternal`): it becomes the task's current tab, and web tools read it and operate on it like tabs in the pool; but it is not attached to the task's web display (it is in the browser page), does not count against the per-task and global limits, is not closed to make room or by memory reclamation, and the pool does not subscribe to its listeners (downloads, popups and login probing still belong to the browser page). Opening a URL in the task (`web_navigate`) does not take away the user's page but opens a new tab (`WebScreen.navigate`); when the user's page opens or closes a popup window, the borrowed one switches to the one visible (`PageLoans.swapped` → `WebStack.swapBorrowed`). At the end of the task it is returned (`PageLoans.end`, before the web set is closed), and the page stays before the user's eyes; the pool closing it (closing `web_tabs`, task end) also only returns it (event `returned`) without destroying it. When the user closes the browser page (including leaving it via "Open in chat"), the page is handed to the task to keep using (`PageLoans.closing` → `WebStack.adopt` → `WebTabPool.adopt`): the same WebView becomes an ordinary tab of the task, attached to the web display, without losing scroll, form or single-page-app state, and closed with other tabs when the task ends; only when the task does not take it (already crashed) does the browser page close it. Browser page tabs are numbered differently (`browser1`, `browser2`...), and a page and its popup can be lent to the same task one after another.
  - **How a borrowed tab differs from the Agent's own tabs**: it is viewed by the user personally (`setHandOperated`, and in desktop mode no viewport emulation is added either), page coordinates are computed by its real size in the UI (`WebScreen.viewSize`), and DevTools clicks are converted by the visual viewport; if the user has pinch-zoomed, the page is zoomed back out to the whole page before reading the screen (`WebScreen.unzoom`: when the visual viewport is narrower than the layout viewport or has moved away, `zoomOut`, at most 12 times), so that the numbered coordinates line up with the screen. Screenshots are not taken from the web display; the view is drawn directly into an image (`WebScreen.viewFrame`, one-to-one with the view's pixels, without the conversation panel overlaid on top). During a dwell it does not lightly scroll it (`idleScroll`). Picture-in-picture and the capsule do not show the web frame at this moment (`WebPreview` counts it as under takeover). When handing it to the user (human verification, sign-in) it is not moved to the takeover UI: it is already in front of the user, so it only locks and waits for the user to tap "Handled" on the browser page's panel (`WebStack.showForTakeover`, `returnFromTakeover`).
  - **Paused as soon as the user touches it**: the web view's container (`PageFrame.dispatchTouchEvent`) only accepts real touches delivered through the window; Claire's own input does not go through it (DevTools mouse and keyboard go straight into the page, and `WebInput`'s native touches are sent straight to the web view), so it does not treat its own actions as the user operating. Finger down → `UserBrowser.userTouched` → the task borrowing it (`TaskRun.userOnPage`): when it is working right now, the borrowed page is the current tab, and it is not operating on the phone screen, `TaskGate.onUserOnPage` records it and shows paused on the spot (`publish` shows the running one as `Held(paused, you are operating; Claire is paused)`), and before the next tool that would change the screen it stops at the gate (whichever kind of screen); the user taps "Continue" (`TaskService.resume`) and it is lifted on the spot, and if the loop has not yet reached the gate it shows again according to its current state. Touching the page in other states such as waiting for the user's answer or under takeover does not pause.
  - **UI** (PageAskOverlay, pure logic in `PageAskModel`): when the user asks, the lower half of the web page covers the upper half with a conversation panel (PagePanel, 58% of the web area's height), and the web page exposed above is exactly the page Claire is operating; while Claire is working, a pale purple glow ring surrounds the page (PageGlow, not blocking touch). At the top of the panel are the site name, "Start from this page · page title", "Open in chat" (the same entry as tapping the notification, `TaskService.openAppIntent`) and collapse; in the middle are this conversation's exchanges (the same set as the floating capsule's conversation card: laid out by `CapsuleThreads`, drawn by ThreadList, and the task's appearance is derived by `ChatViewModel.reduce` and `CapsuleModel.cardRunOf`); while working, below are the steps (`Steps`), each step prefixed with "Web" or "App" (whether that step's activity object is "site …" or a package name); the buttons that need you to tap (answer options, agree, I'll handle it, continue) are the same set as the capsule card (`Pinned`); at the very bottom is the input field: while running it is an interjection (the same path as the conversation page, `TaskService.start` with this sentence's number), with the stop key on the right; when Claire asks you it is an answer; after it ends it is a follow-up question on this page. When the page is in your hands (under takeover) it is "Handled". When the panel is collapsed and that matter has not ended, the bottom is a small card (PageCard), tapping it expands; when you touch the page and it pauses, the panel collapses into this card (writing "Paused" and "Continue"), with a bar at the top "You are operating; Claire is paused · Continue" (PausedBanner); when Claire newly needs you to reply (answer, agree, take over, do something by hand) the panel opens by itself. The task is a complete Claire task: other web pages open in the task's own background tabs, other Apps are operated on Claire's background virtual screen, and the user stays in the browser; each time it is an ordinary conversation in the chat list, and the result goes into the conversation and sends a notification like any other task. Closing the browser page does not stop the task. Element highlighting is not done (only the glow ring).
- **Keeping a page after the task ends** (`web_keep_open`, `core/cap/web/KeepOpen.kt`; `app/cap/web/PageKeeper.kt`, pure logic in `KeptPageSlot.kt`): by default the task closes all of this task's tabs and its web display when it ends (`WebStack.close`). When the user wants a page to keep running after the answer (keep playing, stay hanging and do not close), the model calls `web_keep_open` on that page (with a reason; `keep=false` undoes it), which at this moment only records in `WebScreen.keepMark` which tab it is; the web capability description states "It closes when the task ends; mark first if you want to keep it, and say truthfully in the answer whether it was kept or closed". A borrowed (the user's own) page never closes with the task anyway, and reports `users_page`.
  - **Handing over**: at wrap-up `WebTabPool.closeKeeping` first takes that page out of the pool (`release`: not destroying it, not detaching it from the display, and not putting another tab on top to replace it), then closes the task's other tabs; that page, together with the private display it hangs on (`WebHost`), is handed to the single `PageKeeper` in the process (`WebOwner.pageKeeper`, given by `MikaApp`), and the display is not closed—the page still hangs on the display it used during the task, visible as during the task, with timers and playback running as usual, without any other tricks. After that it is in no task's tab pool, and other tasks cannot list or close it; it counts toward the global tab limit (a member of `TabBudget`, but no slot is freed by closing it).
  - **One page, two hours**: at most one page globally, and keeping a new one closes the old one (reason replaced); after `KEEP_OPEN_HOURS` (2 hours) it closes automatically (checked once a minute by wall clock, and closed even if the time has passed when the system wakes from deep sleep); it also closes when the system asks to free memory (`TRIM_MEMORY_RUNNING_CRITICAL` and above), when the render process crashes or is reclaimed by the system (`WebTab.reclaimed`), or when the user closes it. When closing, cookies are written to disk, the tab is destroyed, the display is released, and the reason is recorded in the turn of the source conversation (`Turn.keptPageClosed`, `KeptPageClose`); which site was kept is recorded when that turn is written into the conversation (`Turn.keptPage`, `TaskService.recordTurn` through `PageKeeper.recordFor`, and it can still be filled in if it closes earlier than the conversation is written). It does not start a new turn and does not ask the model. The next turn's context states whether that page is still open or already closed (`Conversation.context`; one recorded as kept but which exited midway is counted as closed).
  - **Process**: while a page is kept the task service does not exit, and when no task is running the foreground notification is replaced by its persistent notification ("Background web page running" · site · countdown to automatic close, with buttons "Close web page" "Open conversation", progress channel); when a task is running it is another ordinary persistent notification. When the page is closed and there is no task, it leaves the foreground and stops the service; when the service is destroyed it truthfully closes that page (Claire exited).
  - **UI**: on the conversation page, below the turn that kept the page, a green line "This page keeps running in the background · site · how long until it closes automatically · close", and when closed it changes to a gray line "Background web page closed (reason)"; when no task is running the top bar says "Keeping 1 web page in the background" (`app/chat/ui/KeptPageRow.kt`, `KeptPageCopy.kt`). Floating capsule: when there is no task and the result has also finished showing, it expands to "Background web page running" (green dot); when the switch is "Never show" it still does not show, relying only on the notification. Tapping it opens its card (`CardView.Kept`): the live frame (the latest frame of its display, the same source as picture-in-picture), how long is left, which conversation it came from, "Open conversation" "Close web page".
- Apps on the virtual screen when the task ends: the virtual screen on the phone side (`AgentDisplay`) is released with `ResourceScope` at wrap-up (`AgentDisplay.release` → the built-in assistant's `releaseDisplay` → `VirtualDisplay.release`); this screen is created with the "destroy content on removal" flag, so the App windows on it end together with the screen (whether the process stays is up to the system), and muting is restored as it was. Keeping a page only covers web pages, not Apps on this screen.
- **Pacing page by page** (`core/cap/web/PagePacing.kt` pure logic, `PageDwell.kt` wired to the task): besides each action's own mouse and keyboard pacing, changing pages consecutively on the same website (by registrable domain) does not open instantly either. A page counts as arrived when the address changes while reading the screen or opening a page (not counting the part after #), or when the page itself receives another batch of JSON or text responses while scrolling down (loading more); the first page of this website in the last 10 minutes does not dwell, from the second page on it dwells 4–7 seconds, and after that each additional page raises the lower and upper bounds by 0.6 / 1 second each, up to 12 seconds.
