It asks the model once (preferring a lightweight model; role `prompts/roles/app_knowledge.md`, with the request format in `app_knowledge_input.md`; an already-verified supplement is not asked when the last run succeeded smoothly), giving it the App's built-in description, the supplement in use, the outcome and a condensed action list (with error codes and whether the screen changed, without screen text or typed characters), and asks for the full text after the supplement is revised: only fact lines starting with "- ", at most 12; if there is nothing worth adding, answer NONE and output nothing. The goal, the answer or the reason for not succeeding is given as-is in the request; the returned supplement is accepted as-is (the data is only on the phone, with no redaction), and only a whole one that writes things like passwords or verification codes is not accepted (`SensitiveFilter`). Each App has at most one pending revision, and it is not accepted if the one it revises against is not the one currently injected (another one was accepted first); after the one in use changes (accepted, archived, trial changed phase), a pending revision drafted against the other one is archived on the spot, and accepting a stale revision only archives it, without keeping it around to block this App's future drafts; after being accepted it goes into trial, and during trial it replaces the one it revises (the old one is neither injected nor deleted), and only when it goes formal is the old one archived, and if archived it switches back to the old one (`AppKnowledgeTrial.inUse`). When injected it is attached after the App's description, and a trial one is marked as not yet used by a successful task (the `knowledge.learned_trial` in `feedback/loop.md`; a verified one is `knowledge.learned`). A run that uses a trial draft and succeeds moves it to formal; one learned from a lesson is archived only after two consecutive bad results, while others are archived after one bad result (`AppKnowledgeTrial`); like personal skills, runs stopped by a model error, a runtime-environment problem or an operation guard do not count as good or bad (`SkillOutcomes.resultOf`). "Pending confirmation" states whether it is a supplement or a revision, and lists the lines added and removed; the one temporarily replaced by a trial revision is marked "Temporarily replaced by a trial revision", and is not offered for archiving.

## 12. Memory and models

- **Thinking effort**: models whose provider states the tiers in the model list (`supported_reasoning_levels` / `capabilities.reasoning.effort` / DeepSeek's `effort.supported_levels`) can select it (when the OpenAI-compatible protocol's list does not write `none` but the preset's official documentation does, `none` is added so thinking cannot be left on), and each of the four protocols has its own way of sending it: `ResponsesModel` sends `reasoning: {effort: ...}`; `OpenAiCompatModel` sends `reasoning_effort`; `AnthropicModel` sends `thinking: {type: adaptive, display: summarized}` and `output_config: {effort: ...}` (when `none` is selected it sends `thinking: {type: disabled}`); `GeminiModel` sends `generationConfig.thinkingConfig` (`thinkingLevel`, using `thinkingBudget: 0` when `none` is selected, and `includeThoughts: true` when on). `ModelCatalog` keeps the tiers and their order, cached with `ListedModel.reasoningEfforts`; missing and an explicitly empty list are distinguished. The Anthropic list fills item by item according to `capabilities.effort.<level>.supported` and `capabilities.thinking.types.<mode>.supported`: when `adaptive` is accepted the tiers are those marked supported (shallow to deep), and `disabled` is also accepted with `none` in front; when `adaptive` is not accepted (only `enabled` thinking by budget) or tiers are not distinguished, it is an empty list; accepted thinking modes are stored separately in `ListedModel.thinkingTypes` and handed to `AnthropicModel` through `ClientSetup.thinkingTypes`, and an unaccepted one is not sent. The Gemini list has only one `thinking` boolean and gives no tiers. `ReasoningOptions` prefers the list; when nothing is reported only the Responses family (including ChatGPT and xAI sign-in) gets general manual tiers, while compatible interfaces, Messages and generateContent do not guess (each model's accepted tiers and notation differ, and a wrong guess is rejected on every request). Which protocol is actually used is decided in one place, `ProviderStore.adapterFor` (subscription sign-in always uses that provider's interface and does not follow another protocol written in the list), shared by building the model and giving tiers. `ReasoningControl` selects it under the use card, and `RoleChoices.reasoningEfforts` saves it per use in `uses.<role>.reasoningEffort`, so the same model can differ by use; "Use the main model" inherits the main model's effort, and switching that use's model clears its old choice. By default it is unspecified, and old configurations do not migrate to a new default; `ProviderStore.modelFor` passes it to the adapter through `ClientSetup`, and it is sent only when explicitly chosen. With thinking on, Claude's output limit is raised to 16K (thinking also counts in `max_tokens`); in turns with tool calls the thinking block's signature is stored together with the address it was received from in `Part.Thinking.opaque` (`SignedThinking`), and only returned to the same address (`redacted_thinking` is returned the same way), and when the address changes or thinking is turned off it is not returned. When the server rejects the chosen tier it reports an error as usual instead of quietly lowering it; when retrying without the unsupported `include` it still keeps the effort. After refreshing the list, a selected tier that is no longer supported is kept with a hint, and the default can be restored.

- **Memory** (`core/learn/memory/`): three `MemoryKind` — fact, preference, procedure. Facts and preferences are recorded and used immediately and can be undone; procedures need user approval. It records only from what the user says themselves (`UserUtterances`; when there are screen descriptions, browser page descriptions, quoted Claire replies or attachments, it only takes the characters the user typed, and also takes quoted words the user said himself, `UserWords.said`), not from screen and web page content; passwords, verification codes, card numbers, ID numbers and the like are blocked by `SensitiveFilter`. A write not accepted by these rules is a reply to that item, and the result marks `counts_as_failure=false` and does not count toward consecutive operation failures. When the switch "Let Claire summarize and remember on its own" (off by default) is on, and the user personally asked it to remember this turn (recognizing the wording for asking it to remember in each UI language, see `core/general/MemoryRequests.kt`), and what was quoted is the half-sentence asking it to remember, then content with specific values the user did not say is also accepted, with the source recorded as `inferred`: the opening injection, `memory_search` results and the memory page all state that it was not said by the user, and when handed to verification it counts as a fact found by a tool, not a fact given by the user. Tools `memory_save`, `memory_search`, `memory_edit`; a saved quote only accepts this turn's user words, with the sole exception that the previous turn the user explicitly confirmed the assistant suggestion or question in the turn right before it, and the content had already been discussed when this turn asked to save it; updates and deletions are not relaxed to the previous turn, and must use this turn's explicit request and a stable id from the latest `memory_search` result; when there are multiple candidates, this turn's quote must also contain that exact id, and when it was not searched, the id is not among the candidates, the id is duplicated or the target is unclear, it does not change anything, and the change goes into the existing result card and can be undone. After the task ends, `MemoryExtractor` extracts from the user's words in the background (runs that read web pages do not extract; it also requires the run to be started from the conversation UI and to have delivered an answer, `RunLearning`). Memory injected at the start (`MemoryContext`): if the total memory is within `FULL_MAX_TOKENS` all of it is given without omission (pinned first, the rest in reverse update-time order), and only when it exceeds that does it fall back to pinned plus retrieved relevant entries (BM25 in `MemoryIndex`); in full mode procedures are all given too, and in retrieval mode procedures are included by relevance up to `MAX_PROCEDURES`; the product path (`AgentLoop`) uses automatic mode, and `Mode.Relevant` keeps the old behavior. There is also a separate user profile (`ProfileStore`, tool `profile_edit`, changes take effect from the next task). Memory and profile are encrypted at rest with a key from the Android Keystore (`app/memory/EncryptedMemoryFile.kt`). The switches "Use memory" and "Automatically remember what I say" are on by default, and "Let Claire summarize and remember on its own" is off by default.
- **Models**: four interfaces — OpenAI-compatible (Chat Completions), OpenAI Responses, Anthropic, Gemini (`core/model/`); you can also sign in with a ChatGPT account and use the plan allowance (see "Responses and signing in with ChatGPT" below). Models are divided into three uses `ModelRole`: main model, vision model, lightweight model. Speech-to-text is a fourth use, but it is not in `ModelRole`, and is stored separately in the unified voice catalog (see "16. Voice input"). What is chosen for each use is what the user explicitly says (`RoleChoices`, in `app/settings/ProviderStore.kt`), with no default assignment inferred from capabilities: the main model is a specific model or not chosen yet; vision is a specific model, "use the main model" or "none" (none by default on a fresh install); lightweight is a specific model or "use the main model" (the default on a fresh install). A choice is not blocked by capability flags (`ProviderStore.choose` does not refuse), and choosing a specific model records the corresponding flag: choosing it as the main model records tool calling, choosing it as the vision model records vision, and at runtime these two flags decide whether to send tool definitions and whether to attach images; when vision is "use the main model", `configFor` gives the main model's configuration and takes the vision flag as true (the user said it can see images), while the main model doing its own work still follows its stored flag. Capability labels are for reference only. Outcome judgment, memory extraction, intent judgment and route matching prefer the lightweight model; when the speed switch "Give simple steps to the lightweight model" is on, simple steps go to it too (`core/agent/ModelRouter.kt`, and phone steps are not attached when the lightweight model does not support tool calling). "Vision grounding verified" (`Capabilities.groundingVerified`) is changed in Settings › Developer options and applies to the configuration of the current vision model (`ProviderStore.setVisionGroundingVerified`), and is not shown when there is no vision model. The API Key is stored encrypted with the Keystore (`app/settings/KeyCipher.kt`).
- **Provider presets** (`app/settings/ProviderPresets.kt`): prefill the interface type, address and initial capability values; the user fills in the model name and Key. Domestic: DeepSeek, Qwen, Kimi, Zhipu, SiliconFlow, Volcano Ark (Doubao; the model name can be a model ID or an endpoint `ep-…`), MiniMax (domestic site `api.minimax.cn`), StepFun, Tencent Hunyuan (TokenHub `tokenhub.tencentmaas.com`), Baidu Qianfan (`qianfan.baidubce.com/v2`); international: OpenRouter, OpenCode Go, Anthropic, Gemini, OpenAI, xAI, Mistral, Groq, Cloudflare Workers AI; self-hosted: Ollama, LM Studio; and finally "Custom". The interface type of "OpenAI" is Responses (`ProviderConfig.Kind.OpenAiResponses`): the official documentation states that the new main models must use Responses for tool calls, and Chat Completions with tools is rejected; listing models is also `GET /models`. The rest are OpenAI-compatible. The Cloudflare Workers AI address contains the `{account_id}` placeholder (`https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/{account_id}/ai/v1`), and `ProviderPreset.urlEditable` is true to let the user fill the account ID into the address, with a hint to edit it when unreplaced. When adding a "Custom" provider the interface type can be OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic, Gemini or OpenAI Responses (`ProviderConfig.Kind.withApiKey`, Responses last), and one that chooses Responses calls `{base}/responses` through `ResponsesModel`, with model listing still `GET {base}/models`; a custom Responses, like Anthropic and Gemini, requires a Key (among custom providers only OpenAI-compatible can leave it empty, mostly local services, see `AddForm.keyRequired`). For `ProviderPreset.selfHosted` (Ollama, LM Studio) the address prefills the machine's default port (`http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1`, `http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1`), and the form can change it to the computer's LAN address (`AddForm.urlEditable`, with a line below hinting how to change it), the Key can be left empty (`AddForm.keyRequired`), and the stored provider name still uses the preset name. The README's preset list is generated from this file by `./gradlew updateReadme` (see `ReadmeSections`).
- **Listing models and connecting providers**: `ModelCatalog` (`core/model/ModelCatalog.kt`) calls each vendor's public model-listing interface (OpenAI-compatible `GET /models`, Anthropic `GET /v1/models`, Gemini `GET /v1beta/models`, the latter two paginated), and returns model names and the capabilities the interface gives (tool calling, vision, context length, reasoning); what the interface does not give is empty, and `ModelOptions` (`app/settings/ModelOptions.kt`) carries over the preset's initial values and marks them unconfirmed. Failures are classified by reason (invalid Key, network, no listing provided, etc.). A listing interface returning 404 / 405 / 501 is recorded as "no listing provided", but by itself it cannot tell whether this vendor really provides no listing or the address or interface type was filled in wrong, which the trial call below distinguishes.
- **One provider, several protocols**: some providers (such as subscription-based aggregators) have models under one Key that speak different protocols, and each model in the list carries an `api` table whose keys are protocols (`chat-completions`, `messages`, `generate-content`, `responses`…) and whose values contain that protocol's `base_url` (a compatible interface may also write `compatibility.reasoning_field`). `ModelCatalog` takes the first one it can speak in the order Chat Completions, Messages, generateContent, Responses, and records it as `ListedModel.endpoint` (`ModelEndpoint`: protocol, address, reasoning field); one that can speak none of them (such as `realtime`) records the protocol name (`unsupportedApis`), `ListedModel.usable` is false, `ModelOptions.merge` does not list it and the provider's model count does not include it, so the user cannot choose a model that is bound to fail. Capabilities in the same form are recognized too: `capabilities.tools.supported`, `capabilities.reasoning.supported`, `limit.context`, `modalities.input` (with `image/…` means it can see images). `endpoint` is stored with the model list in `providers.json` under `services[].models[].api` (`unsupportedApis` in the same place). When building a model, `ProviderStore.modelFor` looks up this model's `endpoint` in the list the provider remembered, and if there is one it selects the adapter by protocol (`ProviderConfig.Kind.of`: Chat Completions → `OpenAiCompatModel`, Messages → `AnthropicModel`, generateContent → `GeminiModel`, Responses → `ResponsesModel`) and uses its address, and if there is none it follows the provider's interface type and address as before; after re-listing or an address change, the next model build uses the new one. The adapter attaches its own version path (Anthropic attaches `/v1/messages`, Gemini attaches `/v1beta/models/…`), and a trailing `/v1` or `/v1beta` already in the listing address is removed by `ModelEndpoint.adapterBaseUrl`, so it is not duplicated. The Key is always the provider's one. Reasoning echo on compatible interfaces (`OpenAiCompatModel`, judged not by provider name, address or model name): each vendor puts reasoning in `reasoning_content`, `reasoning`, or `content` is a block array with reasoning in `thinking` blocks (text blocks are the answer); received reasoning records the address and position it was received from (`ReasoningVia`, stored in `Part.Thinking.opaque`), and in requests with tools it is put back in place as it was (the same field name; the vendor with blocks puts the whole thing back as blocks, with the thinking block first). It only echoes back what was received from the same address; reasoning given by another address or another adapter is not sent, and providers that do not recognize these fields never receive it; side calls without tools do not echo. When the list states `reasoning_field` (`reasoning_content` or `reasoning`), reasoning of unknown origin (history stored by older versions) is echoed according to it too. The preset "OpenCode Go" is like this: the listing address is `https://opencode.ai/zen/go/v1` (the listing needs no Key), and the OpenAI-compatible interface type is only the fallback when no protocol is written; models that write only `responses` (address like `https://opencode.ai/inference/go/openai/v1`) are called by `ResponsesModel`.
- **Responses and signing in with ChatGPT**: `ResponsesModel` (`core/model/ResponsesModel.kt`) calls `POST {base}/responses`, always `stream:true` and `store:false`, and puts the whole context into the `input` array each time: the system prompt goes in `instructions`, system messages in the conversation become developer messages, images are `input_image` (replaced with a placeholder sentence when the model cannot see images), tools are function tools (`strict:false`), and tool calls and results are `function_call` / `function_call_output` items. By default it sends only `model`, `instructions`, `input`, `tools`, `store`, `stream` and `include`; when the user chooses a reasoning effort it sends `reasoning.effort` as well, and temperature and output limit are not sent (not accepted when using the ChatGPT plan allowance). For reasoning models the request asks for encrypted thinking content (`include: reasoning.encrypted_content`), stored as-is in the first tool call of this turn's `Part.ToolCall.opaque`, and put back in front of the tool call on replay (removing the `id` that only means something on the server); when the server says it does not accept `include`, it is removed and sent again. Success counts only when `response.completed` is received (or `response.incomplete`: `max_output_tokens` records output truncated), `response.failed` / `error` is mapped to `ModelError` by `error.code`, and a stream that disconnects before wrap-up is treated as a network failure (and retried). The token is given by `BearerToken`: an API Key is fixed; a renewable one (ChatGPT) on HTTP 401 first gets a new one and sends again. Usage is recorded into Token usage from `response.usage` (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `input_tokens_details.cached_tokens`).
"Sign in with ChatGPT" (`core/model/chatgpt/`, interface type `ProviderConfig.Kind.ChatGpt`) follows the path OpenAI provides for open-source, locally running Apps: a public client with no secret, authorization code + PKCE (S256), with a new state, nonce and verifier generated on each sign-in. `LoopbackCallback` (`core/model/oauth/`, shared with OpenRouter) opens a system-chosen free port on `127.0.0.1` and accepts only one `GET /auth/callback` (other paths return 404, a mismatched state returns 400 and keeps waiting); when the callback arrives it first finishes the sign-in — checking the callback, exchanging the token, verifying the ID token, checking the plan entitlement, at most 20 seconds — and then returns the result to the browser (the browser keeps waiting on that connection): on success it writes "Signed in; back to Claire", on failure it writes the same reason as the UI, and the button on the page is an intent link to open Claire; on timeout or cancellation it closes. The authorization page `https://auth.openai.com/api/accounts/authorize` is opened in the system browser: the first time it registers with `client_id=dynamic_agent_client`, carrying `agent_name_hint=Claire` and the local identifier `ext_agent_host_id` (`urn:uuid:…`, generated once per device); the callback gives the client_id specific to this account, recorded before exchanging the token (`settings/chatgpt-host.json`, stored with the local identifier, neither being a secret), and later token exchanges, refreshes and sign-ins all use it (a new sign-in carries the previous ID token and email as hints, and after signing out carries no ID token). `scope` is `openid profile email offline_access resource.invoke chatgpt.tokens.use.direct`, and `resource` is `https://api.openai.com/v1`. After exchanging the token (`/api/accounts/oauth/token`, form) it verifies the ID token: verifies the RS256 signature against `/.well-known/jwks.json`, and checks the issuer, audience (= client_id), expiry and nonce; if the granted permissions do not include `chatgpt.tokens.use.direct` it does not count as signed in (`signInWithChatGpt` strings these together). Rejection reasons are classified by `ChatGptAuth.failureOf`, the UI and the callback page say the same plain sentence, and the error code is recorded in diagnostics: a non-JSON 403 / 429 is blocked by the protection (`SignInFailure.Blocked`, change network or try later), `unsupported_country` / `region` / `territory` in the code or description is an unsupported region (`SignInFailure.Region`), `invalid_grant` is the sign-in code expired or already used (`SignInFailure.CodeExpired`), `access_denied` and the like is the user not consenting (`SignInFailure.Denied`), and only when the code or description mentions plan or permission (`invalid_scope`, `plan`, `subscription`, `scope`) or the granted permissions lack the plan item does it mention Plus or Pro (`SignInFailure.NoPlanScope`), otherwise it is "ChatGPT refused this sign-in (error: code)" (`SignInFailure.Exchange`); the `error` brought back by the authorization page is classified the same way, with region and plan ones that cannot be told counting as `SignInFailure.Denied`. During sign-in Claire is in the background while the user enters the password and two-step verification in the browser, and Android may freeze or reclaim the background process, leaving the local port open with nobody accepting, so the browser waits until timeout and the authorization code may expire: therefore the sign-in does not run in the UI's scope but in the App's single `ChatGptSignIns` (`app/settings/ChatGptSignIns.kt`, which survives UI recreation and going to the background, with only one at a time), and when it starts it starts the foreground service `ChatGptSignInService` (`specialUse`, on the quiet "Task progress" channel: "Waiting for you to sign in to ChatGPT in the browser", with "Cancel"), and if it cannot start it only records one line and the sign-in continues as usual; the service watches `ChatGptSignIns.running` and stops itself as soon as the sign-in ends (success, failure, cancellation, timeout). The most recent sign-in failure (time, category, error code, a short redacted description) is kept only in memory and written into "Copy diagnostics". The whole sign-in (`ChatGptSession`: client_id, identity, token, expiry, granted permissions) is treated as this provider's "Key" and stored encrypted with the same `KeyCipher` in `provider-keys.json`. The access token expires in an hour: `ChatGptTokens` exchanges the refresh token for a new set when it is near expiry (less than 5 minutes) or rejected, and the new refresh token and access token are stored together, with only one exchange at a time; if the refresh token can no longer be used (`invalid_grant` etc.) it clears the tokens and reports "Sign-in expired", and on a transient network or server error the tokens are kept. Signing out first revokes the refresh token on the server (`/api/accounts/oauth/revoke`), and if that cannot be confirmed it still clears the local tokens and explains; the provider, model and use are all kept, and signing in again continues to work. Deleting the ChatGPT provider forgets the client_id (registering again next time, and the account can be changed), while the local identifier is kept. The model list is `GET https://api.openai.com/v1/models` (access token), and the returned `models` table keeps only those whose `visibility` is `list`, in server order, with the name taken from `slug` and the display name from `display_name`, and `input_modalities`, `context_window`, `supported_reasoning_levels` filling in capabilities when given; all are called through `ResponsesModel` (`chatGptPlan`). Plan allowance errors: `subscription_sharing_usage_limit_exceeded` (or HTTP 429) is the allowance reaching its limit (`ModelError.ChatGptLimitReached`), `subscription_sharing_user_not_eligible` (or HTTP 403) is the account not being allowed to use it this way (`ChatGptNotEligible`), neither is retried, and the task outcome explains the reason and where to see usage (chatgpt.com/settings/usage); `subscription_sharing_usage_unavailable` and other temporarily unavailable ones are retried later as 503. UI: in the "Sign in with an account" group at the very top of adding a provider, the ChatGPT row, and the panel it opens has "Use your ChatGPT plan" and "Continue with ChatGPT" buttons (stating Plus and Pro work and Claire charges nothing extra), and after the first successful sign-in it explains once that usage goes through the ChatGPT plan; the provider details show the account (email), manage usage (opens chatgpt.com/settings/usage), sign out or sign in again, with no interface type, address or Key.
- **Signing in with an account (xAI, OpenRouter)** (`core/model/oauth/`): side by side with ChatGPT, in the "Sign in with an account · no Key needed" group at the very top of "Add provider" (`settings/ui/models/AccountSignIn.kt`: three rows, ChatGPT, xAI / Grok, OpenRouter; signed-in ones say "Signed in" and open details, unsigned ones open an explanation panel and only start when "Continue sign-in" is tapped; xAI has a risk notice and "Use an API Key instead"). Sign-ins all run in `ChatGptSignIns` (`SignInTarget` identifies which provider it is, and the foreground notification uses those two sentences for account sign-in), and on success it lists models, stores them, selects one if there is no main model yet, and opens that provider's details (`ModelSetupUi.signedInProvider`); the details (`ModelsScreen.kt`) are the account, state, set as main model, open the other party's account page, sign out or sign in again, and signing out only clears the local side. Failure categories and error codes are recorded in diagnostics. The local callback `LoopbackCallback` and `Pkce` are in `core/model/oauth/`, shared by ChatGPT and OpenRouter; `SignInBrowser` opens the browser, returns to Claire, and gives the result page after the callback.
  **xAI** (interface type `ProviderConfig.Kind.Xai`): sign-in, encrypted storage, refresh and calling xAI's own interface are four separate steps. It uses the device code (RFC 8628, `DeviceCodeClient`, `awaitDeviceGrant`): the user opens the verification page in the browser and checks the short code, and the App polls for the token at the interval the server gives (`slow_down` adds 5 seconds, and only 3 consecutive connection failures count as a failure); the panel shows the same short code, the expiry and "Copy code and reopen the confirmation page"; when the verification page's host is not on the allowlist it opens the fixed official page instead. Access tokens, refresh tokens and device codes do not go into logs. The whole sign-in (`AccountSession`) is stored encrypted in `provider-keys.json` the same as an API Key; `AccountTokens` exchanges the refresh token for a new one when it is near expiry or rejected, and if the refresh token can no longer be used it clears it and reports "xAI sign-in expired" (`ModelError.SignInExpired.account`). `XaiAuth` first reads `https://auth.x.ai/.well-known/openid-configuration`, and the issuer and the device-code and token addresses must be https `auth.x.ai` (`OauthUrls`), otherwise it stops. The client reuses the public sign-in entry of xAI's official command-line tool (not one registered for Claire), with scope including `grok-cli:access` and `api:access`; the panel states the reuse risk before signing in. Calls go to `https://api.x.ai/v1` Responses (`ResponsesModel`, `accountLogin`), and HTTP 403 means the subscription tier cannot use this interface (`ModelError.AccountPlanNotAllowed`, not retried, explaining to use an xAI API Key instead).
  **OpenRouter** (`OpenRouterAuth`, `signInWithOpenRouter`): official OAuth PKCE. The authorization page `https://openrouter.ai/auth` carries the local callback (`http://localhost:<port>/auth/callback`, the listener bound only to 127.0.0.1), S256 verification, `key_label=Claire` and state; a mismatched callback state returns 400 and keeps waiting, and no code and no state means the user refused; `POST /api/v1/auth/keys` (JSON: code, code_verifier, code_challenge_method) exchanges for an OpenRouter Key. It is stored as an OpenAI-compatible provider according to the OpenRouter preset, the Key is encrypted as usual, and `Provider.signIn` records `openrouter` (stored in `providers.json` under `services[].signIn`); the one from authorization and one where the user filled in a Key are each a separate provider (`findOrAddProvider` separates them by origin), authorization does not replace the user's Key, and the UI writes it as "OpenRouter (account sign-in)". Signing out deletes the local Key (`ProviderStore.signOutOpenRouter`), with a hint to delete it on OpenRouter's Keys page. See `docs/dev/account-signin.md` (which also explains why Claude subscription, Google account and MiniMax account are not done).
- **Request headers and session ids** (`core/model/ClientHeaders.kt`): every request sent to a model service (the four protocol adapters Chat Completions, Messages, generateContent, Responses, one-shot and streaming, and `ModelCatalog` model listing) carries the headers given by `ClientHeaders.of`, and the header for the Key (or ChatGPT's access token) is still added by the adapter itself; "Sign in with ChatGPT" also carries `User-Agent`, and since it declares no session header it sends no session id. `User-Agent` is `Claire/<version>` (the App passes `BuildConfig.VERSION_NAME` to `ProviderStore`, and core does not write the version). Some providers require each conversation to carry a fixed session id (to route by it and reuse prompt caches, and reject when absent): presets declare the header name with `ProviderPreset.sessionHeader` (currently only "OpenCode Go"'s `x-opencode-session`), and it is recorded on the provider when connecting (`Provider.sessionHeader`, stored in `providers.json` under `services[].sessionHeader`; ones stored by older versions lack this item and get it filled in from the preset whose address matches when read in). Providers that do not declare it send no session id, and the code does not judge by provider name. The session id is `ModelSession.of(conversation id)` (UUID v3, irreversible, not sending the conversation id itself): the three model factories of `TaskEnvironment` take one more `ModelSession` parameter, `TaskRun` builds the main, lightweight and vision models by its own conversation, verification, intent judgment, outcome judgment and the explanation after stopping all use these, and `TaskService` also gets it by the same conversation id when starting the conversation title; within the same conversation every run and every side call has the same session id, and a different conversation has a different one. Requests that do not belong to any conversation (model listing, connection test) use a new one from `ModelSession.fresh()` each time.
- **Plain http** (`res/xml/network_security_config.xml`, referenced by `networkSecurityConfig` in the manifest): model services the user runs on this machine or the LAN mostly only have http, so the whole App allows cleartext (`base-config cleartextTrafficPermitted="true"`), and certificates trust only the system ones. Nowhere else in the code does it reject http: `ModelCatalog` and the adapters only require a valid http(s) address, and the add form only requires it to start with `http://` or `https://`. When adding a provider, a plain http address that is not on this machine or the LAN (`LocalHosts.isLocal`: localhost, 127/8, ::1, 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, 169.254/16, fc00::/7, fe80::/10, `.local`) gets a reminder line below the form (`AddForm.unencryptedWarning`), without blocking it. Because of this the built-in browser can also open http web pages directly: previously the system rejected first (`ERR_CLEARTEXT_NOT_PERMITTED`) and `WebScreen` reopened it as https, and now it opens at the original address, with that https-retry used only when a device policy or WebView still rejects cleartext; cleartext subresources inside an https page are still rejected by `MIXED_CONTENT_NEVER_ALLOW`.
- **Network proxy** (`core/net/NetProxy.kt`, `ProxyAddress.kt`, `LocalHosts.kt`; settings in `app/settings/NetProxyStore.kt`): "Network proxy" in the "Network and connection" group of Settings › Advanced and others (`settings/ui/NetProxySettings.kt`, changed only here), filled as `http://[user:password@]host:port`, and also accepts socks5, socks5h and https (checked by `ProxyAddress.parse`; a typo is not saved), with empty meaning direct. The address may carry an account and password, and like a provider's Key it is stored encrypted via `KeyCipher` (Android Keystore) as `settings/net-proxy.bin`, not into plaintext preferences and not into logs, and the UI shows only the protocol, host and port (`ProxyAddress.toString` also omits the account and password). Earlier it was only for containers and stored in `container-proxy.bin` (a different key): `NetProxyStore` decrypts it on the first read, saves it in the new form and deletes the old file.
  - Who uses it: OkHttp clients built by `NetProxy.shared.client(…)` — `ProviderStore`'s model clients (the four adapters one-shot and streaming, speech transcription, connection test) and the clients for model listing and account sign-in token exchange, `UpdateChecker`, and the built-in browser's downloads (`WebStack`); sandbox commands through environment variables (see §6 sandbox). What does not use it: the remote relay (`RelayGateway`) and run-record export (`OtlpSender`).
  - How it routes: the client installs `NetProxy.selector` (`ProxySelector`), asked fresh for each request: `LocalHosts.bypassesProxy` (the `isLocal` ranges, plus dotless machine names, `.lan`/`.home.arpa`/`.internal`, 100.64/10) connects directly, and the rest go through the HTTP or SOCKS proxy; with SOCKS the domain is resolved by the proxy (socks5 and socks5h alike). An `https://` proxy (TLS handshake with the proxy first) is not supported by OkHttp, `ProxyAddress.forApp` is false, Claire's own connections go direct, and the settings page states it is only for containers. Streaming replies are read segment by segment as before, and the proxy does not change how they are read. When settings change, `NetProxyStore` calls `NetProxy.changed()`: it re-parses and clears the idle connections of each client (in-progress requests still finish as before).
  - Account and password: for an HTTP proxy, `NetProxy.authenticator` (OkHttp's `proxyAuthenticator`) adds `Proxy-Authorization: Basic` when the proxy returns 407 and before building an https tunnel; if it was already sent on the same request and rejected, it does not try again. A SOCKS proxy can only be given it through the single per-process `java.net.Authenticator`: `MikaApp` installs `NetProxy.socksAuthenticator` at startup, which answers only requests whose protocol is SOCKS and whose host and port match the current proxy, and answers nothing else.
  - Errors: when `HttpSupport` fails to connect, a request that went through the proxy is classified by `NetProxy.classify` as `ModelError.Proxy` (`ProxyFailure.Unreachable`: connection refused, proxy host not found, connection timeout, tunnel setup failure, SOCKS handshake error; `ProxyFailure.AuthFailed`: the proxy rejected the account and password), and HTTP 407 also counts as the latter; a disconnect while reading the reply is still `Network` as before. `userReason`, the trial call (`ModelSetupViewModel.proxyReason`) and model listing (`ListFailure.ProxyUnreachable` / `ListFailure.ProxyAuth`) all say it is a proxy problem and do not say the Key or model is wrong; an unreachable one can be retried, a wrong account and password is not retried and does not disable the model.
  - Update download: the system download service (`DownloadManager`) does not go through the App's proxy, so when a proxy is set `ApkUpdater` instead downloads through the proxy in the process via `ProxiedApkDownload` (writing `.part` first and renaming when complete; the process being killed counts as a failure and a retry works), and when installing it gives the system installer a read-only address through `UpdateFilesProvider`; verification is the same as before.
  - Built-in browser (`cap/web/WebProxy.kt`): when WebView supports `PROXY_OVERRIDE`, it sets the same proxy for all WebViews in this process through androidx.webkit's `ProxyController`, and the direct rules are `LocalHosts.BYPASS_RULES` plus simple hostnames; when an HTTP proxy needs an account and password, `WebTab.onReceivedHttpAuthRequest` answers only for the current proxy's host (at most 3 times per main-document load per tab, recounted on the next navigation or refresh). When WebView does not support it, or the proxy is a SOCKS with an account and password (the engine does not support SOCKS authentication), the browser goes direct, and the settings page line says so. With no proxy set, startup does not load WebView for this.
- **Storage of provider and model configuration** (`app/settings/ProviderStore.kt`): `Provider` is a connected provider (id, name, interface type, address, and the most recently listed models), and `ProviderConfig` is a model attached under it (recording `providerId`, with the interface type and address copied from the provider, and the Key taken from the provider when building the model). A model configuration is a layer inside storage that the user does not see: it is created on the spot when a model is chosen (`ProviderStore.use`), and deleted when no use uses it any more (tidied on every use change, provider deletion and read-in; Keys are stored per provider, and deleting a configuration does not touch the Key). In `providers.json`, `services` stores providers (including the remembered model list), `providers` stores model configurations, and `uses` stores the choices of the three uses (`{"use":"model","config":<config id>}`, `{"use":"main"}` or `{"use":"none"}`); the Key is stored encrypted by `KeyCipher` in `provider-keys.json`, one per provider. Older versions had only model configurations and stored Keys by configuration id: on read-in, they are grouped into providers by `ProviderConnection.keyOf` (interface type, address, name), the Key is taken from any one in the group, and the ones stored by configuration id are cleared. The old version's `roles` recorded only explicitly specified configuration ids: on read-in, the assignment actually in effect then is written as `uses` — the main model takes the first configuration when unspecified, vision is "use the main model" when unspecified and the main model can see images, otherwise none, and lightweight is "use the main model" when unspecified (all done once, already-migrated ones are not touched, and repeating it has no side effect). Deleting a provider deletes the configurations and Key under it, and uses that were using it fall back to the default (the main model becomes unchosen, vision none, lightweight uses the main model).
- **Model configuration page** (`app/settings/ui/models/ModelSetupScreen.kt`, state in `ModelSetupViewModel`): the onboarding "Configure models" and Settings › "Models" (`app/settings/ui/models/ModelsScreen.kt` is just the same page without the guide) share the page and state, and the user only manages two blocks: uses (three cards, each stating what is chosen: the model name, "use the main model" and who the main model is, "none" or unset) and providers (one row per provider, stating which uses it works for). During onboarding providers come first and, when there is no provider, the add form is laid out directly on the page, with the bottom "Next" enabled only after a main model is chosen; in Settings uses come first, there is no bottom button, and changes take effect immediately. After adding a provider by choosing a preset or custom and filling in the Key, "Test and save": first list models once, and after listing succeeds (or the service provides no list) try one real call — in the order of `ModelOptions.candidates` (this provider's own models first: model name or display name containing the preset's `ProviderPreset.familyTokens`, such as qwen for Qwen; then ones the list says can call tools; within the provider's same tier, newer versions in the model name come first (`ModelVersion` in `settings/ModelOptions.kt`: version numbers like `x-3.1-pro`, `qwen3`, `claude-4-5`, `4o`; parameter counts and context lengths ending in b / k do not count; within the same version the stable release comes first, the `latest` alias next, and `preview` / `exp` / `beta` etc. after; within the same tier ones without a date come first, then newer dates like `-2025-08-07` and `-0827` first; ones whose version cannot be read come after readable ones; it does not judge which of flash, pro, lite is better, and the same version follows list order; other vendors' models are not sorted by version — provider lists often put old models first, and old models may be offline); the rest follow list order; ones that clearly will not call tools, and ones that look like embedding / rerank / speech / drawing, are not tried — when the list's `type` or output modalities say so it follows them (`ListedModel.chat`), and when not it looks at words in the model name; when there is no candidate at all it tries the first one) — giving the model a no-parameter tool `get_time` and asking it to call it, with a reply limit of 1024 tokens (enough for one tool call plus the thinking of a model that thinks first; Messages and generateContent get it; Chat Completions does not, since vendors disagree on `max_tokens` and `max_completion_tokens` and a wrong one rejects the whole request; Responses does not either, and the ChatGPT plan path does not accept `max_output_tokens`), going through the same model-building path as a real task (`ProviderStore.probe` uses the same `modelFactory`, carrying headers and a session id as usual, with a new session id), waiting at most 20 seconds (signing in with ChatGPT does not go through this form and is not tried: the sign-in itself has been checked); a wrong Key or a missing header the provider requires shows up here, and the server's error is shown as-is and not saved. `ProviderStore.probe` returns `ProbeResult`: a tool call returned (`ToolCalled`) counts as passing; only words returned (`NoToolCall`) means the interface works but this model does not call tools. When it does not pass and it is that model's own problem (only words, or the adapter gives `ModelError.ModelNotAvailable` for the account not having it enabled or having no permission, `ModelNotFound`, `ToolsUnsupported`), it goes on to the next candidate, at most 3 (`ModelSetupViewModel.PROBE_CANDIDATES`): the passing one is saved as the main model, and the page says one line below the provider list which ones were tried first and did not pass (`ModelSetupUi.probeNote`); when none passes it still saves the provider (the Key has been proven right) without setting a main model, says why the first one did not pass and which ones were tried, and below is a "Choose main model", with this provider recorded in `ModelSetupUi.pickToProbe`, and when choosing a main model in it, it first tries a call (`ModelSetupUi.probeBeforePick` → `pickManual`), and when it does not pass it says the reason below that provider and does not choose; testing again or choosing a main model collapses it. If the first one fails for another reason (Key, network, address, timeout, server error) it is not saved; if a later candidate fails for another reason it stops trying and treats it as none passing. The adapter recognizes "this model does not work" with the same `core/model/ModelAccessErrors.kt`: when the error content of HTTP 400 / 403 / 404 contains wording for tools not supported ("does not support tools"…), this account cannot use it ("not activated", "not authorized to access this model", "does not have access to the model", "Model.AccessDenied", "not enabled", "no permission"…, carrying the server's words), or no such model ("model_not_found", "model not exist", "not found for API version"… in 400 / 403, carrying the words in the JSON error), it gives `ToolsUnsupported`, `ModelNotAvailable` and `ModelNotFound` respectively, and a model that is offline ("no longer available", "has been retired", "model … is deprecated", "offline"…) also counts as `ModelNotAvailable`, carrying the words; HTTP 429 counts as `ModelNotAvailable` only when it says this account's allowance on this model is zero ("limit: 0", `"quotaValue": "0"`, "doesn't have a free quota tier") (waiting is useless and only switching models works, so the trial call goes on to the next one), and other 429s are still rate limiting or allowance exhausted; the rest are handled by each adapter by status code, 401 is always a Key problem, and 404 gives `ModelNotFound`, likewise carrying the words in the JSON error (non-JSON ones are not carried). When no models are listed (the listing interface does not exist, or the listing is empty), it does not save directly: the form asks for a model name (`AddForm.noList`, with the input field's example by interface type, `ModelSetupCopy.modelExample`), and testing uses it for a trial call; when it passes it is saved as a provider that does not offer a list with that as the main model, and when it does not pass it is not saved. The form always has "Enter a model name manually" (`AddForm.manual`, expanded automatically when listing models fails and nothing was entered manually): when filled in (`AddForm.typedModel`) only that one is tried, and whether the list can be listed (with or without it), cannot be listed, or another error occurs (wrong Key, 5xx, a non-JSON reply) it is still tried, the trial call's conclusion decides, and when it passes it is saved together with the list that could be listed; ones not in the list are recorded in `Provider.typedModels` (stored with the provider, not lost when refreshing the list, still listed by `ModelOptions.ofProvider`, and marked "manual" by `ModelOption.typed`); when nothing was entered manually and listing models fails, it still states the reason as before (a wrong Key says the Key is wrong) and does not save; when the trial call returns 404 the adapter gives `ModelError.ModelNotFound(model)`, and the form states which model it is and roughly what a model name looks like, and for a custom one also says the address or interface type may be filled in wrong. When the host name of a custom address is the same as a preset (`ProviderPresets.forHost`, except services you run yourself), it reminds you to switch to that preset, and one tap switches with the Key kept; when the address is the preset's but the interface type differs, it says that too. A model name that the trial call got a reply from is recorded in `Provider.verifiedModels` (stored with the provider; old files do not have it, and then services that do not offer a list are not trial-called). Only when all this succeeds is the provider and list saved through `ProviderStore.connect` (updating the original one with the same interface type, address and name), with the name being the preset name; a custom one can be named in the form (`AddForm.name`, at most `ModelSetupViewModel.MAX_NAME` characters (counted by code point, so an emoji is not cut in half), and when left empty `AddForm.defaultName` is used: the host name of the address, the preset name when the address is a preset's, and the input field's hint writes this default name), and the same address with different names is saved as two providers (for example two Keys of the same relay), and when it cannot list it states the reason and does not save. When the first provider is connected and there is no main model yet, one of its models is selected automatically. The use selection page (in `app/settings/ui/models/ModelsScreen.kt`) has "use the main model" and "none" above vision, and "use the main model" above lightweight, and below lists all models grouped by provider (filterable by name when there are many), with tags for reference only, not filtering or graying out, and each provider can have a model name entered manually below (for ones that do not offer a list, or when the wanted one is not in the list); a manually entered one is trial-called first (`ModelSetupViewModel.pickManual`, and the main model also has to be able to call tools), and only when it passes is it selected and recorded as verified (ones not in the list are also recorded as manual), and when it does not pass the reason is stated below that provider; tapping any other one takes effect immediately and returns. For a model that is in use under a provider that does not offer a list but has not been verified (`ModelSetupUi.unverified`), the provider row reminds "did not pass the test", and the details page has "Test again" (`ModelSetupViewModel.retest`). When a model call in a task returns 404, the failure reason states which model does not exist and to check the model name in Settings › Models or switch to the corresponding preset; when the server's JSON error has the original words it does not conclude that the model does not exist, but quotes the original words instead ("Server reply: …"), says the model name may be wrong or the service may be temporarily unavailable, and gives the same checking advice (the quoted original words in the sentence have one trailing period removed, and the same goes for the "server explanation" of the ChatGPT allowance and subscription tier kinds); a model this account cannot use states which one it is and to switch to another in Settings › Models or enable it in the provider's console; one that does not support tools states to switch to one that can call tools (`userReason` in `core/agent/StopRules.kt`; when a lightweight model hits these, the rest of the steps in this task all use the main model). Provider details (the same file) are the connection (state, interface, address, Key can be changed), at most three models (ones in use first, marked with which use they work for) and "View all N models", and delete (before deleting, state clearly which uses need to be chosen again); the "All models" page's search box is fixed at the top, and refresh at the top right lists again. Tapping any model pops up from the bottom "Use as" main model / vision model / lightweight model, going through the same operation as the use selection page. The names, what they do and the explanations of the three uses are written only once in `ModelsCopy`. When opening the use selection page or a provider page, providers whose list has not been remembered yet (for example ones migrated from an older version) are listed once.
- **Token usage** (`core/model/usage/`): accounted at the source, not left to each caller. Models that can be called in the App are only obtained from `ProviderStore.modelFor` (`buildModel` is private to it), and before going out they are all wrapped with metering by `metered` (`MeteredModel.kt`): name and capabilities as before, exceptions thrown as-is, and text segment callbacks as usual; at the end of each call it records one entry — provider id, the provider name at the time, model name, use (`UsagePurpose`, which is the `ModelRole` when the model was obtained: a vision or lightweight call that uses "use the main model" is recorded under vision or lightweight), with usage recording input (including cache hits), output and cache-hit tokens, and ones that report no usage or throw (including cancellation) only recording a count, with no estimation. The main loop, outcome judgment, intent judgment, vision, memory extraction, route matching, skill and App knowledge drafting and other side calls are therefore all counted; model listing is not a conversation call and is not recorded. Anthropic streaming reply usage is taken as cumulative values per the interface documentation: a field in `message_delta` with an integer value (output, and possibly input and cache) overrides the one in `message_start`, and ones not present, null or not an integer carry over; some compatible interfaces have all zeros in `message_start` and the real numbers only in `message_delta`. The ledger `UsageLedger` accumulates by UTC whole hour × provider × model × use (when local midnight falls in the middle of an hour, the part after midnight gets a separate cell, and the daily Token budget uses the local date to count how much was used today; the local time zone is fetched fresh each time, and if the phone changes time zone while the process has not exited it follows), stored only on this machine in `files/usage-v1.json` (JSON like other settings; a key table plus rows as compact arrays): accounting only adds in memory, is thread-safe, and about 2 seconds after a change a background thread writes the whole thing atomically, so a process kill loses at most the last few seconds; only 400 days are kept. The statistics `UsageQuery` is a pure function, with the local time zone converted at statistics time: "24 hours" is the rolling most recent 24 whole hours (including the hour of this moment) split by hour, "7 / 30 / 90 days" is local calendar days including today, and "custom" is local start and end dates (inclusive), split by day, and over 45 days is split by week (every 7 days from the first day, `UsageStep`); a whole-hour bucket falls on the local day of its start time (and correctly on a daylight-saving day). Each cell carries each provider's tokens, for stacking. Each provider has a fixed order (`ProviderUsage.order`: connected ones by connection order, deleted ones after, by the earliest accounting time), and colors are taken from the theme's categorical colors by it (`MikaColors.series` three, the rest gray and grouped as "Other" in the trend chart), not changing when the range changes or a provider is selected; text always uses the ink color. When a provider is deleted its history remains, with the name at accounting time plus "(deleted)". Use is only a storage dimension, and the UI does not split by use or show price. Settings › Models › "Token usage" enters a separate page (`Route.Usage`, `app/settings/ui/UsageScreen.kt`, with no next level): time (24 hours / 7 days / 30 days / 90 days / custom, with the actual range on a line below, and "Change dates" for custom), a total usage card (always all providers: a big number, input / output / cache hits / call count), then three charts — "Usage trend" (bars stacked by provider, with a legend, two dashed grid lines and a right-side scale, tapping a bar shows that cell's total and each provider's usage), "By provider" horizontal bar ranking (usage and share, tapping a provider selects it and tapping again deselects, with others faded when selected and others in the trend chart faded to a quarter), "By model" horizontal bar ranking (the row of capsules on top is the same selection as the provider ranking; when selected only that provider's models are listed, the bars use its provider's color, and the share is computed within this row); the footer states the number of calls that reported no usage and "figures are as returned by the provider".

## 13. Run records

The computer task console views the conversations and runs saved on the phone through the `RemoteHistory` read-only interface. `PhoneHistory` reads `ConversationStore`, `RunStore` and `TraceStore` directly, checking run ownership by conversation, and reads active tasks' state from the task center's own state. `McpSettings.historyEnabled` is off by default, authorized by the phone's "Connect computer → Advanced settings", and applies to USB and private networks. The new interface still goes through `RemoteAccess` first; after the owner authorizes, the model request and reply bodies are read paginated only when the task console explicitly expands them. The control interface still recognizes only tasks registered by `RemoteTaskHub`, and history reading does not expand the scope of task control. The task state carries the actual `"run_id"`, so the front end can merge a remote task with the same history turn; the task console updates recordings in the existing refresh scheduling of visible pages, keeping the selected turn and draft.

Each run writes a record (`app/record/RunStore.kt`): `run.json`, `events.jsonl` and screen screenshots under `files/runs/<time>-<id>/`, keeping the most recent 30. Event types are in `core/run/record/RunEvent.kt` (model replies and usage, tool results, observations, activity, confirmations, takeover, origin, compression, outcome judgement, etc.); before writing, `Redactor` masks input into password fields and key-like strings. In the UI "View process" opens the timeline (`app/task/ui/RunTimelineScreen.kt`): at the top are the goal, criteria, outcome and duration, with details such as model call count tucked into "More details", and below the steps are listed with a vertical line (`components/StepLine.kt`), and tapping a step with a screen shows the screen at that time. While a task is running, the conversation page has "Run process" right below the steps: the id is taken by the conversation the card is in (`TaskCenterState.run(conversation).runId`, from the same source as `endedRunIds` at the end, not the service's external displaying id); the outcome at the top says "In progress", and it re-reads the record every second and the steps grow downward without auto-scrolling to the bottom; it stops when there is an end record or the task is no longer there (`RunTimelineViewModel`'s `alive`, the kind where the process was killed and the record was not finished).

The computer side uses the same persisted record for troubleshooting (`core/remote/RunRecordExport.kt`, `app/task/RemoteRunExport.kt`): `GET /api/v1/tasks/{id}/export` (CLI `mika task export`) exports by task id or run record id, writing `task_id`, `conversation_id`, run_id in the header, with `exportFormat` 1. During export strings go through `Redactor` again. It only recognizes tasks in `RemoteTaskHub`'s receipt index that belong to the current computer access token; the origin field in the run summary and a missing index cannot substitute for ownership verification, and runs started on the phone are not exported. A run in progress, or one whose task was handed over but whose record has not been opened, is marked `partial`. It does not read screenshot files and does not attach an OTLP Trace. When a run record is opened, `TaskRun` records the id on the corresponding receipt, and after a process restart it can still be exported by task id.

**Detailed Trace** is off by default. Debug and performance-test builds can turn it on in "Settings → Developer options → Run records", collecting from the next task, and stopping on turning it off. Each main-loop request (including resends) and top-level tool call of the four model interfaces records one span each. In "View process → Detailed Trace" you can compare the first changed block between adjacent requests of the same model, and unreported cache usage is displayed separately from zero hits.

- "Save this Trace" by default exports an OTLP Protobuf file containing only diagnostic metadata (`.pb`). Only when "Include redacted body" is checked does it save a ZIP containing the same `trace.pb` and redacted bodies named by span ID.
- In "Detailed trace" tap "Send via OTLP/HTTP", fill in the full traces receiving address, and optionally fill in an authorization header. After confirmation it sends only the default `.pb` diagnostic metadata, and the authorization value is used only for that request. A public address must use HTTPS, while this machine, LAN IPs and `.local` names resolving to local addresses can use HTTP. The UI shows the receipt confirmation, the number of rejected spans or an HTTP error, and after a failed send you can still view or save the archive locally.
- When encountering "Partial record", first look at the root span's `mika.trace.partial_reasons`. To locate a truncated block, look at the `mika.block.path` and `mika.truncation.reason` of the `mika.trace.truncated` event on the model span. A single run's body budget is 8 MiB, recording at most 1,024 child spans.

The total capacity of the App-private archive is 100 MiB by default, adjustable in developer options. When space is insufficient it first clears the oldest finished archives, and archives being recorded or exported are kept.

**A summary per run** (`core/run/record/RunMetrics.kt`): `RunMetrics.of(events)` is a pure function and only looks at the events in the run record; all times count from the start event (knowledge loaded before and phone setup are not counted).
- The total duration goes to the outcome event (an unfinished one goes to the last event and the outcome is recorded as unfinished); the outcome also carries the failure category (the outcome event's `kind`, i.e. the lowercase name of `FailKind`) and the outcome judged after handing over the answer. Done = reported success (including unverified ones) and not judged as only partly done or impossible.
- **First feedback**: from the start to the first time the user sees actual progress of this run — the moment the earliest model reply that actually executed arrives (the reply event's `repliedAtMs`; a reply with calls is recorded only after execution, so the arrival moment is recorded separately), at which point the UI shows its progress description, what to do, or the answer; or the earliest activity (the first step done when replay does not go through the model). Lightweight model replies that were re-dispatched do not count; when the answer is shown segment by segment the first segment of text is earlier, has no record, and does not count.
- **Back and forth with the model**: the number of model reply events (requests of the main loop, including re-dispatched lightweight model replies); tokens are the sum of the usage of these replies, and ones that report no usage count separately. Side calls (intent judgment, verification, vision, compression) and failed retries do not emit reply events: the run puts `RunUsageTally` into its own coroutine context, and each request of a model wrapped by `metered` adds one entry along the way, recorded at wrap-up as one `RunEvent.ModelCalls` (all requests, the count of ones that reported no usage, and tokens). The outcome judgment and memory extraction after handing over the answer are outside this context and only go to the Token usage page.
- **Where the time goes**: model thinking is the sum of the request durations of each reply (failed requests before a retry and backoff are recorded separately); screen reading / operation is the execution duration of top-level tool calls (ones the model called, and replayed ones), classified by the kind the single execution entry assigns to the activity (a tool that declares read-only risk counts as reading, the rest as operation), with no new timing points added. The outer call duration of a batch of actions or a page script already includes its sub-steps: reading in a sub-step counts as reading, and the rest as operation, not double-counted; control calls such as waiting, asking the user, asking for confirmation and handing to the user count as neither reading nor operation. Other = the total duration minus these three items: waiting for the user, retry backoff, control calls, verification and the remaining overhead.
- The run record's writer thread computes while writing (`RunMetrics.Builder`, keeping only a few numbers per tool result), and when closed hands the summary to `RunMetricsStore`: `files/run-metrics.jsonl`, one per line, only the most recent 500 are kept, not cleaned up along with the 30-run record; it is added after the outcome is judged. Unreadable lines are skipped, and writing replaces the whole file. There is no extra read or write on the main loop.
- UI: a line at the top of "View process" reads "2 min 13 s total · 14 back-and-forths with the model · 86k Token · first feedback 3.2 s", and tapping it opens the durations and shares of model thinking / screen reading / operation / other, input / output / cache-hit tokens, and step count (`app/task/ui/RunMetricsUi.kt`; tokens are written the same way as on the Token usage page). It is also visible on the conversation page without tapping (`app/chat/ui/ChatTokens.kt`): the line at the end of each turn continues with this turn's tokens ("5 steps done · 32 s · 12k Token", and turns with no steps write only tokens). Tapping the Claire title at the top opens "Conversation usage and context" during or after a run. The panel accumulates the main model and auxiliary calls attributed to this conversation (including title generation, verification and wrap-up learning), and the cache hit rate is weighted by input Tokens with a cache report. The "Current model" section shows the main model this conversation will use for its next task (`chat/CurrentModelAllowance.kt`, going through `ProviderStore.effectiveRoles` the same as task start), with model groups deduplicated by the provider of the enabled members and each provider's balance queried fresh (using `rememberAllowanceQuery` the same as the Token usage page); the bottom "View Token usage" goes to `Route.Usage`. The most recent main model request is estimated by source after compression, trimming and prompt hooks, keeping only category counts, model name, step number and window size. Conversation statistics are saved and deleted with the conversation, and branches start consuming from new. Early conversations have a usage lower bound filled in from the retained run summaries, with unknown cache report coverage and context composition. Developer options › "Run statistics" computes the done rate, median duration and first feedback, average back-and-forth count and tokens, and time destination over the most recent 10 / 30 / 100 runs or all (`RunStats`), and can copy or share the summaries of these runs as JSON.
- "Export usage records" in "View process" saves this run's usage details (JSONL) through the system file picker, and also works for old records still retained. Each `model_reply` keeps the step number and raw Token counts, and multiple replies in the same step are listed one by one. Input already includes cache hits, and a missing count means unknown. `compressed` events keep the time and `foldedSteps` in the original order, and old records have no separate step number for them. `model_calls` is the whole-run total including side calls. Export takes only diagnostic fields, and lines that fail to parse are marked with `unreadable_event` and the original line number.
- Eval: each task's `Observed` carries `RunMetrics`, and `EvalReport`'s summary has one more section "Run summary" (one line for the summary and one per task), and the results directory additionally writes `metrics.json` (`EvalReport.metricsJson`).

## 14. Prompts

The fixed text the model sees is all in `core/src/main/resources/prompts/`, with the layering and rules in its `README.md`: `system/` (identity and general guidelines), `capabilities/` (one paragraph per capability, spliced in only after that group of tools is expanded), `tools/` (each tool's description), `entries/` (short descriptions of entry tools while that group is withheld), `feedback/` (tool results and main-loop feedback), `roles/` (internal roles such as verification, outcome judgement, memory extraction and skill drafting). The system prompt is assembled by `SystemPrompt` (`core/agent/SystemPrompt.kt`) at task start, and reassembled only when a group of tools is expanded (paragraphs in a fixed order); all text has golden-sample tests, and the full picture and size of the four cases—withheld, phone only, web only, all on—are in `prompt-surface/layers/`.

## 15. UI and visibility

- **Style**: colors (light and dark sets, following the system; the accent color can be changed in Settings › Language and appearance, `ThemeAccent`, only replacing `accent` / `accentSoft` / `onAccent`, with the default blue being the original two sets, `palette` in `Colors.kt`; a background image can also be set, see below), font size, dimensions, corner radii and animation durations are all tokens, in `app/ui/theme/` (`MikaTheme` in `Theme.kt`, `Colors.kt`, `Tokens.kt`); line icons are in `app/ui/theme/Icons.kt`; common components such as grouped cards, list rows, switches, buttons, top bars and the glass input bar are in `app/ui/components/`. Pages only take colors and components from here and do not hard-code color values. The glass input bar does background blur from Android 12, and older systems use an opaque background.
- **Background image** (`app/ui/theme/SkinImage.kt`, `SkinAccent.kt`, settings in `settings/SkinSpec.kt`): in Settings › Language and appearance, choose an image from the system photo picker, scale it to the screen size, re-encode it as WebP and store it in `files/skin/` (without the original image's EXIF), and on import also extract the dominant hue from the image. The page background (`app/ui/components/Surfaces.kt`) fills with the image (cropping the extra part, with vertical position adjustable) and then covers it with a mask of the page background color (75%–92%), and cards stay opaque as before; blur is done by shrinking first and then enlarging, which also works on Android 11. The fade below the top bar and the conversation page input bar has to block scrolling content, so `Modifier.pageBackground` draws another background in the same position (`SkinBackdrop`), joining seamlessly with the page background. When "Accent follows the image" is on and the image has a dominant color, `MikaTheme` uses the color produced by `SkinAccent.tone` instead of the accent color chosen in settings. The floating capsule, terminal, file viewer and browser do not get the image.
- **Navigation** (`app/ui/MainActivity.kt`, `app/ui/Navigation.kt`): the conversation page is the root page, and the other pages (settings, models, memory and skills, scheduled tasks, browser, run process, sources, phone operation guide, onboarding welcome and model configuration) are pushed onto the page stack `NavStack`, and both the back key and the back button on the page return to the previous page (entering from the drawer returns to the open drawer, and entering from settings returns to settings). Pages only go in and out through callbacks.
- **Drawer** (`app/chat/ui/ChatDrawer.kt`): the menu at the top left of the conversation page slides out from the left. The top is the logo, Claire and the ready state; the entries are new conversation, scheduled tasks, memory and skills, browser, settings; below are "In progress" (the current conversation and other conversations with running tasks, switchable when running in parallel; the "Background screens ›" on the right of the section title goes to the background screens page `app/chat/ui/ScreensScreen.kt`: one card per running task, stating what it is doing, which App it is currently using (only when the task does something does it re-check the top App through the connected built-in assistant, without polling) and its state, tapping a card returns to its conversation, and "View screen" expands a live screen inside the card (subscribed only while expanded, reusing the capsule's rate limit), and tapping the screen enlarges it to the full screen with pinch zoom; it does not write development things like screen numbers or pixels; the data comes from each task's own stream, so screens not currently shown can be seen too when running in parallel), "Pinned" (pinned conversations, most recently pinned first) and "Recent" conversations (`drawerSections`); each conversation is listed in only one section: a pinned conversation with a running task is listed in In progress (also with a pin), and returns to Pinned when done. When another conversation has a running task the menu button has a small dot. Long-pressing a stored conversation (with a vibration; in accessibility it is "Conversation actions") pops up an in-place menu (`LongPressMenu()` in `app/chat/ui/ConversationMenu.kt`, also used by long-pressing a message on the conversation page): the whole screen dims, and from Android 12 the conversation page and drawer behind it also blur (older systems only dim); that row "lifts" in place into a slightly enlarged white card with a shadow (the same content as the row in the drawer), followed below by a narrower white card aligned to the left with "Pin" (or "Unpin" when pinned), "Rename" and a red "Delete", with text on the left and icons on the right and thin lines between items. When there is no room below (the row is near the bottom, or the keyboard is up) it goes above that row, and when there is no room on either side it moves that row up and attaches the menu below, never exceeding the status bar, navigation bar or keyboard (`placeMenu`). Tapping outside or pressing back dismisses it (playing the appearance animation in reverse); with animations off, appearance and dismissal go straight there without scaling. While the menu is open the content behind is not read by accessibility, the menu has a panel title "Conversation actions" and a "Collapse" action, and each item is focusable and reads out its name. The menu is drawn in the same layer as the conversation page, covering the whole screen, positioned by the coordinates of the long-pressed row in the window. A new conversation not yet saved to the list does not respond to long-press. **Rename** pops up an input field containing the current title (fully selected), saved after trimming both ends, and an empty one cannot be saved; it is written into `.title` (`ConversationStore.rename`, replacing whether or not there was a title). A generated title is written only when there is no title yet (`setTitle`), so a title generated late in the background cannot overwrite the name the user changed; the drawer and the "Continue \"…\"" at the top of the floating capsule card all change to the new name with `ConversationStore.changes`. **Pin** writes a `.pin` (the pin time, `ConversationStore.setPinned`), and unpinning deletes it; the conversation list carries the pin time (`ConversationSummary.pinnedAt`), does not change the conversation's modification time, and returns to its original place in "Recent" after unpinning; a pinned title has a small pin in front. A deleted conversation can no longer be renamed or pinned. **Delete** pops up a confirmation "Delete this conversation?", stating the conversation title; when it has running or queued tasks it states they will be stopped, and when it is bound to scheduled tasks it states those will be kept; on the right a red "Delete", on the left "Cancel" (the same look as confirmations for deleting things elsewhere). When conversations cannot be switched (a task is running and parallelism is off) it cannot be tapped open, but long-press still works (the explanation is written above the first stored conversation). After confirmation the drawer stops listing it on the spot (`ChatViewModel.deleted`), and if the one deleted is the conversation on screen it switches to a new empty conversation and no longer follows its task; the rest is done in the background by `ConversationDeleter` (`app/chat/ConversationDeleter.kt`): scheduled tasks are not deleted (they have their own management page, and the next trigger leaves its result again under the original conversation id), it first stops all its running and queued tasks through `TaskService.cancel` and waits for it to wrap up (at most 15 seconds, and the "stopped" turn saved during wrap-up is also within the deletion), then deletes its workspace (attachments `inbox/`, user-sent screens `screens/`, files written by tasks; deleting by handle without following links, through `SandboxDirs.deleteWorkspace` with the tree lock's exclusive permission, mutually exclusive with commands, imports and resets; when the conversation never had a workspace it skips directly without taking the permission, and taking the exclusive permission has to reclaim the leases of terminals and stdio MCP servers opened by other conversations; this check is outside the permission, writes that are creating a workspace do not wait and what they create stays in "Used space" and is listed as "Deleted conversation"), and finally `ConversationStore.delete` deletes the conversation file, `.title`, `.pin`, half-written temporary files and corrupt files moved aside. Writes that arrive afterwards (a title generated in the background, the outcome and explanation added after wrap-up) do not write it back; if someone saves another turn under this id it counts as starting over and the drawer lists it again. Run records (run process, run statistics) are global and not deleted; files exported to the download directory belong to the user and are not deleted.
- **Conversation draft**: the conversation page's text, attachments to send, quotes and guide/queue choices are kept by `ChatViewModel` per conversation id, and saved and restored when switching conversations. A non-empty draft that has not sent its first sentence is also listed in the drawer, and only opening a draft does not run a task. A successful send clears this input, and it is kept when the send is blocked or the attachment send is not accepted; when an attachment description returns asynchronously it is accepted under the original conversation id and not written into the newly switched conversation. The content URIs of ordinary attachments are kept only in the current process; text is restored with Android saved state. System sharing supports a single file (`ACTION_SEND`) and multiple files (`ACTION_SEND_MULTIPLE`, at most 9 at a time, truncated with a hint above the input field when over the limit, and when some files fail to copy the successful ones are kept and the failures reported one by one), and does not send automatically: on a cold start it enters a new conversation; when the UI is already alive in the background it enters the currently open conversation, and when the current conversation already has an unsent draft it first hangs as `ChatUi.pendingSharedDraft` for the user to choose "Replace draft" or "Cancel" (a share awaiting confirmation is also persisted by `SharedDraftStore`, so the dialog can still be restored after the process is reclaimed).
