Private copies of attachments and share metadata are saved per conversation by `SharedDraftStore`, supporting reading the original single draft and the old single-attachment format; the corresponding copies are cleaned up only when sending, removing an attachment one by one, or deleting the owning conversation.
- **Conversation page** (`app/chat/ui/ChatScreen.kt`, text in `app/chat/ui/ChatCopy.kt`): the top bar is "Claire" and what it is doing right now (which App it is operating / waiting for you to handle), and the top right ⋯ (`ChatMoreMenu.kt`): new conversation, call records (tapping one jumps to the start of that call), things in this conversation (each thing dispatched by the front desk, newest first, tapping opens its run record, or its conversation if there is none), call settings. An empty conversation is a greeting by time of day and a list of examples, and tapping one sends it directly. A running task is a status line (spinner, current progress, elapsed time) plus a vertical step line (a step with a concrete action has a small arrow on the right, and tapping it lists under the line the things done in this step: the UI wording of the action · the object · the result, the same wording as the run process page, `Exchange.stepActions`, `ChatCopy.actionLine`; no tool names or error codes are exposed), with a line of small text below when operating the phone in the background; when it needs your handling (takeover, question, confirmation, pause, lock screen, blocked) the progress stops at the current step (orange dot, "Paused"), and a white card below states clearly what you need to do and the buttons. When done the process collapses into one line "N steps done · duration · View process" (going to the timeline), and the answer is laid out as Markdown on a very light gray background (bottom-left tail corner, symmetric with the user's blue bubble), with a "Sources" capsule below (tapping opens the original text and screen) and file cards. Long-pressing a message (a completed answer, a reply already finished before an interjection, the explanation added after stopping, or the user's own blue bubble; with a vibration, and in accessibility "Message actions") pops up an in-place menu of the same look as the drawer (`LongPressMenu()`, see the drawer item below; the conversation page part is in `app/chat/ui/MessageMenu.kt`): the background dims (and blurs from Android 12), and that message lifts in place into a card with a shadow (the answer is a white card, the user's words still a blue bubble; a very long answer only lifts its first half screen), with "Copy", "Quote" and "Select text" below, and the user's words are right-aligned with the menu right-aligned too. **Copy** puts the whole message's plain text on the clipboard and shows "Copied": the answer drops Markdown symbols (`MarkdownModel.toPlainText`: paragraphs, headings and code keep their text, each list item is one line with "• " or "1. ", quoted lines carry "> ", table cells are separated by tabs, and horizontal rules are dropped), and the user's words drop the attachment, screen and quote lines; the "could not verify" hint above, source capsule, file cards, photo grid and view process do not count. **Select text** switches that message back to `SelectionContainer` (with a hint "Long-press the text to select it"): long-pressing again gives the system's selection handles and copy bar, and a passage can be selected alone; it stays this way until the conversation is switched, and other messages pop the menu as before. A reply still growing and the segment interrupted by an interjection do not pop the menu and cannot be selected. The selection handles are in a separate popup window, and dragging a handle does not bring out the drawer; after a long press, further drag-selection movement is consumed by the selection, and neither the drawer's horizontal swipe nor the list's vertical scroll follows; a horizontal swipe or vertical scroll without a long press works as before. Links in answers open as before. **Quote** puts that message's plain text into a small bar above the input field (a blue line on the left, at most two lines, and a × "Remove quote" on the right, `ChatUi.quote`), sent with the next sentence, and the bar disappears after sending; it is saved with that conversation's draft when switching, and restored when switching back; a quote alone with no words is not sent (it can be with an attachment). The quote is written at the very front of the user's sentence (`core/agent/QuoteMessage.kt`): the first line states whose words are quoted, `> [Quoting your earlier reply]` (Claire's answer) or `> [Quoting my earlier message]` (the user's own words), then each line starting with "> " is the quoted content (at most 1000 characters, with the rest truncated and "…" added), then a blank line, then what is said this time, and attachment lines are still at the end. This sentence is stored as-is in `Turn.userText` (an interjection is stored in `Turn.steers`) and handed to the task, with no new field: a new task, an interjection (while a task is running) and answering a question are all the same, and the model directly sees what is quoted, and the earlier turns' context carries it too; old conversations' sentences do not have that line and are shown as-is. The conversation page draws the quoted message as a small pale block above the bubble (at most two lines), and the conversation title, drawer and notification do not carry the quote when naming this sentence (`ChatCopy.plainTitle`, `ScreenCopy.plain`). The floating capsule's card does not do these: it is in an accessibility overlay window, does not take focus normally, and selection handles and the system copy bar do not appear or are unreliable there; the sentence at the top of it that names the task likewise carries no quote. The input bar is a glass capsule, and the user can still type while a task is running, as an interjection (explained by the gray text in the input field). The turns on screen follow what `ConversationStore` has stored: whichever conversation it wrote to is announced with that conversation id in `ConversationStore.changes`, no matter who wrote it (here, the floating capsule, scheduled tasks); when `ChatViewModel` receives the one currently on screen it re-reads, merges by run id with the turns it followed here (using this copy, with steps and elapsed time, without duplication), and the question-answer it is currently following stays at the end; when the drawer is open it also re-reads the conversation list with it. When the conversation page becomes visible again (returning to the foreground, coming back from another page, `ChatViewModel.refresh`) it catches up once more, and attaches tasks started elsewhere in this conversation that it was not following here (using the task's own original words, and interjections made elsewhere are attached too); without waiting to return to the foreground, a task started elsewhere in the conversation on screen is attached as soon as it has a state, and while running there are those words and steps. The service first registers the task into the task list (`publishRuns`) and then shows it (`TaskRun.show`), so when the UI receives the state the original words are already available; if they are not yet available when attaching, they are filled in as soon as the task list updates. When the parallelism switch is off the UI still identifies by the conversation id carried in the state, and tasks of other conversations are not attached to the conversation on screen; when the service switches to showing a task that just started in another conversation, the task being followed here is made to be shown again by the service.
- **Replies of a conversation taken over by the front desk** (`app/chat/ui/DeskExchange.kt`, `DeskTray.kt`, `DeskNeedCard.kt`, `ChatTimeMark.kt`): several replies below one sentence (what the front desk promised, the result when done, old-style answers) are all the same light gray block (`colors.answer` + `MikaShapes.AnswerBubble`), full Markdown, short ones hugging the text width and long ones filling the width, 8dp apart; replies do not write "Claire · duration · Token", there are no buttons below, and the long-press menu is as usual. When two adjacent turns are more than 5 minutes apart, a time line goes between them (today "14:02", yesterday "yesterday 21:30", earlier with a date; not drawn when right next to the call start/end line). Before the front desk has spoken it is a "thinking" block with three bouncing dots. A successful result is written as one line of report, not paraphrased through the front desk model and not truncated; a failure is explained by the front desk with the reason and next step. Things being done do not put cards into the conversation: a glass progress bar above the input field (`DeskTray`), collapsed into one line (status dot, name, the current step, elapsed time, and how many more `+N`), and expanded into one section per thing (name, stage, the original words handed to the background, the vertical step line and each step's duration, with "Stop" and "View detailed process ›" at the bottom); each thing that needs you to choose, answer, take over or confirm gets a question card at the end of the conversation (`NeedCard()`), and tapping an option answers that thing directly; for takeover, agreeing once, or asking you to do something first, the card draws the same buttons as the chat page according to that thing's current state (`ChatReduce.needStatus`) (`NeedStatusCard()`), acting only on that thing through `deskNeedOps` without jumping to its conversation; when the state cannot be read for a moment it only offers "Stop this thing". The process the user sees is only the run record page "View detailed process", and the sub-conversation that thing runs in is not exposed. When the conversation has no task of its own, the small screen follows the things dispatched by the front desk: among the ones being done that have a screen it takes the one that needs your handling, then the most recently moved one, and has the service show it; when more than one is being done, the bottom left of the small window says which one. Enlarging and closing are remembered separately per thing. When the page is not there (`DeskNotifier`) starting, progress and needing your handling do not write lines into the conversation, only send notifications; a success writes the original text, and a failure writes one sentence of reason. The call page does not put ⋯, and call records and call settings are entered from here.
- **The user sees only Claire**: front desk and background are an internal division that the user cannot see and should not hear. The words the model can read (`front_desk.md`, `front_desk_phone.md`, `LlmBrain.BACKGROUND_PREFIX`, the descriptions of `DeskTools`, the front-desk turns in `Conversation.context`) only say Claire, and the prompt requires Claire to speak in the first person about what it is doing, without mentioning front desk, background, dispatch, task board or task id; `OneMikaWordingTest` enforces this. Every time history is re-seeded each turn (`LlmBrain.seed`) the latest Claire turn carries the full text (at most `DESK_SEED_LATEST_MAX_CHARS`), so when the user follows up on a long result just written into the conversation it can answer; earlier ones are truncated per `DESK_RESULT_MAX_CHARS`. Real-time voice tool results are limited by the protocol frame length and are still given per `DESK_RESULT_MAX_CHARS`.
- **Conversation branching** (stored as `branch` / `branchBefore` in `app/chat/ConversationStore.kt`, workspace copying in `app/chat/ConversationBrancher.kt`, judgment in `app/chat/ui/ChatBranch.kt`): long-pressing an answer that has ended (success, failure and stopped all count) adds an item "Branch from here" to the menu; long-pressing that turn's user words adds an item "Ask again from here". Whether the two are shown and whether they do anything after tapping are the same judgment (`canBranchAt` / `canRetryAt`, given to the menu through `ChatViewModel.branchTurn` / `retryTurn`): this turn has ended, the conversation has been saved, no task is running or queued, it is not being deleted, and the input field is not busy; asking again also requires that sentence to be non-empty. **Branch from here** copies the turns of the source conversation up to and including this turn into a new conversation; **Ask again from here** copies only the turns before this turn (there are no earlier turns before the first turn, and a prefilled non-empty draft can be opened from the drawer and only counts as the first turn once the first sentence is sent), prefills the original words into the input field after removing web page descriptions, quotes, attachment lines and screen lines, and does not send automatically; when this sentence or earlier turns' sentences carry attachment lines, the files in the source conversation's `inbox/` are copied one by one into the new conversation's `inbox/` (this sentence's attachments first, then the ones written in earlier turns, then the rest, all subject to the same limits below; `AndroidBranchWorkspace.copyInboxForRetry`), each copied and each counted as success or failure, without going through a temporary directory; ones over the total limit (200 MB), whose source file is gone, or that fail to copy are skipped. Attachments of this sentence that were copied are put back into the input field; ones written in this sentence and earlier turns that were not copied are listed with a hint, and the rest are not hinted when not copied. Before sending it checks once more that the files put back are still readable (`checkInboxAttachments`), and if one is not readable this send does not happen and these attachments are listed as not accepted. When none of them carry attachment lines, the workspace is copied whole as in the branching below. Screens are not carried along, and the user is reminded to add them again. The new conversation's title is the source title (or the first sentence when there is none) plus the localized suffix " · Branch", and when the total length is over the limit the source title is truncated and the suffix kept, truncated by code point so half an emoji is not cut (`ChatCopy.branchTitle`); the web page source `.page` is copied as-is. Which conversation's which turn it branched from and whether this turn is included are recorded in a `.branch` file next to the conversation file (`ConversationStore.branchOrigin`, JSON: parent, turn, after, at; written to a temporary file first and then renamed; deleted with the conversation), and such a conversation has a small branch icon before its title in the drawer (`DrawerConversation.branch`). When the source file cannot be read, the turn is out of range, or the target already has a conversation file, nothing is written and it does not switch. When branching from here (and when asking again with no attachment lines in this sentence or earlier ones), after the turns are copied it copies the source conversation workspace's `inbox/` (user attachments) with the tree lock's shared permission: only ordinary files are taken, symbolic links are not followed, the normalized path must still be under its own inbox, and non-ordinary files such as pipes are skipped; it first copies into a temporary directory in the target workspace and renames it to `inbox/` only when everything succeeds; when over the limit (200 MB) it aborts and deletes the whole temporary directory, leaving nothing half-copied, and the UI says "Not all attachments were copied"; an existing `inbox/` in the target is not overwritten. Generated audio, images and video (`out/audio/`, `out/images/`, `out/video/`) are copied by the same rules: four directories including `inbox/` are each copied, each with its own limit, and when the source conversation does not have one of them (for example it only generated images and never received attachments) or one fails to copy, that one is not counted and the rest are copied as usual; when one directory is over the limit the UI says "Not all attachments were copied" (`AndroidBranchWorkspace.copyInbox`). A workspace copy that fails does not make the branch fail, and the turns are still branched.
- **Conversation page attachments** (`app/chat/ui/ChatAttachments.kt`, `AttachSources.kt`, pure logic in `AttachmentMessage.kt`, attachment line format in `core/agent/AttachmentLines.kt`): the "+" on the left of the input bar opens a panel below the input bar, occupying the keyboard's area at the keyboard's height (using a default height when the keyboard has not been seen), opening the panel dismisses the keyboard, and tapping "+" again (turned into ×) or tapping the input field collapses it and brings the keyboard back, with the input bar not jumping up and down when the two swap; back collapses the panel first. On the panel's top row are camera (takes a photo with the system camera, with the photo first written into a `camera/` cache and no camera permission needed), album (system photo picker, no permission needed) and files (system file picker), and below are four columns of "Recent photos" (newest in the media library first, thumbnails read in the background at cell size). Photo-reading permission is asked once when the panel is first opened (reading photos from Android 13, partial photos allowed from 14, and external storage on 12 and earlier); when not granted, that area is a sentence of explanation and "Allow photo access" (going to App info when it was chosen and never ask again), and when only partial access was granted it lists the granted ones with a change-range option beside them. Selected photos carry a sequence number, photos and files together are at most 9, hung in a row above the input bar (photo thumbnails and small file cards, removed with ×); with words or attachments it can send, and it can send attachments only. Only when sending are they copied into the conversation workspace's `/workspace/inbox/` (`Attachments.importInto`, each at most 200 MB as before), and at the end of the message one line each "Attachment: <path> (<size>)" is handed to the task — what the model gets is still these lines of paths, and images are still handed to vision by the sandbox's file-reading tool as before; attachments need the sandbox, and when the device does not support it there is no "+". The conversation page draws the user's message as photo cells from these lines (one large one keeping its ratio, two side by side, three and up in three columns) and file cards (a type badge colored by extension, the name and the size), with the written words in a separate bubble and the path lines not shown; messages in old conversations are drawn the same way. Tapping a photo or card copies one from the workspace (through the file gate) into the `viewing/` cache and hands it to the file viewer page (images can be zoomed), and types that cannot be viewed are handed to another App; these two cache directories are given content:// addresses by `AttachFilesProvider`, not exported externally and temporarily authorized when used, and the workspace itself is not opened. The conversation title and drawer name a message that only sent attachments as "Photo", "3 photos", "Contract.pdf" and the like (`ChatCopy.plainTitle`). The floating capsule's card is unchanged.
- **Camera declaration and refresh rate** (`AndroidManifest.xml`): photo attachments go through the system camera, and the manifest declares camera use but not as required (`required="false"`), so devices without a camera still install normally. Some systems classify apps by APK traits, and without this declaration Claire may be treated as a game and have its refresh rate limited, so this declaration must stay, and `ManifestProvidersTest` enforces it.
- **File library** (`app/chat/FileLibrary.kt`, `app/files/ui/FileLibraryScreen.kt`): the drawer shows all conversations by default and the conversation top bar filters the current conversation by default; it builds an index from attachments of saved messages and exported files, without traversing the workspace. It filters by file name, conversation and source, and can preview, share and add to a specified conversation's draft; it supports selecting multiple and adding them all at once to the same conversation's pending bar, truncated to the limit of 9 by the remaining slots, reporting failures one by one when copying; multiple selection can also be shared to other Apps at once, with workspace files copied to the viewing cache and exported files using the original MediaStore URI directly. Read failures and unavailable files are each hinted. Workspace files are read through the file gate of the source conversation, and the file library is not a backup.
- **File viewer page** (`app/ui/viewer/FileViewerActivity.kt`, UI in `FileViewerScreen.kt`): tapping a file card in a conversation opens it here, in a separate Activity, and it can also be opened elsewhere with `FileViewer.open`. The type looks first at the MIME the content provider gives, and when that cannot be recognized it looks at the extension (`ViewerKinds`, `app/ui/viewer/ViewerKind.kt`): web pages use a newly created WebView (scripts on, zoomable, not going through the web capability's browser); Markdown uses the same parser and block styles as in the conversation (`MarkdownText`), lazily loading by block so a large file is not fully laid out at once; PDF uses the system `PdfRenderer` to draw page by page at screen width, drawing only visible pages, with pinch zoom and the page number at the bottom; images fill proportionally and can be zoomed and dragged; text is shown monospaced and can be selected. Text and Markdown read at most the first 1 MB, and when over it a line at the top says "The file is large; only the first part is shown". The top bar of text and Markdown also has "Find" and "Copy all": the find bar searches as you type and shows which match / total matches (when there are too many hits it counts only the first 1000 and shows "1000+"), and can jump to the previous and next match, with matches colored and the current one darker; copy puts the whole text read so far on the clipboard, with a hint sentence when it is too long to fit. The top right is "Share" and "Open with another app"; when it cannot be read it says a sentence and offers "Open with another app". Other types do not enter this page and are handed directly to the system chooser.
- **Small screen** (`app/chat/ui/AgentPip.kt`): a draggable floating small window on the conversation page, normally parked at the bottom right above the input bar, showing in real time the virtual screen or web page Claire is operating (view only, no operation); a thin green edge means working, and an orange breathing glow means it needs you. It is an in-app overlay, not system picture-in-picture. The web screen comes from the current tab attached to the web display; when reading a web page only in a temporary background tab (`web_read_page` with an address) the web display has no tab, `TaskRun` counts it as not being on the web by `WebPreview` (`webOpen` is false and no screen is put up), so the small window does not appear for it, and the floating capsule also shows the conversation card, showing the page as usual as soon as one is opened visibly. On the phone side, a created virtual screen does not mean there is a screen: tasks that only use system commands and event tools have an empty screen, so `TaskRun` sets `appShown` (`TaskEnvironment.agentAppShown`) only when the target App is first opened on the screen, and the small window appears by `TaskEnvironment.previewOpen` (an App has been opened or the built-in browser is in use), so the whole task is not a black block. After expanding, the top left has "Do not show the small screen for this task" (`ChatViewModel.closePip`, `PipUi.closed`): until this task ends there is only a small "Show screen" button at the bottom right, and tapping it opens it again (`reopenPip`); it is cleared when switching to the next task or attaching to another task. When it is closed, starting takeover does not expand it automatically either; when it needs your handling the small button becomes an orange breathing "Waiting for you", and tapping it scrolls to the card that needs your handling. There is no settings switch; to see all screens go to the drawer's background screens page.
- **Floating capsule** (`app/capsule/`): when the user is in another App, a Claire icon floats at the top right of the screen below the status bar, saying the most important thing in the background at this moment; normally it is just an icon (about 40dp), and only expands into a one-line capsule when there is something to tell you. The overlay is drawn through the accessibility service (`app/cap/phone/a11y/OverlayHost.kt`: an accessibility overlay window, needing no "display over other apps" permission; it gives Compose a host with lifecycle, state saving and ViewModel outside an Activity; normally it does not take focus, and can switch to receiving the keyboard when typing is needed; when the service disconnects `ScreenAccessibility` tears down the windows it added too; when it cannot be added it returns empty without reporting an error). What is shown is decided by the pure logic `CapsuleModel`: when the switch is off, the accessibility service is absent or the overlay cannot be drawn, Claire's own UI is in the foreground (`AppForeground` counts visible Activities), or the screen is off or locked (`DeviceStateWatcher`), it is not shown; otherwise by priority it says only one thing — you are handling it (the App has been moved to the main screen, a bar at the top center "You handle it · Don't do it · Handled", the two buttons being cancel the task and hand back), needs you to take over / agree / answer, waiting for a condition, working, done / failed / stopped. States on the icon: while working an arc of blue circles around it (a still full circle when animations are off), a gray circle while waiting for a condition, the whole icon turns orange and the outer ring breathes when it needs your handling, the number of things is written in a corner when there are several, a green check when done, a gray badge when failed or stopped, and nothing when idle ("always show" with no task); it does not expand when the step changes while working. It expands into a capsule only in two cases: there is something that needs your handling and you have not yet seen it in the card (it stays still, not shrinking or fading, until the card is opened or that thing no longer needs you; the ones seen when the card is opened — the same thing, the same wait — are marked as seen, and after collapsing it returns to an orange icon, expanding again only when a new one or another wait arrives), and just finished ("Done · tap to see the result" etc., expanding for 4 seconds and then shrinking back to an icon with a result badge, disappearing after 10 seconds in total, or returning to the icon when "always show"; ones already seen in the card are just an icon; when the conversation is deleted, the one that just ended disappears immediately and the card no longer follows it). When there are several things the capsule says "N things", and with something needing your handling it says "1 needs you · 2 things"; the text uses `Running.narration` first, then `reason`, and replay and batch execution are written as "Following the route 3/7" (when read by a screen reader the icon's description is `CapsuleCopy.describe`, such as "Claire is working: …; tap to see details"). Within the same thing and the same state each sentence stays for at least 1.5 seconds (only the last sentence is kept during this), the capsule only grows wider and never narrower, and the width is set again only when the state changes, it expands or it shrinks; a line is at most 60% of the screen wide. The icon and the capsule share the same background, the width slowly changes between the two, and the icon stays attached to the docked side without moving (it switches directly when animations are off). `CapsuleContent` is the stateless capsule UI, and `CapsuleController` connects task state, the switch, the foreground, the screen and the service to the model, adding, moving and tearing down windows by the result. Tapping the icon or the capsule expands the card (`CapsuleCardContent.kt`, stateless; the content is decided by the pure logic `CapsuleCards`, see `app/capsule/CapsuleCardModel.kt`): the card says only one thing, has no list and does not switch inside the card: first the task of the conversation that just spoke in the card (to see the answer), otherwise the first thing by needs your handling (takeover, agree, answer), in progress, just finished; when there are other things at the same time, go to Claire's conversation page to see them. The chosen thing selects the card by its current state (not by history): one that has not ended and is currently operating an App or web page (with its own virtual screen `TaskRun.displayId`, or using the built-in browser `TaskRun.webOpen`) gets the task card, and the rest — pure chat, already ended (including ones that operated an App and returned an answer when done), and when idle — get the conversation card (`CapsuleCards.taskCard`). The task card shows the most recent three steps while working (the same reduction as the conversation page's steps, `ChatViewModel.reduce`) and the elapsed time; while the task is still in progress (working, Claire asking you, waiting for a condition), stop is the black stop key on the right of the input field like the conversation page's input bar; taking over is why (listing the item, amount and address when paying), "I'll handle it" (carrying the id of this wait, the same as on the notification), and stop, and when queued after another takeover it says "Finish handling the other takeover first, then this one" and gives no "I'll handle it", and when first with more after it says "After handling this one, N more are waiting for you" (the order reads the `TakeoverQueue` shared by several tasks, re-read whenever the queue changes; among equally important ones the one earlier in the queue is mentioned first, and after the earlier one is handed back the next immediately becomes the capsule's "needs you"); agreeing uses the same wording and report id as the conversation page's confirmation card, with agree / disagree, plus "Allow all for this task" and "Always allow", and bottom-line operations only get "Agree this once"; when Claire asks you and options are given they are listed as buttons (as the answer); waiting for a condition says why, and offers continue when it can continue; at the bottom is "Back to Claire to see the full process". The conversation card is that conversation's exchanges (ordered by `CapsuleThreads` in `app/capsule/CapsuleThread.kt`, drawn by `LazyColumn`): the same record as the conversation page (the turns `ConversationStore` recorded, attached to those not yet recorded and this conversation's current task, deduplicated by run-record id), your words are a small bubble on the right, Claire's answers are the full text with markers removed (`CapsuleCards.answerText`), older ones above; the reply segment interrupted by an interjection is placed before that interjection as on the conversation page, in a pale color with a line below "Interrupted by your new message"; a reply already finished before the interjection is also placed before it, looking like an answer; an interjection not yet handed to the model is placed after the answer being written; a turn that operated an App or web page (reading its activity object from that turn's run record, with cleared records treated as pure chat) is collapsed into one line "✓ Shopping · done" or "12306 · failed" followed by the answer, without steps, screen or sources; pure chat grows its text as it answers, with the stop key on the right of the input field, and when it needs you to answer or agree, that question is in the exchanges with the buttons fixed below. It lays out the most recent 10 turns first, and scrolling to the top lays out 10 more before them (each keyed by run-record id, with the list staying in place); when opened, the latest sentence you said stops at the top of the visible area, and text that grows downward follows only when you were already at the bottom (same as the conversation page). At the bottom are the input field and "Back to Claire". The buttons all go through `TaskService`'s existing actions (cancel, continue, takeover, answer, agree, disagree), carrying that thing's task id; "Back to Claire" and tapping the notification use the same entry `TaskService.openAppIntent`. Tapping outside the card, the collapse button or back collapses it to an icon (or a capsule when there is something you have not seen that needs your handling); while expanded the model's timer stops, so a result the user is looking at is not taken away, and after collapsing it continues the remaining time; when the App is handed to you the card collapses and is replaced by the top bar. The icon and the card are each in their own overlay window, each staying in its own place (when collapsed the card window is explicitly made invisible, with empty content only doing layout, not relying on zero size to retract the system's old frame): when expanding, the card in its own window scales and fades in from the icon's position (about 240 ms; it first waits for the card to be laid out before starting, and before that the icon is still in place), and the icon's window empties accordingly; when collapsing, the card shrinks back toward the icon's position and fades out, and the icon appears at its side at the same time (all in the same animation in `app/capsule/CapsuleCardContent.kt`, moving only the layer, and going straight there when animations are off). It does not let one window move back and forth between two places, because Android turns a mere move of a window into a sliding animation; nor does it change the window size frame by frame (every change re-lays out and swaps the canvas, which stutters). The width between the icon and the capsule also changes only while drawing: the layout goes to the wider of the two at once, and it shrinks only after drawing. While working, taking over or waiting for a condition, the virtual screen small picture is on the left (that thing's own `TaskRun.frames`: the virtual screen's frames on the phone and the built-in browser's frames on the web, the same source as the in-app picture-in-picture; tasks with no screen do not get it), received only while the card is expanded and looking at that thing, at most 5 per second, with ones the same as the previous one not pushed (`previewFrames`, in `app/capsule/CapsulePreview.kt`); tapping once enlarges it, and tapping again or tapping outside returns. The capsule can be dragged (`OverlayHost`'s window is laid out over the whole screen and only as large as the content, dragging is computed on the window root in screen coordinates, and it counts as dragging only after passing the touch threshold, with a pressed button cancelled accordingly and not treated as a tap), and on release it docks to the nearest left or right side (12dp from the edge, avoiding the status bar, notch and bottom gesture bar, with the range read from the system bars by `OverlayHost.area`); which side it docks on and the fraction of the placeable range its height occupies are recorded in `FeatureFlags`, and it is placed back by the fraction next time and after rotation; the "You handle it" bar is at the top center and can only be dragged up and down. The card width is fixed and centered horizontally. The conversation card has a fixed height that does not change with content: 58% of the screen height, and when the input method pops up it is brought above it (the height and position are set by the tallest shape of this round's input method after it pops up, so one more or fewer pinyin candidate row does not change it; the middle section gets shorter and the input field is still visible); the task card is as tall as its content with no empty space below, and the middle section takes the taller of the left small picture (by the picture's aspect ratio) and the right text, and without a picture it is as tall as the right text, at most the same as the conversation card, with longer text scrolling in the right section. When the middle does not fit, both cards scroll inside it, and the buttons and input field are fixed at the bottom. The card's position is recorded by its top edge: the fraction of the top edge within the placeable range (the range is based on the tallest card, and cards of different heights align their top edges at the same fraction), and when switching cards (task card and conversation card swapping) or when the content grows or shrinks the top edge does not move and goes straight there (the window changes size with the content only once, not frame by frame), and when there is no room below (gesture bar, input method) it moves up to just fit. The card is dragged up and down by its title bar (`OverlayWindow.draggable` gives a handle, dragging only when pressed inside it, and sliding up and down in the middle is scrolling; likewise it counts as dragging only after passing the touch threshold and does not tap the collapse button), and on release it is clamped within the status bar, notch and gesture bar, with the top fraction recorded separately in `FeatureFlags` (the fraction recorded for the previous fixed-height card happens to be its top fraction, and is still read), and it is placed back by it next time and after rotation. The algorithms are the pure functions `CapsuleDocking` and `CardDocking` (in `app/capsule/CapsuleDock.kt`). When read by a screen reader the capsule has an extra "Move to the other side of the screen" instead of dragging, and the card's buttons, small picture, collapse and back all have descriptions. The bottom of the card has an input field (absent when taking over or agreeing): while running or waiting for a condition it is "Add or change requirements…", handed to that thing (the same path as an interjection while a task runs on the conversation page, with `TaskService.start` carrying the id of this sentence); when Claire asks you it is "Answer Claire…" (the option buttons stay); after finishing it is "Continue saying…", continuing in the same conversation; when there is no task (the switch is "always show") tapping the Claire icon is the idle conversation card: continuing the conversation the user is in at the moment (`ConversationStore.current`, announced by `ChatViewModel` when starting a new conversation, switching conversations or speaking in a new conversation, with the choice in the pure logic `CapsuleIdle`: when there is no record it continues the most recent one, and a newly started conversation that has not spoken is a new conversation; the one it is in takes priority over finished results still hanging in other conversations); the top says "Continue \"<most recent conversation title>\" · New conversation", the middle is that conversation's exchanges (empty for a new conversation), and the input field is "What would you like Claire to do?". The latter two start a task the same as sending on the conversation page (recorded into that conversation; queued and not lost when another task is running), after which the card follows that conversation's task and the answer grows in the conversation card; when that thing opens an App it switches to the task card. When the card is collapsed or the capsule hidden, unsent words stay in the input field (`CardInputUi.collapsed`; the captured screen is not kept, and a note written for it is kept), to be edited next time it is expanded with the cursor at the end, and cleared only when sent; it exists only in memory. The left words do not follow the thing they were originally for, and are sent next time to the thing the card is currently facing; double-tap to send the screen and long-press to talk and release to send directly do not carry it, and it stays as before. The input field is normally only one line of hint (showing those words when there are unsent ones), and only when tapped does it let the overlay receive the keyboard (`OverlayWindow.setFocusable`), returning immediately when sending, collapsing, tapping outside or pressing back; when the input method pops up it makes way for it (the input method window's position is read by `OverlayHost.imeTop` from the accessibility window list, re-read whenever the window changes, no matter which App the input method popped up for): the card, the icon and the expanded capsule all move above it, and return when the input method collapses. When untouched for 3 seconds the icon fades to about 40% (the outer ring and badge stay solid), and it recovers immediately when touched, dragged, or when the state or count changes; the expanded capsule, needing your handling, the "You handle it" bar and the card do not fade (the rule is the pure logic `CapsuleFade`). On rotation it is placed again by the recorded side and height fraction; full-screen playback is not treated specially. The switch is in Settings › "Floating capsule" (in `app/settings/ui/SettingsSubPages.kt`): off / show when there is a task / always show (the default, able to double-tap the capsule to send this screen at any time), stored in `FeatureFlags`; ones never chosen (including ones upgraded from older versions) follow the default. The × at the top right of the card only collapses it; the bottommost line of the card (after "View process" on the task card and "Back to Claire" on the conversation card, and alone on an idle card with no conversation yet) has a gray "Turn off the floating capsule" (`CardActions.hideCapsule`): the same place as choosing "Off" in settings (`FeatureFlags.setCapsuleMode`), and when the switch changes `CapsuleModel` stops showing it and the capsule and card are retracted on the spot, with tasks still running and still sending notifications; immediately a hint pops up "The floating capsule is off; you can turn it back on in "Settings › Floating capsule"" (with the two names taken from settings, changing with the language).

- **The current screen** (double-tap the capsule): double-tapping the icon or the capsule sends the screen the user is looking at on the main screen at this moment to the conversation in the card that a single tap would expand; afterwards it is exactly like sending a sentence in the card (an interjection while running, an answer when Claire asks you, continuing after finishing, continuing the most recent conversation or starting a new one when idle, the same `CapsuleController.submit`), and then the card is expanded to see the answer. A single tap waits for Android's double-tap timeout (`ViewConfiguration.getDoubleTapTimeout`) before expanding, and the single/double tap judgment is the pure logic `DoubleTap`; in a screen reader there is also "Send Claire the current screen". When that card has no input field (taking over, agreeing) there is nowhere to send, and a double tap is the same as a single tap. Whether it is shown still follows the floating capsule switch, with no separate switch; when Claire itself is in the foreground or the screen is locked the capsule is not there anyway. The card's input field has a screenshot button on the left: the capture hangs above the input field (a thumbnail, "Your current screen", the App · time, and × to remove), a sentence can be added before sending, and sending only it is the same as a double tap.
  Screenshot: the overlay window is not torn down, only its content is hidden (`OverlayWindow.hiddenWhile`), and the screenshot is taken after the frame with it hidden is drawn and handed to the system (frame commit callback) and after two more refreshes (the compositor swaps it in); when the card's input field is open it is collapsed first and the input method is waited out. The screenshot uses the accessibility service's `takeScreenshot` on the main screen (`ScreenAccessibility.takeMainScreenshot`, waiting a second and taking it again when too early), and right after it the overlay is shown again and the icon flashes, and at the same moment the accessibility tree of App windows on the main screen is read (the status bar, navigation bar, input method and overlay do not count). Organization is in the background (`MainScreenCapture.build`): the text on screen first uses elements with names in the tree, and when the tree is not enough (the same `TreeAdequacy` as task screen reading) it recognizes text on the picture (`PhoneOcr`), adding only what is not in the tree and removing misrecognized lines and text in the status bar and navigation bar lines; an element that merely joins several passages of text inside it (the whole row of a settings item) is not listed separately, keeping the passages inside; each line carries a center point (0–999, with the pixel centers of tree elements converted too), top to bottom and left to right within the same line (`core/screen/user/UserScreenText.kt`). A screenshot-protected page (the screenshot reports a secure window error, or the capture is almost all black while there is an App window on screen) keeps no picture, only records which App it was and that it could not be captured. When the foreground is Claire itself it does not capture.
  Storage: it is stored with the conversation in that conversation's workspace (`/workspace/screens/<id>.jpg` and `.json`, `app/chat/UserScreenFiles.kt`, read and written through the workspace file gate, holding the tree lock's permission when writing), in the same place as attachments, and it disappears when the workspace is deleted; commands in the sandbox can read this image too. The message itself is an ordinary sentence: a separate line `[[screen:<id>]]`, and the rest is the note (`core/screen/user/UserScreenMessage.kt`), stored into the conversation as usual, as an interjection or an answer, and an image chosen from the album or files is still an attachment line, the two not mixed. The conversation page and the card draw it as a screenshot bubble (`app/chat/ui/ScreenMessage.kt`: a thumbnail, "Your current screen · App · time", with the note in a separate bubble; one that cannot be captured is one line "Not captured"), and the conversation title, drawer and notification call it the note or "Your current screen" (`ScreenCopy.plain`).
  For the model (`core/agent/UserScreenTurns.kt`, text in `UserScreenPrompt` and `user_screen.*` in `feedback/loop.md`): this sentence becomes "The user just captured the screen he is looking at (App, time), with no note / note: …" (when it cannot be captured, say truthfully that it was not captured), and when the main model can see images the screenshot is attached to this sentence; immediately after is a `user_screen` context (not counted in the budget, kept during compression): id, App and package name, time, the text on screen (tagged as untrusted), and when there is no note it also carries the rule for guessing intent from the screen — if it is just answering, reading or looking up, do it directly; to write or change something elsewhere (note something down, send, publish, book, pay, delete, change settings) first ask once with one sentence via ask_user, giving options (buttons in the card), and do it after agreement; when it is unclear which App the user uses for such things, ask that too, and remember it when memory is available. With a note it is handled as usual without extra rules; confirmation of irreversible operations is as before. Sending only the screen without speaking does not ask the intent judgment (there are no words to judge). Screens in earlier turns are left as one line (App, time, the first few characters on screen and the id), and to see them again use `view_user_screen`: by id it gives the App, time and text, attaching the screenshot when the main model can see images, and when it cannot see images but a vision model is configured it asks the vision model about the stored screenshot with the question (role prompt `user_screen`); it is offered only when this turn or earlier turns carry a screen. The text on screen is recorded into the evidence ledger (answer sources); outcome judgment and memory extraction read the replaced sentence; sentences with a screen are not recorded as experience routes.
- **Notifications**: running, needing takeover, needing an answer and results all have notifications; the takeover button is on the notification. The "Needs you" and "Task result" channels are built at high importance (popping up), and "Task progress" stays quiet; when the capsule is currently showing on a lit screen, "Needs you" is still sent but quietly enters the notification bar (using the system's grouped-alert rules: putting this one into a group without a summary and only letting the summary alert means the system neither rings nor pops a banner, the same approach as the system's own silent notifications; no channel change and no new channel), and when the screen is locked, the capsule is off or cannot be drawn it pops up as before; the result channel changed id (`task_result_v2`, with the old one deleted at startup), and channels are created everywhere through `TaskService.ensureChannels`. The status monitor checks whether these two channels were lowered, hints when they were, and the fix button opens that channel's system settings page directly, falling back to the app notification overview page when it cannot; during phone setup it also checks once after notifications are allowed. The Settings › "Notifications" page (in `app/settings/ui/SettingsSubPages.kt`) only looks at states and goes to the system to change them, and does not put notification switches in Claire: the top is the notification master switch ("Go turn it on" when not allowed, requesting permission first from Android 13, otherwise opening the app notification page), and below it "Needs you", "Task result" and "Task progress" each in a row, stating when it sends and whether it currently "pops up", "only enters the notification bar" or "is off", and tapping a row opens that channel's system settings page (the same jumps and fallbacks as above). The state judgment is the pure function `NotificationOverview.of` (`app/settings/NotificationOverview.kt`): by channel importance, `IMPORTANCE_NONE` is off, no lower than `IMPORTANCE_HIGH` pops up, and the rest only enter the notification bar; "Needs you" and "Task result" are marked when they do not pop up (progress being quiet or off does not count as abnormal), and when the master switch is off all three rows are off; the "Notifications" row in "Frequently used" on the home page shows the overall state on the right (normal / N items will not pop up / off); the phone operation page no longer repeats the entry. Channel importance is read into `SystemStatus` by `SystemStatusMonitor` and updated with `refresh` when coming back from system settings.
- **Other pages**: Settings (`app/settings/ui/SettingsScreen.kt`) has a total status card at the top (reusing `Prerequisites.check`, "All ready" when everything is fine, becoming a hint with a fix button when there is a problem), and the home page is split into "Frequently used" (models, phone operation, task runs, floating capsule, language and appearance, notifications), "My data" (memory and data, app access) and "Other" (advanced and others, about Claire). Advanced and others adds only one level, gathering network proxy, files and compute environment, connecting a computer, MCP servers, recovery and repair and the developer options in support builds; its sub-pages return to advanced and others. Token usage belongs to the models page and opens via `Route.Usage`; autonomous mode belongs to task runs; site sign-in and operation routes belong to memory and data, with the MemoryDataScreen managing internal returns, and the original memory / skills / pending confirmation home pages don't add a level. Models, usage, memory and data and about go through `NavStack`, and the rest of the settings sub-pages are still switched by the settings page itself. The "Recovery and repair" page is the authorization switch for phone operation (on by default, and toggling executes directly without another confirmation), and unsupported systems (below Android 13) and devices whose compute environment is unsupported do not show the corresponding entry. Engineering switches (`app/settings/FeatureFlags.kt`) stay in developer options, in "Speed" and "Experiment" groups, plus vision grounding verification, detailed Trace and run statistics; developer options are shown only in debug and performance-test builds, and release builds still apply the saved switches without showing the entry. The memory and data page (`MemoryScreen`, `SkillsSection`) has three sections "Memory" (grouped by fact, preference and procedure; tapping a row raises a panel: edit, pin, delete), "Skills" (the switch enables and disables) and "Pending confirmation" (procedures awaiting approval, new skill drafts, App knowledge drafts), with switches, site sign-in, operation routes and clear all at the end. There are also plans and the phone assistant setup wizard; the sources page (`SourceScreen`) shows only the screenshot when a source has one (tapping opens it full screen and enlarged), and the original text excerpt when there is none, with the same top bar, grouping and rows as the licenses page (`LicensesScreen`).
- **Settings search**: the search box at the top of the settings home matches each entry's title, location (group name, parent page) and synonym option resources in the current UI language, without writing separate keywords, so the 13 languages follow automatically; the index covers settings sub-pages, direct dialogs (auto-archive, network proxy, web search, fully quit), the sub-pages under models (usage, voice input, read-aloud, call mode, voice service, image/video models) and the internal entries of each high-frequency sub-page (`SettingsFocusKey`). Entries are registered in `SettingsSearchIndex` (stable id, destination, string resources for title/location/synonyms, required capability), and matching and sorting and highlight ranges are in `SettingsSearch` (pure function, insensitive to case and Latin diacritics, comparing other scripts as-is), the UI is in `SettingsSearchUi`, and the destination is turned by `SettingsScreen` into switching pages or a callback to the outside. When a page-internal entry is tapped, `SettingsScreen` sends a locate request through `FocusTargetHost` in `ui/components/FocusTarget.kt` (passed via CompositionLocal), and `Modifier.focusHighlightTarget` on the target row automatically scrolls it into view and flashes it briefly with the accent color after entering composition and finishing layout (a collapsed entry in the advanced area such as "Connect a computer" expands first accordingly). Entries requiring a capability (`SettingsRequirement`: developer options, Root, compute environment, recovery and repair, model sub-pages, parallel tasks, real-time task notifications, etc.) do not appear in search when they do not appear in settings. Each `SettingsPage` either registers entries or states the reason in `unindexedPages`, and `SettingsSearchIndexTest` also checks page and `SettingsFocusKey` consistency.
- **About and checking for updates**: the about page (`app/about/ui/AboutScreen.kt`, text in `AboutCopy`) is entered from the settings home page or "About Claire" at the very bottom of the drawer (at the bottom of the drawer "Settings" is on the left and "About Claire" on the right, going to `NavStack`'s `Route.About`). At the top are the version number, the Beta marker (`BuildConfig.BETA`) and the build number (`BuildConfig.GIT_HASH`, taking the short hash of the current commit at compile time, or dev when there is no git); below are checking for updates and the update record; report a problem (go to GitHub to file an issue, with the issue template prefilled with three lines: device model, Android version and Claire version; copy diagnostics); open-source repository and usage instructions; data statement; the footer "Released under the Apache-2.0 open-source license" goes to the licenses page (`Route.License`: the license text `assets/licenses/Apache-2.0.txt`, with third-party components listed below). External addresses are all in `app/about/MikaLinks.kt`. Checking for updates (`app/about/UpdateChecker.kt`) by default accesses GitHub's releases/latest interface (no login, short timeout), and being unable to connect, rate limiting or being unable to parse all count as not found; in Settings › About › Update mirrors, mirrors can be turned on and the list managed (order, add, delete, edit, `app/about/UpdateMirrors.kt`, file `files/update-mirrors.json`, with gh-proxy/ghfast/ghproxy.net measured as usable among the presets in November 2026), and after turning it on the mirrors and the official source are queried in parallel, a new version found by a mirror is shown first, and after the official one is found the official version and verification information take precedence; when the official one fails the newer version found by a mirror is kept; when a mirror does not proxy the API it falls back to the redirect (302 Location) of `/releases/latest` or the tag in the page, and then reads the APK file name and sha256 from `/releases/expanded_assets/<tag>` (`app/about/UpdateProbe.kt`); every time the App opens (`MainActivity.onStart`) it quietly checks once, only blocking back-to-back ones (not checking when less than two minutes since the last automatic check, `UpdateSchedule.MIN_GAP_MS`; it records the time before starting so simultaneous ones do not check twice), and on failure it does not hint and shows no banner, and only a manual "Check for updates" shows the result. A found version is stored in `files/update-v1.json`, and when it is newer than the current one the drawer, settings home and about page carry a red dot; when there is an APK attachment, "Download update" in the update panel hands it to the system DownloadManager to download in the background (`app/about/ApkUpdater.kt`): it only accepts HTTPS addresses under the official repository's `releases/download/` (`ApkIntegrity.requireOfficialUrl`), the file is placed in this app's external dedicated directory `Download/updates/`, only one is kept at a time, the task id is kept so progress can continue after reopening, and it can be deleted and retried after failure; the download notification is shown only while downloading (tapping a completion notification would bypass verification and open the installer directly, so it is not kept). After the download completes, tapping "Install update" first needs permission to install unknown apps, and continues after authorization returns; before opening the system installer it checks in order: the size and SHA-256 of the release attachment (the interface's `digest` field, verified only when present, and refused when it does not match), the package name, the version number being higher than the installed one, and the signing certificate matching the installed one (or the installed certificate being in the new package's signing history); ones that do not pass have the file deleted and the reason explained (`ApkRejected`). The system installer verifies the signature again and asks for confirmation. After the update is installed, the next start clears the left installer package (`dropInstalled` in `MikaApp`). When there is no APK attachment it still opens the release page.
- **Diagnostics**: collected only when the user actively taps "Copy diagnostics" (always at the bottom of the guide page, and also in the step that failed when there is a failure; and "Copy diagnostics" in the about page › report a problem), first shown in full to the user in a bottom sheet (`app/ui/DiagnosticsSheet.kt`), and only tapping "Copy" puts it on the clipboard and tapping "Share" hands it to the system share sheet, with nothing sent automatically. Collection (`app/about/DiagnosticsCollector.kt`) only reads existing states: system status, the built-in assistant's state and the original text of the most recent pairing or connection failure (`BuiltinShellEngine.lastError`), how many candidates each source gave when looking for a connection port, how many ports were open and how many looked like adbd when scanning this machine, whether there is a port the user filled in (reported by `AdbConnectPort` to `BuiltinShellManager.lastConnectCandidates`, counts only, without writing addresses or port numbers), those automatic recovery items, model configuration, run summaries and update checks; the guide page's steps are computed with the guide page's own input (`setupInputs`, including steps the user confirmed) by the same `PhoneSetupFlow`, not computed again separately. When written as text (`app/about/Diagnostics.kt`, pure function) the sections have a fixed order and English keys: App, Device, Phone control, Recovery, Models, Recent runs, Update; providers only write the interface type and host name, and runs only write relative time, outcome, failure category, step count and model, not keys, sign-ins, task goals, answers or screen content; finally the whole thing goes through redaction (IP plus port, port numbers, emails, "name=value" that looks like a credential, then `Redactor`). The last section of the GitHub issue template only hints where the user can copy and paste it, without bringing it along automatically.
- **Compatibility self-check** (Settings › Recovery and repair › Compatibility self-check, `app/selfcheck/ui/SelfCheckScreen.kt`): the user taps "Start self-check", and in about a minute it checks what works on this phone and produces a report that can be copied and pasted to GitHub. The pure logic is in `core/selfcheck/`: the items (`SelfCheck`: device, permissions, built-in assistant, virtual screen, opening an App, screen reading, tapping and typing, continuous running, main screen undisturbed, built-in browser, sandbox, another copy of an App) run in order, each states which items must pass first, and when one fails or is skipped the ones depending on it are skipped with which item is missing (partly available does not count as not passed); each item has its own time limit (5–25 seconds), and the whole run has another 90-second total limit, with the remainder recorded as skipped when it is used up; errors count as failures without interrupting later items; on cancellation the running one and later ones are recorded as skipped, and `SelfCheckPort.release` is always called once at the end (`SelfCheckRunner`). Each item's conclusion is "state + one line of English fact + one plain-language piece of advice for the UI (`Advice`)"; the report (`SelfCheckReport`) has only the version, device model, system version, processor architecture, memory, screen size, whether there is wireless debugging and one line per item, with English keys, and finally the whole thing goes through the same redaction as "Copy diagnostics" (`Diagnostics.sanitize`), without writing accounts, the App list, the user's content or serial numbers. The Android implementation is `AndroidSelfCheck` (`app/selfcheck/`), and the phone items go through the same set the task uses: the assistant connection shared in the process (`SharedShell`), `AgentDisplay` creating the screen, `AndroidScreen` opening the page and `tapRef`, `typeText`, `scroll`, `back`, so the result reflects the Agent's real behavior. What is operated on the virtual screen is Claire's own test page `SelfCheckTargetActivity` (a counter button, an input field, a column of rows that can scroll, numbers that keep changing and a bar that keeps changing color, stating that it is Claire's test page): like the service connected to the computer, it is open only to the shell level: exported in the manifest but requiring the caller to hold INTERACT_ACROSS_USERS_FULL (shell and the system have it; ordinary Apps cannot get it), with no intent-filter, in a separate task and out of recents (`ManifestProvidersTest` enforces it), and it closes itself immediately when opened on the main screen. `AndroidScreen.openOwnPage` hands its component to the assistant through `IMikaShell.startSystemAction`, and the assistant, recognizing this component (`SystemIntents.OWN_PAGE_CLASS`, with the package name still having to be Claire's), starts it as shell directly onto Claire's screen (the same path as opening an App: startActivityAsUser with the start screen, falling back to `am start --display`); a shell start is not subject to Android's restriction on starting pages in the background, so it can open whether or not Claire is in the foreground. No new assistant interface is added. System settings are only opened through the ordinary `openApp`, with a check that it opens on Claire's screen and not the main screen, and then going back once to leave, without tapping anything inside. The continuous running item watches the test page for 15 seconds: the virtual screen's live picture not changing for 3 seconds, or the changing numbers in the control tree not changing for 3 seconds, counts as stopped. The main screen item compares the top task on the main screen at the start and at each node. The built-in browser item builds a separate web display and tab pool (without giving it the site list and without recording access into site sign-in) and opens the local page packaged with the App `app/src/main/assets/selfcheck/page.html` (through WebViewAssetLoader, without network access), going through `WebScreen`'s page reading and tapping by id and then checking. The sandbox item runs only on arm64 `echo ok` and one line of Python; a compute environment that is not ready counts as partly available and is not installed just for the self-check. The other App copy item only counts how many usable other copies of users there are (`appCopyUsers`, private space not counted), without listing Apps. Leaving this page cancels it, and the virtual screen, test page, web tabs and assistant connection are released on the spot; it does not let it start while a task is running. When done the whole report is shown on the page, and "Copy report" only copies; "Give feedback on GitHub" copies the report and opens the new issue page, selecting the device feedback template `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/device-report.yml` and prefilling only the title (`[Compatibility] Android version model`), with the report pasted by the user and not put into the address (the address has a length limit). The test that runs the whole set on a real device is `SelfCheckDeviceTest`.
- **First onboarding**: welcome (`app/setup/ui/WelcomeScreen.kt`) → configure models → phone operation (`PhoneSetupScreen`, a numbered checklist that can be skipped; after connecting successfully the checklist is kept and all ticked, with a line at the top "You're all set" and the bottom main button "Start using it", which enters the conversation only when tapped, and "Finish" when entered from settings). Which steps to take is decided by `Onboarding` (`app/setup/ui/Onboarding.kt`) by "whether it has been seen": only when there is no main model and no model has been configured in the onboarding flow does it take the first two steps (ones already configured skip directly); phone operation follows the original judgment (the system is new enough, not ready yet, and the guide has not been seen).
- **UI language** (`app/i18n/`): UI text is all Android string resources, in 13 UI languages in total: English is the default (`res/values/`), Simplified Chinese is in `res/values-zh/`, Traditional Chinese is in `res/values-b+zh+Hant/` (Android matches by script, and `values-zh` does not take effect on Traditional Chinese systems), and there are also Arabic, Persian, Japanese, Korean, Spanish, Portuguese, Russian, Indonesian, Turkish and Vietnamese (`values-ar`, `values-fa`, `values-ja`, `values-ko`, `values-es`, `values-pt`, `values-ru`, `values-in`, `values-tr`, `values-vi`; Indonesian keeps Android's old language code in). Each set of entries corresponds one-to-one with English (`StringResourcesTest` checks entries, placeholders, the quantity words that each language's plurals have to write, Traditional against Simplified, and that no Chinese is left in English and languages that do not use Han characters), so when the system is in these languages the corresponding translation is shown, and systems in other languages show English. Settings › "Language" can choose follow the system or one of them (`AppLanguage`; each item's name is written in its own language, and "Follow system" is written in the current language; the language tags are zh-Hans, zh-Hant, ar, fa, en, ja, ko, es, pt, ru, id, tr, vi, and languages other than Chinese are recognized by language code without looking at the region, with old codes such as in given by Android Locale first replaced by current codes before comparison; when a Chinese tag does not write a script, it is filled in by ICU's `ULocale.addLikelySubtags`, with Hant being Traditional and other Chinese being Simplified, and zh-Hans stored by the old choice of "Chinese" is Simplified Chinese): from Android 13 it is handed to the system's "App languages" (`LocaleManager`, with `android:localeConfig` in the manifest listing these languages, and what can be changed in system settings is the same value, with the system responsible for remembering it and recreating the UI); on Android 11 and 12 it is stored locally, applied through `AppLanguage.wrap` to each Activity's `attachBaseContext` and the floating capsule's Compose host, and the current UI is recreated after a change. In Compose, use `stringResource` directly; where text is counted outside the UI (status copy, notifications, the task service, the capsule's data, tool wording) use `tr` / `trPlural` (`app/i18n/UiText.kt`), which take resources by App language, and in JVM unit tests they are replaced with `ResFileTexts` reading resource files directly (tests asserting Chinese install `ChineseTexts`, and device tests use `ChineseUi` to temporarily switch the app language to Simplified Chinese). Quantities use plural resources, and dates, weekdays and months are written by `uiLocale()`. Tool wording is looked up in resources by the label the tool declares (`ToolLabels.TOOL_LABELS`). Only the UI is translated: prompts and the bodies of the built-in app/website knowledge and skills are English; on-screen names, matching tables and localization resources keep Chinese where the product has to recognize or reproduce it, and answers, questions and titles written by the model follow the language the user speaks.

## 16. Voice input

Native Doubao streaming speech recognition 2.0 can be configured separately on the speech-to-text page, uploading PCM in real time and returning intermediate full text; credentials are encrypted independently and do not go through the Ark chat model path. For the protocol, lifecycle and test limits see [doubao-asr.md](doubao-asr.md).

"Qwen real-time speech recognition" is also configured in the same group (three tabs on the settings page: self-hosted service, Bailian · Beijing, Bailian · Singapore), sharing `LiveSpeech` (`voice/LiveAsr.kt`): text control messages plus binary PCM, producing text while uploading when held down, and after release waiting for the server's end event (`stopped` / `task-finished`) before handing over the final text, with disconnection or timeout counting as failure and intermediate results never sent to the model as final text. The self-hosted service only needs an address; Bailian's API Key is encrypted independently by region, with an optional workspace ID; it tries to connect once before saving, without sending audio. See [live-asr.md](live-asr.md).

Hold-to-talk has two entry points: the conversation page's input field and the floating capsule. Both get their session only through `VoiceSource` (`voice/VoiceInput.kt`, `VoiceInput` in the App), replaced by a fake in UI tests; the recognition and transcription logic is all in `app/voice/`, and the UI only handles gestures, visuals and wiring.

- **Unified voice catalog** (`app/voice/model/`): reading aloud, speech-to-text and real-time calls share one catalog, no longer each keeping their own selection. `VoiceCatalogStore` stores services (`VoiceService`, whose account is an existing provider, credentials managed by the catalog itself, or none), models (`VoiceModel`, one model can carry `stt`/`tts`/`realtime` bindings at the same time) and selections (`VoiceSelections`: transcription is `SttSelection.Auto`/`Off`/a model, reading aloud and real-time are `VoiceModelRef`), with one snapshot per JSON (`VoiceCatalogSnapshot`). The three selection controllers (`SttSelectionController`/`TtsSelectionController`/`RealtimeSelectionController`) share through `VoiceModelEnvironment`; `VoiceAdapterRegistry` maps resolved models (`ResolvedVoiceModel`) to the three families of typed adapters (`SttAdapter`/`TtsAdapter`/`RealtimeAdapter`, implementations in `adapters/`) by the bound protocol, and neither the UI nor runtime decides on its own who to use. Old selections (`SpeechChoice`/`VoiceChoice`/`CallVoiceProfile`, and the `ttsModels` field kept in serialization) are read only once during one-time migration (`LegacyVoiceMigration`, building the whole thing first, writing self-managed credentials, then taking effect atomically), after which `legacyMigrationComplete` closes the door; a failed migration goes down the old path as before and records the reason in `VoiceModelEnvironment.migrationError`, with old fields still readable and not rolled back or deleted.

- **Voice key pool and fallback chain** (`voice/model/VoiceFallbacks.kt`, `VoiceAccountResolver.withPooledKey`, `FallbackTtsModel.kt`): a voice model that borrows a conversation provider account does not set up its own rotation, and each request takes a key fresh from that provider's `KeyPoolManager.shared` (the same `activeApiKey` as the conversation model's `keySupplier`), and when rejected (429/401/403, `keyRejectionOf` in `core/model/KeyRotation.kt`) it records cooldown/exhaustion as on the conversation side and switches to the next one, one use each; a service that manages its own credential still has a single key. Voice input and read-aloud each have a fallback chain (`VoiceSelections.sttFallbacks`/`ttsFallbacks`, at most `VoiceFallbacks.MAX`, changed the same way as the conversation fallback chain, and `VoiceCatalogStore` tidies it up when a model is deleted or the chain head changes). It switches to the next only when the whole model is rate-limited, overloaded (5xx), unreachable or timed out, judged only in `VoiceFailover.canTryNext`, and provider errors are left to the same `FailoverPolicy.canFailover` as the conversation chain. For voice input only "record then transcribe" models can be the chain head and backups, the same recording is handed out in turn, and each waits at most `ProviderSpeech.TRANSCRIBE_TIMEOUT_MS`; streaming recognition and system recognition have no chain. Read-aloud backups use their own default voice (a `TtsProtocol.GeminiTts` request must carry a voice, and when the model has no recorded voice it uses the first in the `VoiceVoices.tts` list), and streaming read-aloud switches only before handing out the first segment of sound. The chain is used only at real runtime (`SttSelectionController.backupsFor`, `TtsSelectionController.createSpeaker`), and the settings page's preview and test only try the selected one; transcribing an audio file and real-time calls have no fallback chain. The UI is `voice/ui/VoiceFallbackSection.kt`, sharing `ui/components/FallbackChainCard.kt` with the conversation fallback chain.
- **The phone's built-in read-aloud** (`voice/SystemTtsModel.kt`, adapter `adapters/AndroidSystemTtsAdapter.kt`): the built-in model `android-system/system-tts` goes through standard `TextToSpeech`, creating a new engine for each synthesis, synthesizing to a temporary WAV and shutting down right after use; initialization and synthesis each have a limit (`SystemTtsModel.INIT_TIMEOUT_MS`, `SYNTHESIS_TIMEOUT_MS`), and an engine that does not respond or dies midway fails as a timeout. Which engine is used is recorded in the catalog as this model's `TtsBinding.engine` (package name, empty = the phone's default engine; it is kept when environment initialization rewrites the built-in binding), stored separately from the voice (`VoiceSettings.voice`, a usage preference). When system read-aloud is chosen, the read-aloud page has two more rows "Engine" and "Voice" (`voice/ui/SystemTtsRows.kt`): the engine queries services by `TTS_SERVICE` (registered in the manifest's `<queries>`), and voices are listed with `getVoices` for those with data installed, all using only public interfaces without recognizing vendor package names; changing the engine writes the catalog first, and only after it is written are voices reset to the default. When synthesizing, if the engine has the chosen voice it is used (no longer picked by UI language), and if it does not (an engine was changed, or another vendor's voice name is left from old settings) the default voice for the UI language is used; an error setting the voice counts as the system being unavailable. When the specified engine is not installed, `TextToSpeech` connects to the default engine itself, and when it connects but fails to initialize it falls back to the default engine once.
- **Self-connected voice services** (`voice/model/VoicePresets.kt`): "OpenAI-compatible service" is a protocol, not a specific vendor, and has no preset address; the user fills in the address (this machine, the LAN, or any service that speaks the OpenAI audio interface). The address accepts `http://` and `https://`, and may have a path and a non-default port; query, fragment, account and password are never accepted. The default port (http 80, https 443) is not written into the service id, so previously stored service ids and credential ids do not change; when the id has illegal characters, the credential id is replaced with legal characters plus a digest (`VoicePresets.secretId`). Recognition, read-aloud and real-time voice each have a group, and the read-aloud voice is recorded in the model's `TtsBinding.voice`, being the default when no voice is chosen (`withDefaultVoice` in `adapters/TtsAdapters.kt`). "Self-hosted recognition service" (Fun-ASR / Qwen3-ASR deployed by yourself) carries a recognition language, recorded in `SttBinding.language`, empty = automatic, unspecified, and at runtime does not follow the UI language. Changing the read-aloud model (`selectReadAloud`) is the same as changing the system read-aloud engine: write the catalog first, and only after it is written reset voices to the default.
- **Responsibilities of the `voice/` package**: `SpeechChoice` is the user's choice (automatic, phone built-in, none, or a model of a provider) and its storage in settings (`SpeechSettings`, one key and one JSON segment, treated as automatic when it cannot be read). `SpeechResolver` matches the choice, the phone's capability (`SystemCapability`) and existing providers to produce this time's engine `SpeechEngineSpec`. `SpeechSession` is the interface of one session and only emits `SpeechEvent` (volume, partial result, transcribing, final result, failure); there are two implementations: `SystemSpeech` (phone built-in recognition, showing text as you speak) and `ProviderSpeech` (recording with `PcmRecorder` and handing the whole `Wav` to `Transcriber` after release). `HoldToTalk` is the press-to-talk state machine (press, slide up to cancel, release, wrap up on time), without touching the UI and gestures, shared by the input field and the capsule.
