When the session ends by itself while the finger is still pressing (built-in recognition does not restart after one segment ends, or an error occurs midway but words were already heard), the result is kept first and the UI still shows the words heard, and when the finger leaves it is handled by where it is then: if it stops on cancel it is discarded, otherwise it is handed out, so when you want to cancel it does not fill in or send the words; an error while pressing is hinted without waiting for release, and only when it is stopped on cancel is it kept first, with no hint when released on cancel. `Segments` joins the segments that the recognition service ends and restarts, and `Restarts` decides whether and how long after an empty segment to restart.
- **The engine is chosen only in `SpeechResolver`**: the UI and `VoiceInput` do not judge who to use themselves. When "automatic" is chosen (never chosen), if the phone's built-in recognition is usable it is used; when the built-in system says it has it but it has been tried on this phone and does not respond, it gives `Unavailable(SystemNoResponse)` for the UI to state truthfully and point to settings, and **it will not quietly switch to a paid provider model**. When a provider model is chosen it is used, and when the provider is deleted it gives `Why.ProviderGone`. The various reasons of `Unavailable` are turned by the UI into a "voice input is not available yet" explanation, and the button goes directly to the choice page in settings.
- **Fallback when the phone's built-in recognition does not respond**: the system can only say whether this recognition service exists, and whether it responds is only known by trying. If `SystemSpeech` gets no callback at all within 3 seconds of starting to listen (`NO_RESPONSE_MS`, allowing time for the recognition service process's cold start), this time it ends with "No response"; after release, if there is still no final result after another 2 seconds (`FINAL_WAIT_MS`) it ends the same way: if it has responded it uses the words heard, and if there was no callback at all it is a no-response, and it will not stay without ending; within the same session a no-response is recorded only once. Only two consecutive times (`SpeechSettings.SILENT_STRIKES`) count as "no response" (`SpeechSettings.noteSystemSilent`, the count is also stored in settings, since one stall is mostly just a slow cold start), and any response in between recounts (`noteSystemAnswered`). After it is recorded, `SpeechResolver` no longer treats it as usable, and the settings page label shows "No response". When the user chooses "automatic" or "phone built-in" in settings, both the mark and the count are cleared, and a phone whose recognition service was changed or system updated can try again.
- **The phone's recognition language spelling does not match**: each vendor's built-in recognition registers languages differently, and Pixel registers Taiwan Chinese as `zh-Hant-TW` and `cmn-Hant-TW`, and sending `zh-TW` makes it report "language pack not installed" (13). When built-in recognition reports the language unusable (12, 13), `SystemSpeechSession` first uses `checkRecognitionSupport` (from Android 13) to ask which languages it has installed, and `SystemSpeech.installedTag` finds a spelling of the same language and the same region in them (not looking at the script subtag, treating `cmn` as `zh`), and when found it switches to that spelling and continues with built-in recognition; only when it is not found, cannot be asked or does not answer within 3 seconds does it switch to online recognition, sending the language in the original spelling. It asks only once per session.
- **Try another recognition service**: the system default recognition service may only be for the system's own voice assistant and gives no callback to other Apps. When more than one recognition service is registered on the phone (`SystemSpeech.services`), and the default does not respond while the finger is still pressing, it tries the next one by name, trying ones open to other Apps first, at most `SystemSpeech.MAX_ATTEMPTS` (`SystemSpeech.attempts`); the one that passes by name is recorded (`SpeechSettings.systemService`) and tried first next time. Usually, when there is only one service it still only tries the default entry. For build devices limited by `SystemSpeech.serviceCompatibility`, even when only one service is registered it keeps the by-name candidates, and when the default entry returns a client error, the finger is still pressing and no words have been heard yet it tries the next one; when released or words already exist it only wraps up and does not start a new recording. A client initialization failure says "Built-in recognition could not start" and is no longer attributed to the microphone being occupied. The `[Speech]` section of diagnostics lists the choice, the built-in recognition situation and the recognition services registered on the phone (component name, whether open, required permissions).
- **Provider transcription and the way it is remembered**: `Transcriber` sends requests by `TranscribeMethod`, only by protocol and without special-casing by model name, and errors go through the same `ModelHttp` mapping as conversation models. There are five forms: the audio transcription interface uploading a file (`TranscribeMethod.AudioTranscriptions`), bare base64 `input_audio` in the chat interface plus a transcription instruction (`TranscribeMethod.ChatInputAudio`), the same but with `data` as a data URI (`TranscribeMethod.ChatInputAudioUri`), Gemini's inline data (`TranscribeMethod.GeminiInline`), and a data URI `input_audio` in the chat interface sending only audio without an instruction (`TranscribeMethod.ChatInputAudioOnly`). The method (`method`) is part of the choice: `SpeechChoice.Provider.method` stored in an old choice is mapped into `SttBinding` when migrated into the catalog (audio transcription/bare base64/URI/Gemini each map to a binding), and at runtime the adapter derives `TranscribeMethod` from the binding (`ProviderSttAdapter.spec`), using it directly for each transcription instead of guessing by model name. The enum can only be appended to, not renamed or deleted, so old settings can be read back. Choosing a model on the settings page triggers a trial transcription, and it is selected and returns only when it passes; a model not in the list can be typed in manually.
- **Foreground capability compatibility for by-name services**: only on the by-name path of the above limited build, `SpeechServiceLease` temporarily binds a public service the caller already has access to, carrying the foreground capability the caller already has; recognition still goes through `SpeechRecognizer`, without calling private Binder protocols and without changing the system default service or permissions. It starts listening only after the connection is established, and the connection is likewise limited to 3 seconds; on release, if it has not connected yet it ends, and normal recognition unbinds after the final result/wrap-up timeout. Switching, failure and cancellation all clear the connection, and late callbacks from the old recognizer and old connection cannot act on the new session.
- **The recognition service itself lacks the recording permission**: the recognition service is another app with its own recording permission; if Claire's is granted but its is not, it still cannot record, and reports "no permission". At this point sending the user to Claire's permission page points to the wrong place. When `SystemSpeechSession` receives this error it asks `SpeechServiceMic.missing`: Claire itself has the recording permission, which service is used this time can be identified (the named one is it; when none is named, look at the system's default recognition service; on-device recognition cannot be identified, and it does not guess with the first registered one), and that app requested the recording permission but did not get it — only when all three are certain does it report `SpeechFailure.ServiceNoPermission`, carrying its package name and the name in the system; when any one is uncertain it is the original `NoPermission`. The explanation popped up on the conversation page states which app lacks the permission, and "Go to settings" opens that app's app details page (when it cannot be opened, it hints to look in the system's app management); the floating capsule gives a sentence of hint, and tapping it returns to Claire. It only queries, and does not request or grant permissions for other apps; after returning it does not start recording again by itself, and judges again the next time the finger presses. The `[Speech]` section of diagnostics additionally writes the system's default recognition service and each registered service's own recording permission.

- **Conversation input field** (`voice/ui/VoiceHold.kt`): the gesture covers the **unfocused** input field, and tapping still focuses it and brings up the keyboard, while holding for about 300 ms starts listening; when the input field is already focused this gesture is not installed, and long-pressing is still the system's own text selection and paste; then there is an extra microphone between the input field and the send key, listening only when held, and tapping it only prompts "Hold to talk". The release landing point is decided by `voiceAim`: sliding a finger up into the "Listening" card (and sliding up more than 32dp, a slight thumb movement not counting) is "Release to send", the input bar, card border and dots on the card turning green (the same style as cancel's red), and after release the words are appended after the existing text in the input field and sent directly (the same path as tapping the send key, an interjection while a task is running; when it cannot be sent it stays in the input field), or on the provider path sent after transcription; sliding past the card's top edge (where the small "Swipe up to send/cancel" capsule is) is "Release to cancel"; when the card is not laid out yet, sliding up more than 80dp still counts as cancel as before. Releasing at other landing points **only fills the words into the input field without sending**, for the user to edit and send; on the provider path "Transcribing..." is shown right on release, filling in after transcription; transcription waits at most 30 seconds (`ProviderSpeech.TRANSCRIBE_TIMEOUT_MS`), and on timeout it prompts as a network failure, never spinning forever. If it is still transcribing after release (or built-in recognition is still wrapping up), holding again discards the previous segment and starts listening anew, and the capsule is the same. A press that is too short (less than 0.5 seconds from finger down) is treated as an accidental touch and not transcribed, to avoid wasting quota. Leaving the page, rotating, or the App going to the background all discard this segment.
- **Floating capsule** (`capsule/CapsuleVoice.kt`, `CapsuleController`'s `talkHold` and `CapsuleTalk`): long-pressing goes through `cap/phone/a11y/OverlayHost.kt`'s `OverlayHold`, counting as a long press only after the finger stays still for 400 ms (`HoldGesture` judges, and moving past the touch threshold first is a drag and no longer counts as a long press), with `start/move/end/cancel` callbacks to the controller. While holding, the whole screen dims and a panel with two landing points, "Cancel" and "Edit", floats beside the capsule; releasing elsewhere sends directly to the thing it is addressing at the moment (the conversation being continued, a run waiting for an answer, etc.), dragging to "Edit" opens the card to continue editing (the words are appended after the unsent text in the input field, the same joining as the conversation page's `HoldToTalk.join`), and dragging to "Cancel" drops them. Screen-reader users use the "Speak" action: tap once to start and tap again to send, and having no finger they can only send. While the capsule is speaking it switches to the "Your turn" bar (or can no longer speak), and these words are dropped directly and the microphone released. Recording happens in the accessibility service's overlay window gesture, with the finger on screen while holding, without starting a separate foreground service. The speaking panel is a third overlay window: it covers the whole screen and appears while speaking, and when collapsing it is first explicitly set invisible and then shrunk to no size (the same set as the dot and the card: after the content is cleared no new frame may come out, and zero size still cannot remove the system canvas's old frame, so the panel would stay at "Transcribing…"); if after release the engine's own wrap-up limit (2 seconds for built-in recognition, about 20 seconds for streaming, 30 seconds for whole-segment transcription) plus a little margin has passed with no result, it collapses the panel and treats it as not heard clearly, so a stuck session is not kept forever.
- **Too many words heard**: when they do not fit in the card while holding (6 lines for the input field, 5 for the capsule), it measures lines by the real layout, removes complete sentences already finished from the front (breaking after periods, question marks, etc.) and adds "…" at the front, keeping the latest words always visible; when even the one still being spoken does not fit, its end is kept (`VoiceCopy.fitHeard`; the displayed part is in `voice/ui/VoiceHold.kt`, shared by the input field and the capsule). Only the display changes; the text handed over does not.
- **60-second cap**: both the input field and the capsule listen at most 60 seconds per time (`MAX_MS`), treating reaching the limit as release and never holding back from sending; the input field counts down at the card's bottom right in the last 10 seconds. `HoldToTalk.tick` calls `release` at the limit, because recording stops by itself at the limit without a separate notification, and without wrapping up the words are lost. The exception is sessions that stream as they speak and do not accumulate a whole recording (`SpeechSession.unlimited`, currently the self-hosted and Bailian "Qwen real-time speech recognition"): hold as long as you like, with no countdown and no wrap-up at the limit, and the recorder keeps no whole recording either (`PcmRecorder`'s `unbounded`); when there are too many words the preview removes finished sentences from the front as in the previous item.
- **Recording permission**: requested only when holding for the first time (`RECORD_AUDIO`). In the input field: with no permission it shows the system dialog; when it has been denied and the system will no longer show a dialog, it shows an explanation instead, with a button going straight to Claire's permission page in the system. When recording has been allowed but the recognition service still reports no permission, or reports a recording error (`ERROR_AUDIO`, `SpeechFailure.AudioError`), it shows another explanation instead: whether the master microphone switch in quick settings (from Android 12) is off, whether Claire's microphone has been separately turned off in the system's permission management, and that it may also be occupied by another App; the button still opens Claire's app details in the system. When the recognition service reports a client error, an error is thrown when starting to listen, or the provider path's recording cannot start, it still only prompts that the microphone may be occupied by another App. In the capsule: with no permission it does not start, popping a prompt that returns to Claire when tapped (`capsule_voice_need_mic`) to authorize. `VoiceInput.start` returns a session that emits only one `Failed` when there is no permission or the engine is unusable, and the UI handles it with the same failure path instead of each judging on its own.

### On-device offline recognition (voice packs)

When the phone has no usable built-in recognition and the user has not configured a voice model, downloading a voice pack enables offline press-to-talk; a voice pack can also go into the voice input fallback chain. See the design at [offline speech recognition design](../design/specs/2026-11-26-offline-speech-recognition-design.md). Only arm64-v8a is supported; other devices do not offer this source, and the UI says "This device does not support offline recognition".

- **Recognition library**: sherpa-onnx 2.0.3 AAR statically linking onnxruntime, placed in `app/libs/` (checksum in `app/libs/README.md`), with sherpa/onnxruntime libraries other than arm64-v8a excluded at packaging. Model weights do not go into the APK, and executable code is not downloaded from the network.
- **Manifest** (`voice/ondevice/VoicePackCatalog.kt`): the `assets/voice-models/catalog.json` shipped with the APK is the only trust anchor, and the App does not read the manifest from the network. The top level is `schema` (currently 1; a mismatch counts as a packaging error), `release` (the download prefix published by the Claire repository `voice-models-v1`) and `packs`. Each pack: `id`, `version`, `backend` (inference backend, such as `sherpa-onnx`), `family` (model type within the backend: `sense_voice`, `whisper`, `dolphin`), `displayName`, `languages` (languages the model can recognize, ISO 639 language part), `recommendedFor` (which UI languages it is recommended for, `AppLanguage` codes), `files` (each file's `name`, `role`, `size`, `sha256`; files not at `upstream + name` also carry an absolute `url`, such as the license text), `license` (`name`, `file`, `attribution`), `upstream` (a fixed-commit `resolve/<commit>/` directory on HuggingFace). The license file is also an item in `files`, verified and installed together. When parsing, packs whose backend is not in the registry, for which `supports(family)` is false, or whose pack id/file name is not a safe single-segment name are dropped, and the rest are kept as usual. The manifest is generated by `tools/voice-models/publish.py` from the hand-written `packs.json`, and a unit test blocks a manifest that was not updated.
- **Storage** (`VoicePackStore`): `filesDir/voice-models/<pack id>/<version>/`, with staging in `voice-models/.staging/<pack id>-<version>/`. There is no "installed" record file: whether it is installed only depends on whether the version directory exists and every file in the manifest (including the license) is present with a matching size; after installation only the size is checked, without recomputing hashes. Files that are downloaded or imported are first checked in staging one by one for size and SHA-256, and only when all pass is the whole thing renamed into the version directory (an old directory of the same version is first moved to `<version>.previous`, deleted on success and moved back on failure), after which old versions are deleted; a failure midway only clears staging, and installed ones are unaffected. Cleanup, commit and deletion are within the same process-level lock. At App startup it clears leftover staging and packs no longer in the manifest, keeps only one usable version per pack (the current version, or the installed older version when there is none), and fills in registration for packs that are "files installed but not registered in the directory" (killed between commit and writing the directory). `BackupEngine` does not back up `voice-models`.
- **Download** (`download/VerifiedDownload.kt`, `VoicePackInstaller`): the generic downloader has one task download a group of files in order, each file trying sources in order, writing `.part` first and verifying after the complete download is renamed, and switching to the next source on failure; tasks are distinguished by key, App updates and each voice pack do not cancel each other, and there is only one task per pack at a time (importing also takes this lock). Source order: when mirrors are on, "user mirror prefix + Release address" (in the user's order) → official Release address (attachment name is `<pack id>--<file name>`) → upstream address. Every address goes through `DownloadUrls.requireTrusted`: it only accepts the address given by the manifest itself, or an HTTPS mirror prefix applied to the Release address; upstream only accepts a fixed-commit HuggingFace file and a likewise fixed-commit `raw.githubusercontent.com` original file written in the manifest. Downloads go through `NetProxy` without binding to Wi-Fi; on a metered network a dialog first states the size; when the remaining space is less than the pack size plus 50 MB (`VoicePackStore.SPACE_MARGIN`) it does not start. It starts only when the user taps "Download"; the process being killed counts as a failure and a retry works.
- **Import**: choose a zip (the file may or may not be in a pack id subdirectory) or multiple files with the system file picker, and check file name, size and SHA-256 one by one against the manifest, ignoring ones not in the manifest; missing files or mismatches are rejected, saying which file it is. After passing it goes through the same staging and commit as downloading. "Copy download address" on the card gives each file's Release address, so it can be downloaded on a computer and imported.
- **Inference backend and extension points** (`OnDeviceBackend.kt`): `OnDeviceSttBackend` (`id`, `supports(family)`, `abiSupported()`, `load(InstalledVoicePack, EngineOptions)`) loads an `OnDeviceSttEngine` (`transcribe(pcm, language)`, with 16 kHz mono PCM in and text out, `language` null meaning automatic; `close` releases). `OnDeviceBackends` is the backend registry, constructed with a list passed in like `VoiceAdapterRegistry`, replaced with a fake in tests; `InstalledVoicePack.file(role)` takes a file by `role`, not by file name; a load failure throws `OnDeviceSttException` (`Missing`, `Corrupt`, `Kind.OutOfMemory`, `Timeout`, `Unsupported`). Adding a new model is one of three kinds, with more changes the further down: a `family` already supported by sherpa-onnx only needs one entry in the manifest and a released App version; a type sherpa-onnx supports but is not wired here needs one branch in `SherpaOnnxBackend.modelConfig`; a new inference library needs writing a new `OnDeviceSttBackend` and registering it, with the library shipped in the APK. `OnDeviceSttAdapter` only knows these two interfaces and does not know sherpa-onnx. A user importing a model outside the manifest is not done in the first version.
- **Model residency** (`OnDeviceEngines`): each pack (id + version) is loaded only once, preloaded in the background when the finger presses to speak (`prewarm`), and loading does not count toward the recognition time limit and waits for itself at most 60 seconds; after that it stays resident, and is released when idle for 5 minutes or on `onTrimMemory` (`TRIM_MEMORY_RUNNING_LOW` and above; the UI going to the background does not count), and the old one is released when changing version or deleting. Recognition also runs in a background scope (native decoding cannot be cancelled, so the caller's timeout must be able to return immediately), with the thread count the smaller of the CPU cores and 4; Whisper sets `max_tokens_per_second=25` per stream, otherwise short instructions are truncated. The language is the language part of the recognition language tag (`OnDeviceLanguage.pick`), specified when it is in the model's `languages`, otherwise automatic; Dolphin is always automatic. SenseVoice and Dolphin output simplified Chinese for traditional Chinese, and the first version does not convert.
- **Wired into the voice catalog**: the protocol `SttProtocol.OnDevice`. When a pack is installed, the fixed service `on-device` (`VoiceAccount.None`) and this pack's `VoiceModel` (`SttBinding(OnDevice, endpoint = pack id)`, `origin = Builtin`) are written into the catalog snapshot, stored in the snapshot like other models, so the fallback chain and tidy-up logic need no special handling. When the files are gone (restored from backup, deleted), the model and the choice are both kept, and using it reports `VoiceModelError.PackMissing` with "Needs download" in the UI, and after downloading no re-selection is needed. When the user deletes a voice pack it deletes the files and the model in the catalog: if it was selected it returns to "automatic" (not "none" as when deleting other models: "automatic" only uses built-in recognition or another voice pack, costs nothing and leaves the phone), and ones in the fallback chain are deleted as usual. The adapter `OnDeviceSttAdapter` is whole-segment transcription of "WAV in, text out", so it can be both the chain head and a backup; the time limit is recording duration × 3 with a minimum of 30 seconds (`OnDeviceTimeout`), and a timeout is not recorded as a network error; all kinds of failures (`PackMissing`, `OnDeviceFailed`; when corrupt, the UI says "The voice pack is corrupted; please download it again") switch to the next in the chain. Recognized empty text is handled as "did not catch that".
- **The order of "automatic"** (`SpeechChoice.Auto` branch of `SpeechResolver`, `VoiceInput` opening a session, pack picking in `OnDeviceAuto`): ① the phone's built-in recognition is usable → use it (it can show text as you speak, unchanged); ② otherwise, an installed voice pack recommended for this recognition language; ③ otherwise, any installed one (in manifest order, with automatic detection when the model does not support this language); ④ otherwise keep the original `Why.SystemNoResponse` / `Why.NoSystemNoModel`, with an extra "Download offline voice pack" entry in the hint. It only goes to the offline model when built-in recognition is unusable. "Automatic" has no fallback chain: `backupsFor` returns a chain only when a specific model is chosen.
- **Calls do not use voice packs**: `CascadeEngine.listenRef` excludes `OnDevice` models, and voice packs are not shown in the dictation model list of in-app calls and AI phone calls either; users who only installed a voice pack get `StandardBlocker.NoListenModel` for calls, the same as users who only use the phone's built-in recognition. Whole-segment recognition has too much latency in a call, and will be opened separately later after measuring that it is fast enough.
- **UI** (`voice/ui/VoicePacksSection.kt`, `VoicePacksViewModel.kt`): "Settings › Models › Voice input › Offline voice packs" is a separate page, with one card per pack (name, languages, size, license, state, download/cancel/delete, import from file, copy download address), and ones recommended for the current UI language come first and are marked "Recommended"; installed packs in the voice input choice list are marked "On-device · Offline". It does not promote downloads on first launch.

The `content-transcribe` skill is the Agent transcribing **audio files**, which is a different thing from the voice input here, and the two do not depend on each other.

## 17. App access

By package name, it restricts which Apps the standard phone tools can read and operate; see [`app-access.md`](app-access.md) for the rules and boundaries.

- **Policy** (`core/device/access/AppAccess.kt`): Apps with no setting are allowed by default, and classification does not decide permission. An empty block list does not reject because a package name is missing; when it is non-empty and ownership is unknown it waits briefly and re-reads once, and if still unknown returns `app_access_unverified`. An unavailable file returns `app_access_unavailable`, reset by the user in Settings.
- **Gate** (`core/screen/uistate/AppAccessScreenPort.kt`): wraps the task's phone port, protecting screen-reading results and explicit targets; `ActionExecutor` checks before and after actions, and rejections are returned to the model as tool results. Every member of `ScreenPort` is written out explicitly in the gate, without `by` delegation, and tests check member by member that it "is forwarded to the port inside" and that "except for a few that do not touch the App UI, all are checked", so a member added to the port later will not escape checking. The Android port provides only window ownership (`AndroidScreen.accessPackages`), and only looks at the list where the gate cannot see the target: who handles links and system actions with no App specified, and Apps opened onto the user's main screen; it does not downcast to read the settings implementation. `ScreenSession` clears old element references, screenshots and positioning caches by permission revision.
- **Cost**: when the list is empty nothing extra is done (no asking for windows, and the system prompt has no app access section). When the list is non-empty, screen reading checks directly against the window ownership in the screen it just read, without asking for windows separately; other reads' and actions' check results are reused for at most 1 second when the window has not changed (the accessibility window change count has not changed) and the list has not changed, so a screenshot right after the same screen reading and the task preview's frame-by-frame images do not have to ask for windows every time.
- **Prompt**: app access rules are in `prompts/capabilities/app-access.md`, appended after the phone section only when the list is non-empty (or the settings cannot be read); for ones disabled only while a task is running, the explanation carried by the rejection result still tells the model what to do next.
- **Storage and UI** (`app/settings/AppAccessStore.kt`, `app/settings/ui/AppAccessScreen.kt`): process-shared and atomically written; opening the settings page does not upload the list, and before refreshing classification it confirms sending App names and package names. `AppClassifier` reuses the existing model configuration, and failure does not change permissions. The page, classification and sharing copy support English, Simplified and Traditional Chinese.
- **Proactive sharing** (`app/cap/phone/a11y/ScreenShareConsent.kt`, `ShareChoice.kt`): the user can allow sharing the screen only, allow this App, or cancel; a separate sharing permission does not lift automatic operation restrictions, and the source and permission are rechecked before and after collection. Sensitive authorization screens can only be operated by the user.
- **Boundaries**: shell commands, the sandbox and computer access (MCP) are not restricted by this gate; calling standard phone tools inside scripts is still checked. On encountering a restricted App the model should re-evaluate its route and, when it really needs it, explain where to allow it in "Settings › App access".

## Post-call summary and follow-up draft

`CallRuntime.summarizeCall` (`app/calls/CallRuntime.kt`) follows the call summary switch and the pre-send authorization check, and in the summary request after the end `CallSummaryGenerator` generates both the structured summary and an optional `CallSummary.followUpDraft`. It adds no extra model request; the draft is saved at most 600 characters. A missing or incorrectly formatted optional draft does not affect the rest of the summary, and old records have no draft by default. `CallHistory.saveFollowUpDraft` saves edits through the existing mutex, encrypted file and publish-after-successful-write path, without overwriting the transcription, to-dos or processing state and without reviving a deleted record. `app/calls/ui/CallFollowUpDraft.kt` only provides editing, saving and copying, keeping the edited content when saving fails; there is no contact lookup or sending capability.

## Call background standby recovery

Incoming-call standby is a foreground service (`app/calls/CallStandbyService.kt`). After the owner taps "Start standby" on the phone page, the authorization is stored in `CallSettings` and remains after a reboot or the process being reclaimed. Afterwards the system has two ways to bring it back up, restoring only incoming-call standby and never replaying outgoing calls:

- **When visible**: when Claire is in the foreground and interactive, `CallRuntime`'s recovery gate requests standby again.
- **Periodic check**: `CallStandbyRecoveryJob` is a persistent JobScheduler periodic job (about 15 minutes, and the system may delay it), which survives a reboot and catches the case where the system did not restart the sticky service. While authorization exists the job stays scheduled; the battery optimization exemption (needed only from Android 12), background restrictions and notification permission are all checked only at each run, so when a permission is revoked only that run does not start, and the next check goes back to standby once the permission returns, without opening the App.

Both paths follow the same rule `CallStandbyPolicy.mayRequestIncomingStandby` (`StandbyTrigger.Visible` / `Scheduled`): authorized, auto-answer ready, notifications available, and not configuring, starting, already on standby, in a call or outgoing work, lock-screen dialing, or an unhanded managed call; only the conditions under which the system allows a start differ (foreground interactive / allowed to start a foreground service from a job).

An explicit stop is turning off standby, auto-answer or AI calling in the App (revoking authorization, cancelling the task), or "Fully quit" (`MikaQuit`: the quit flag makes `hasAuthorizedIncomingStandby` false and cancels the task, and after reopening Claire it is scheduled again by the stored authorization). Swiping it away in recents does not count as stopping; force-stopping in system settings is the system itself cancelling the task and the sticky restart.

When standby is blocked, tapping the notification opens the standby check page the phone page already has (`MainActivity.EXTRA_OPEN_CALL_STANDBY`, a one-time request cleared after the check page opens), without a separate state.
